Would it enhance some poem to list my regrets in a pattern
of three, slant rhyme falling on the final kicker? No dice.
I am not dying at all. I am becoming the space between branches
of the tallest tree, the perfect white circle contained
in the letter "o". The beaks of the warbler clack shut.

I have lied about hate for some time now,
calling it a preference for this over that: apples
instead of pears, mealy and prone
to attracting small white flies.

How absurd, the philosopher in his kitchen,
the qestion of being amidst the potclamor,
no match for the pleasure of actual,
sliceable things.

Ergo my life in Spanish: me pica. It pricks me. It stings me.
Incites me with spice (not temperature) to inner
warmth and pain. Our neighbor's youngest struts
down the street with a ring of construction paper
on her head, calls out to her friend, "mira - un crown!"
sticks out her tongue and wiggles a taunt in dance.

Sophistication means being able, e.g. to communicate
sarcasm  by the way one arranges
place settings for dinner.

Magritte you would be proud: feelings
laid out like pieces for chess.

It is true, what they say about me.

6/21/08

What good is longevity anyway? This poem
won't last as long as the plastic bottle
on my desk. Perhaps we have always dug up
(since, after all, statistically, what are
the chances of being "right"?) the wrong
artifacts, coalesced all the worst bad cues.
Perhaps I should write history instead.

7/2/08

Still Life With Bougainvillea

The bougainvillea is tapping
at the window, and you
are gone. The cat is watching
over the path where you
might return. I am watching
the cat, and the small
flowers inside the flowers,
as they brush the pane.
On the cat, there are fleas.
In the flowers, flowers.
In me, your absence drums its
fingers at the points
where I notice my pulse, taps
its beak against the bars
of my chest. Small creature
in my own creature body,
white flowers enveloped in red.

bowing its head to drink,
skin becoming fabric becoming
skin, trigonometry of body,
watery tissue, softening
the all-consuming cloth.

Overhearing College Students In The Cafe After Mass

"Franco was a good Catholic," she sets it down again,
this thought, her plank, and from here they can reach
the conclusion that someone must take the reigns
when a herd threatens to stampede. They are
young. One is learning Latin. The bullets have not
yet reached the backs of their minds.
The mandatory course for Catholic school teachers
was helpful but vague: sexual predators
are charming, they appear to be good. In nursing,
one rarely uses more than one simple math formula.
An absent schoolmate has reached the rank of
captain. Afghanistan is hard. Active duty, though,
will be good for his career. They muse on the global
threat of the Communist party, its propaganda,
while tapping at their touch-screen mobile phones.
It was their clothes I noticed first, how sensible.
One apologized for not having the formula down
for how he likes his mocha. It took several questions
to get it right. Each answer painful. It is the clarity
of their eyes I notice now, their grown-up
shoes, the quiet between words. The paper
on the table says the Pope has gone
to Australia, to apologize for what some priests
have done. The girls, with womanly posture,
glance occasionally my way. I am reading Williams
like medicine, gulping down the tonic of his mind.
His face, on the cover, I realize, is terribly handsome.

Yehuda Amichai's Cat

He sits like that, at the window,
sometimes all day, drumming
his finger like machine gun fire,

or gathering the silence up to
himself like a blanket under which
he might take a nap.

His eyes are always open.

I climb up his leg and circle, flattening
the field of his lap.

When he sighs, the great lion-
body trembles, and I don't know whether
to dig in my claws, or flee.

Outside, dust is blowing
through the brick-oven streets.

Sometimes, I rest my chin on his hand,
to make sure he knows I am there.

7/23/08

After the Bombs

Only the tusks of my village remain.
The stars frown down with their
penitent rage, their penitent power
while the moon changes masks
at her pleasure. The starlight remains.

Only the night can cover this bruise,
the game of chance, we played, this game
a ruse in rosy glasses bending light
into the windows of the soil-stained
cacophony. The sound sounds, a stain.

The bells complain their cinder note,
a smoky haze in which to drown
the boat-like stretchers floating by,
upon a street of sky, screeching
with the traffic of the dying, dying dead.

Remains of tissue, remains of bone, a stone,
the walls of wells sunk in, the water
black as blood, a bruise within the circle
of the town, its walls torn down by fire,
and by water, and the fissure gushing red.

I give you my word, absurd, my hand, my
both of them to have and hold, the bold
inscription on the turrets of my tomb: here
was a witness, pitiless, stone-cold, crumbled
sand, a man caught in a sandstorm, storming.

7/25/08

The Prophet Remembers The Desert At Dusk

Go into the desert,
at sundown, a chalice

of wind, the sky
like the surface of water

seen from the ocean floor.
The dust crackles

under foot, and sparks
against the air.

A tumbleweed rocks
on its bones,

skeletal planet.
The evening spreads

its precise stillnes,
spiders weaving a mesh.

Cracks in the salt flats
spread like lightning.

Something burrows in
to the crumbling ground.

The skin of the drum
shrinks tighter.

The ground pulses its
warmth into the dark.

and spread over the sand, covering
each beach he planned and won.

7/28/08

The bodies made bunkers, the ruinous
bodies, surf pooling in their laps.

to his legs, and he gave the nurses

in the Veterans' hospital "a hard time."
As a Boy Scout leader he made the dirty

kid, who never obeyed his order to wash,
wear a toilet seat like a stockade.

As a seargent, he taught his men to breathe
under their helmets, let the bullets ricochet

off the shallow waves, and survive the day.
He and his wife had a deal never to speak of it.

Ars Poetica

I want to shake him,
my little duende,
but he might just let
his arms fall off.
He would do that,
throw off his limbs
like two lizard tails,
leave them flapping
in my fists, and scram.
I want to help him
help himself, but he
won't stay put in
the chair. He chews
through the ropes
like they're taffy,
looks back just to
flap his tongue
in a red salute,
and still, I follow
him around, hoping
he'll find a corner
someplace quiet,
curl up, and sing.

8/10/08

Memory Speaks of the Desert at Night

Go. Go into the desert. I will find you there, in the place where so little survives. Isolation, yes, but something more: the tumbleweed rocks on its stem, skeletal planet. Scorpion flexes his pincers, conducting. And the sky is the surface of water, seen from below. Go into the dust, where it sparks against the brittle air. Walk slowly. The evening spreads precise stillnes, the weaving of spiders, a mesh. Carry yourself out, to find what you came for: cracks in the salt flats spreading like lightning. Burrow in to the crumbling ground. I will find you. Crow is circling slowly. The skin of the drum draws tight. The ground beats out its warmth in to the dusk. Now, go.

8/16/08

Reading James Joyce At The Berkeley Marina

I had been up all night in an Oakland diner, talking
about not very much. Now the sea keeps me awake, the gulls
eye me like a suspect, and the long poles sway.

Here, the wind blows in all directions,
flipping my pages, churning the algal spray.
Beside me, the hooded fishermen pace for warmth.

8/17/08

4:12 AM

And then, there was sound again. Rowboats tumping
the dock, insects crackling, the low motorized drones.
In my dream, the fisherman pours his catch from a bucket
onto my lawn. The grass becomes seaweed, the fish
slither down through the pond. My reflection wavers
on the surface, while at the bottom, the dead play cards,
and wave away fish indifferently, like flies.

8/18/08

Visitation on the Dock

The fisherman approaches, with his bucket
of bait, and the distant gaze of a seer.
My hands drill in to my pockets.

Up close, I can see he is shaking with fear
or maybe cold, and his vision is fixed behind
me, his fierce eyes glazed with tears.

I turn around to see what he stares at: blind
air and distant trees, nothing in my sight.
He places a hand on my shoulder and winds

these words into my ear: "You wanted light
when there was darkness, now in the brightness
you shrink away, to a personal night."

"Even in the night there are stars, the sightless
eyes of heaven," I remind him, stepping away,
but he is unmoved by my turn of mind, pitiless,

as all fishermen are, unable to be swayed
by liquid words or the jeweled sparkle of scales.
His hands are scared, his coatsleeves frayed,

He does not grab ahold of me again, but impales
me like a hooked fish with these words:
"You are a ship, fully rigged, refusing to sail."

8/19/08

"To where?" I reply with false indifference, absurd
since now my hands are trembling, and my words fail
to keep a level tone, swooping like a wounded bird.

I pivot to face him, but only confront a salty gale
which takes me aback, my chest blown in like a sail.

8/28/08

Yellow

The weed has no mind,
except what I lend it, there
between two concrete slabs,
growing flowers so yellow
they burn in my sight, remain
long after I close my eyes,
as if I might see them in death,
smoking torches, suphurous
beacons, guiding me on their
tough green stalks, lighting
the damp walls of the cave,
itself a borrowed mind, thinking
what stones must think when wet --
thinking sparks from flint,
thoughts about sharpening metal,
thinking what concrete thinks
when tree roots whisper deep down,
conspiring against its underside,
first a crack, then a gap,
a birthing ground for seed dust
to take hold, and rain to fill,
and then a stalk emerges, popping
buds, which become the living
thoughts of yellow beyond yellow.

The Ice Has Come Back

Listen, I tell you, it
covers the place where I
used to live, it smooths
the handholds and grips
tight against the belly
of my dreaming. I sleep
in ice, and follow blue
veins of thought. I dream
the cracking sound, plunge
and startle myself awake.
The ice has returned
to the place where I live,
the waitress pours change
into the cup of my hand,
and soundlessly, it covers
with frost. The sky clouds
like the marbled interior
of packing house blocks,
cut from a frozen river,
whitened by the pickaxe.
It has come back for me,
to fill the spaces between
myself and myself, always
arguing, white breath
like smoke from a pistol
obscuring the duelers face.

Write a poem in which any sort of clock has a place but is not the whole story. Indeed, the clock might show up early and not be seen again or only show up late. Employ as many of the five senses as possible. Include at least one proper noun and at least one body part.

Try to use at least 10 of these 18 words, or versions of them, at least once: window, salt, tumble, ice, fall, hoof, spectacle, quartz, ring, whisker, stab, scab, tone, stranger, writhe, fabric, naked, secret.

Aim for 14-30 lines + a title. Associate freely.

9/1/08

Ocotillo Drive

I am a stranger now, but once
my knee scabs
sprung from falling, prayer-like
onto this tar-
scented street, my bruises
gathered color
from tumbling in rollerskates
onto this ragged sidewalk --
white quartz, hot ice, rock salt --
the cement would tick
like a wound-up clock
gathering heat
like a brick-oven's walls.

Once, in the shade
of the Bottle Brush tree,
my skateboard rang out into the air,
and I met the naked
pavement with my chest,
the sound of my breath
escaped in one long, low tone,
and I knew only to writhe
on my belly like a lizard,
the few feet I could
toward home. Then the blackness,
and the spectacle,
as the neighbors circled, in their car,
a second time, and saw me,
still splayed there
in the phosphorescent sun.

9/11/08

Ritornello

The bells of the campanile are playing Messiaen.
Playing Messian requires a certain kind of grace.
A certain kind of grace pervades this night.
This night is full of Messiaen, gliding on bells.
Gliding on bells, the blades of grass all shiver.
The blades of grass all shiver and gather damp.
Gathering damp, the night unfolds its silver wings.
Silver wings unfold upon the dewdrops in the grass.
Upon the dewdrops in the grass, the bells grow full.
The bells grow full, the bells of the campanile,
playing Messiaen, gathering a certain kind of grace,
pervading the night, the grass, ashiver in the damp,
the bells grow full and spread their silver wings.

9/24/08

4am - Sunrise

I have watched the morning from a long way off
coming out across my ruined thoughts, laid out
like pieces for chess, and the dark silhouettes
of the trees becoming golden and dimensional,
if not to say, becoming real, unlike my thoughts,
which only seem to gather, as the light gathers
itself up, into a crescendo of holy weeping.
Those who sit opposite me, in my mind, can hardly
be called my enemy. And yet, I click each move
into place, and follow the line like certainty.
I am becoming the darkest object in the room.

Vignette With Cobwebs And Legs

I see her through the window,
the blue light from the television,
baptising her living room
as it throbs in a sensuous dance
over the chrome of her lotioned
legs. She eats cereal, then chocolate,
closing the distance to her mouth
like a crane relaying materials
to the building crew above.
In the corner, a spider hangs upside down
over the gauze of some hasty repairs.
The speaker in the television
pulses sound in muffled bursts,
sounds not unlike the human voice
to the woman on the other side
of the glass, and which, I'm sure
the spider is far too focused to mind.
I watch the pitch of their wooden cabin
before the aquatic screen. The shadows
have resolved to leave them both alone,
tiptoeing along the baseboards,
like children stealing toward the kitchen
in search of late-night snacks. Nothing
will disturb them, the home-fixer, the loafing
muncher, splayed on the plumped-up
cushions of her couch. Suddenly, I want
to shake them like a snowglobe, cast
the objects into a syrupy drift.
And I want you to help me. Take hold
of your end, and on the count
of three, let us work together, like a bellows.
I sense your hesitation, admiration
of the cotton filigree, placed
there by our arachnid friend. And the
woman, legs gleaming, mouth agape,
could have been painted there,
as social commentary, a modern
Olympia, Ophelia, Cordelia,
swapping the lace corset
for a housedress packed with flowers
and some stains. The spider gathers
cotton over the corpse
of a motionless fly. The woman raises
a spoonful of milk to her mouth.
Where is the beauty? A car passes,
and the radio warns, "It don?t
mean a thing if it ain?t
got that swing." Ella Fitzgerald
in the late autumn of her life,
winging through the neighborhood in a
souped-up neon-green Chevy.
The stars roll out their puzzle
in a pool of sprinkler water, black
as spit-shine. The lawn-grass
gathers moisture from the night.
I have stayed here far too long.

10/8/08

Falling Asleep Reading Ashbery

I say I do it often, but really, it does it
to me, the sleep I mean, for in the thin
light of the mind, wakefulness forms only
a nail-thin crescent; the rest is self-
deception, like all power, the power
to sleep being no more mine than Ashbery,
the man, or Ashbery, the idea one can work
toward or against, in waking, but Ah!
in dreaming--that is another matter.

Suppose I were to walk down a long, thin
plank toward the open sea, a bridge, suppose
to nowhere, through the jittery dark, waves
of lofty veritgo siezing upon my strides
like a tonic, mixed with gin. Look out, though!
Wham, the plank could flip, and down we go
(yes you are with me in this darling now)
and where, might you ask, in all this is our
Ashbery? I'll tell you a truth about precision:
like lightning, it strikes, but the same place?
Hardly. Just as one will never slip into sleep
upon the same thought twice. So, tonight it
is Ashberry, supposing, and tomorrow night,
the idea of Ashberry, frosted around the edges
with the gentle decline of some civilized snow.

10/12/08

Postcards from the War Hospital

The leaves are silvering
in a patch of sun,
the gray leaves, catching light.

I have run out of topics
for dreaming, so I make them up:
each morning, a new lie.

The sister of Patience
is Suffering. Gray wool skirts,
a nurse's cap. The end of ends.

Nothing more prismatic than
a pirate film at the matinee:
dust in a shaft of light.

Under the dragon's tongue,
a tiny pebble of black saliva.
Each of us, our ignominies.

The leaves are sleeping
in a patch of darkness.
Let them. They have earned it.

The war. Always the war.
We will run out of morphine soon.
The radio flickers indifferently.

Cold morning. Cold night.
In between: the radiator
groaning like a sore old man.

If I lose the leg, another
will sprout in its place.
I will walk upon the air.

The leaves are decomposing.
Unseen creatures eat away.
The gangrene has come back.

10/13/08

What I Do Before Dawn

(In Response To Marvin Bell's
"Why Do You Stay Up So Late?")

I write under the blister of morning.
The part of my mind on salary
is still asleep. The lights
are camphor, and everything
depends on the crow.
The lament of the doves
never mattered. It was only
to bring you these crumbs
that I cracked the ice
above my bed, and threw off
the body-warm covers. I have watched
the blue light coming from
a long way off, the trees
becoming golden, and dimensional.
I rub out the dust from my eyes
like cleaning a gravestone.
I massage each finger
like the barrel of a gun.
For you, for this: an elegy
before breakfast, written
to remember myself.

"Power Up Lovegun"
    (spam email message subject)

Depress the heart-shaped button
and the gun begins to purr,
rows of lights advancing
like the aisle of a move theater.
Something clicks, deep within,
and now it is a hummingbird,
gyroscopic in your hands.
Bring your cheek up next to it,
close one eye, line up the scope,
pretend to fire at windblown trash,
transforming it to rose petals.
In the final stage of preparation,
the gun begins to sing old tunes--
wordless favorites of French cinema,
Western themes, low crooner notes.
Cradle it back against your shoulder,
this glowing weapon of song,
and click the safety off, since when
could a switch make such things safe?
This is your lovegun, Cupid--crossbow
of the modern age, feel how it wants
to fire, and fire. And now, take aim.

10/26/08

How Not to Write a Poem

A wadded napkin blooms
in a pool of egg yolk.

Toast crumbs gather
on the bus boy's rag.

The radio is playing be-bop.
The air conditioner hums.

Motionless under the glass
display, glazed donuts glow.

Man, this is boring syntax.
Avoid frozen diner haiku.

How Also to Not Write a Poem

In a pool of egg yolk, the napkin
uncurls its fist. Toast crumbs
line the dinner knife like a disease.
The radio is playing be-bop against
the air conditioner's drone. Under
the glass display, pink donuts consipre.
I better make soemthing violent happen.
But I haven't introduced any characters.

10/30/08

A Halloween Poem

In the dusty light of evening,
you will see them shuffling,
a band marching to funerial
tones, the small feet plodding
and scratching at the stitches,
adjusting the elastic of a mask,
slow-motion marauders on prowl.
Here is the part where I say
how fun it was to be one of them.
In the washed-up light of Autumn,
turning Winter, one has strayed,
a samurai cut from his small band,
overlooked on the open road, eyes
narrow, determined not to break
from character, he draws a plastic
sword, sucks back the urge to cry.
It was only a ruse, it was only
for candy, a bit of pretend fun.
He is still not crying at the Sheriff's
station, and while the clerk makes calls,
he plans a new life for himself--
sheltering in the park, living
on one piece candy or of gum per day.
He counts out his rations carefully.
Here is the part where I say
how fun it is now to stand at the door,
doling out sweets, admiring costumes,
the part where I ask, "what are you
supposed to be?" and smile at the reply.

The smell of new coffee grounds.
Commit as steam to the cup, the cup
to its drink, the drink to its smell.
Each line pressed up to its predecessor,
the plop of sugar lumps in the foam.

Pedestrian Crossing

The ring of the pavement against my shoe
finds me sleepwalking through the neighborhood
in daylight, crossing the street wrapped up
in a lace of memory, its wild odor, a tremble
of wind at the edge of the hour in a house
made only for survival--red-roofed, bare slats,
inhabited by dust and reverie, smoke in a bowl,
the dragon's nostrils flaring with each breath.

11/7/08

Reading James Joyce At The Berkeley Marina

I had been up all night in an Oakland diner, talking
about not very much. Now the sea keeps me awake, gulls
eye me like a suspect, and the long poles sway.

Here, the wind blows in all directions,
flipping my pages, churning the algal spray.
Beside me, the hooded fishermen pace for warmth.

I will leave college as I entered, a ghost.
I will study the planks of the boardwalk for syntax,
debate the existence of God, and miss my finals.

In a darkened room, capacity three hundred,
I will find enough space to for my thoughts.
In the sea, a companion, in books, the broken word.

I study the globe of my fist, the chapped knuckles.
The fishermen are shouting jokes above the wind.
The one-eyed man on the cover cracks a smile.

11/10/08

Begin where you are. Take a step
onto the moist planks of the deck,
bouyant under foot, and rolling
with the gentle harbor tide. Today
is a good day, and you can see
beyond the seagulls gathered along
the railing, a horizon washed
in blue, guileless and unspeckled,
unlike the railing, where the gulls
protest and shit, staring at land.

With the salt in your nose, begin
to soap the rough planks of the deck,
the suds dispersing like spume, the mop
trailing its tentacles like a squid.
Fling the excess overboard, and see
how algae swipes the prow like a matador,
as if you were fast under sail again.
But the ropes are coiled to their moorings.
The anchor chain plunges down into the dark.
The gulls shift their feet, and scream.

11/11/08

I have been this boat under anchor.
I have washed myself in the dim harbor
light, while the slap of the sea
and the chatter of birds castigate me.
The old man was right. The hull bobs
on its tether, tied down like a cork.
Some clouds congregate in the distance,
thinking of rain, with the most planks
of the deck just beginning to dry.
One white feather drifts along the prow.

# # #

Learning to Write Poems in a Coffee Shop

The milk frother roars as it drowns,
sputtering advice: commit to each word
the as steam to the cup, the cup to its
drink, the drink to its smell. Each line
pressed against its predecessor, convincing
as the plop of two sugar lumps into foam.

The coffee grinder grits its teeth,
exhaling the fragrance of coffee oil.
This, it admits, grudgingly, is wordless,
but tangible, the dirty butter-nut char
of the well-roasted bean must permeate
the space between your better lines.

The clink of the cups onto saucers
tinkles with a momentary thought:
the bell within the language sounds
and dissipates, muffled by the velvet
touch of thought. Sound the note
that makes the place reverberate.

# # #

Incident in the Diner

In a pool of egg yolk, the napkin
uncurls its fist. Toast crumbs
line the dinner knife like a disease.
The radio is playing be-bop against
the air conditioner's drone. Under
the glass display, pink donuts consipre.
Insert something violent here. Insert
the butter dish specking with blood,
the dropped plate shattering silence,
a woman's scream, insert the gunman's
squeaking boots, the flapping dooor--
anything but this scene in which the bus
boy grips the rubber bucket to his hip
and puts his shoulder into the dustrag
just enough to keep it gliding, keep
the debris gathering into his bucket,
into himself, the gruesome repetition,
as one of him rubs out chicken grease,
and the other lies face-down, flung,
into a spreading pool of blood.

# # #

At Harbor

Begin where you are. Take a step
onto the moist planks of the deck,
bouyant under foot, and rolling
with the gentle harbor tide. Today
is a good day, and you can see
beyond the seagulls gathered along
the railing, a horizon washed
in blue, guileless and unspeckled,
unlike the railing, where the gulls
protest and shit, staring at land.

With the salt in your nose, begin
to soap the rough planks of the deck,
the suds dispersing like spume, the mop
trailing its tentacles like a squid.
Fling the excess overboard, and see
how algae swipes the prow like a matador,
as if you were fast under sail again.
But the ropes are coiled to their moorings.
The anchor chain plunges into the dark.
The gulls shift on their feet, and scream.

I have been this boat under anchor.
I have washed myself in the dim harbor
light, while the slap of the sea
and the chatter of birds castigate me.
The old man was right. The hull bobs
on its tether, tied down like a cork.
Some clouds congregate in the distance,
thinking of rain, and the most planks
of the deck are just beginning to dry.
One white feather drifts along the prow.

Begin to make sense of the bouyant sky,
if you can, which is better than the gulls
might do, even though they inhabit the air.
No child will come to this place, to play
on the dock, marvel at the anchor chain,
watch the sails uncurl their blinding white.
Begin, right here, to feel how the wind
can blow through you, as though you were
fabric, loosely woven, a feather coming apart.
The gulls will soon forget that you were here.

11/24/08

A Fabricated Memory

The village in my mind
fills up with snow,
the walled village
with its caraway buns,
vendors flogging pork,
the round chapel stones
and rough mosaic streets
edged in fine white powder,
glossed with ice.
The doors on the high street
close quietly, one by one,
the windows burning orange,
and now the clop of a cart-
wheel pulled up onto the curb,
and the village, on its hill,
pulling down in silver light
the snow, which I remember
as though it were real,
the muffled slush of strange
feet companionable,
worn slippers fit just right.

11/25/08

Wax Candle

Enclosed in glass, the paraffin pools,
gathering ash, and surfacing bubbles.
Warmed by the fire that it feeds,
melted clear, it climbs the wick,
into the blue flame, and escapes
as smoke from the white-orange tip.
At night the flame wavers, filling
a book's crevice with shadow ink,
gathering like oil toward the tip.
What is said by candlelight can not
be repeated in daylight without shame.
Maddened by love, by rage, a wick
consuming himself, our painter friend
held his hand above a candle flame
hoping to be made, himself, translucent.
The madness of candles cannot be denied.
The tip of the breathing wick glows red.
The flame whispers to the wax, "Consume."

11/26/08

Good News for the Bed
    (Spam Email Subject)

The sofa had it coming. They all say so.
The price you pay for being near the action:
springs clawing through fabric, stuffing fluff
gushing from inch-long wounds. Bad news.

But the bed kept quiet. That was her way,
out of the way, hushed up under a duvet,
gathering dust motes on her underside,
caving a little, in the middle, with time.

Nothing you could say would build her up,
or bring her down. Propped on her frame,
resolute, horizontal, she gave in places,
but would not break. And would never take.

So the chair and I were happy for her,
genuinely chuffed, when the table chipped
in for her valance. Sliding it on, between
box springs and mattress, she blushed.

The desk lamp said "give us a twirl,"
so she did, in her way, kicking up
the pleats like a spanish dancer's dress,
rippling, like a stingray through water.

11/27/08

Color Study

Black water on the road at night, and the sound
of tires rolling through leaves, the sound of black
tires crushing the damp, black leaves on the road,
at night, low voices, doors closing with a thump
against the moistened night, the dry black interior.

Red lights in a pool of black water, splintered
like fragments of red stained glass in the chapel,
signifying God's wounds, signifying glass mixed
with gold dust, the metal bleeding vermillion,
mimicked now by taillights burning in plastic domes.

A purple bruise of oil at the edge of the pool,
which also flashes up amber, and sometimes reptile
green, as it wavers agains the tailpipe's breath,
in and out of the incandescent street lamp's glow,
which is white--all colors--scalding the street below.

11/28/08

Meteorology

And then, it is over--
a break in the clouds,
which were never evil,
and the sun, which is not good,
streams into the wet yard,
glistening, not as a symbol,
but the simple refraction
of light. The rain and I
leave messages for each other
in this way, in the language
of facts: seven drops
on a mulberry leaf, a streak
of mud in the gutter, twigs
for divination, scattered
together and apart. I give
the rain a few stacked stones,
offer up an old chair, one
I never liked much, let it
work away at the varnish.
And my mind, which is also
neither good nor evil,
I offer up now, to the sky's
window cleaner, the one
who summons the worms,
and scatters the trash,
that I might contain, someplace
in my own clay body, the gentle
indifference of rain.

Making Love to the Sound of Gunfire

"It appeals to us to shake our bodies and our muteness, / to send a thousand emissaries to love."
-Ibrahim Nasrallah

Let our muteness be,
like the pressing of hands together,
cold at its core,
stones at dawn, night in their heart,
embers going out,
let us make of our bodies a silence--
clocks stop ticking,
the branches of trees hold their breath.
Our muteness is ours.
Let the disc of the sky stop spinning,
the starlight starve,
under the mask of our skin, the shush
of blood just pause.
Breathe into my mouth, me into yours,
dip down into the well.
The clay jar splinters soundlessly,
returns to soft clay.
Let us shape it, and fire it, again.

and as light as the transparent wings.

where the music is stillness, is tapping,
the brush of abdomen, antennae, and legs.

12/7/08

Phrases from Val: potted shrimp, life force, cat basket

Mischief by the Almanac

Dawn smoothes out his trousers.
The garden leaves conspire.

The cat, in her basket, dreams
of potted shrimps and sardine oil.

The clouds begin to cackle,
electrons being their life force,

even as ours is blood and breath,
even as the wind inhabits trees.

So begins a day of whispering--
leaves, clouds, and snoring cat.

When it comes, the rain will sigh
like old lovers finally spent.

For now, the air is cellophane,
a twitch of retractable claw,

the tom-tom drum of a pulse
in the sea-green light of day.

12/13/08

The horizon is blue, and guileless. Spume gnashes and spits into the sand. Under the white sun, crabs scamper and joust. Birds peck at the damp sand, turning it in their bills like wet cement. And what of the ocean? It seems an afterthought, a picnic blanket spread out far beyond the mulling clouds. Is that a sailboat? Or a napkin? Too soon to tell. Everyone is lunching: the crabs, the sand-birds, and now some gulls swoop down upon an unknown mass of something dying, discarded, or dead. Watch yourself.  The wings clip close above your head. And in the pattern of shell bits, strewn across the black sand, it seems a message could be read, if only we could learn the code.

Explanations for the Deaf and the Hearing

The motorcycle sputters
like an amplified can
full of coffee beans
tumbling down a cliff.

The clouds would make
a swishing noise, polite
laughter, and the occasional
sneeze, if they could.

The sound of people's thoughts
in the outdoor cafe
are like the clucking of hens
showered by seeds and grain.

The corrugated metal silo
sounds wounded in the wind,
the bolts groan in pain,
rimmed with dried red rust.

The sound of a bullet--
not the gunshot, but the silver
trajectory, is air gasping
over smooth white metal.

A fly tunes a static radio,
never finding the signal,
the electric wings crackle
as he tries, and fails, to escape.

1/4/09

Admiring Space from my Couch

The frame is gathering light
as the window collects its dew.

Everything belongs to something.
A spider leaps, trailing silk,

the umbilicus is clamped and cut,
fluid seeping into its small pool.

My first kiss on the lips involoved
more wetness than I was expecting,

more contact with human water,
warmed in the body, not spit,

which is what it becomes when evicted,
but something amneotic, protean,

drops like the ones on the pane,
becoming jewel-like, then priceless,

as though their only purpose
was holding sunlight in a quaver,

as though the thread of photons
knew their journey through dark space

would be worth it, touching here
into the dewdrop, then my iris,

into a language that predates thought,
received by that submarine, the brain,

a flash in the dark lake of the skull,
a moment's beacon spread across

the channel, connecting ship and shore--
a thread, a rod of prehistoric light.

1/5/09

Glitter

Down the mine shaft,
beneath the miner's houses,
pressed together
like an observation deck,
there is something harder
than brick,
than hands hardened
by the pickaxe and shovel,
keener than eyes
accustomed to carbide lamps,
discerning a face
from a patchwork of shadow,
something that breathes
the air from the shaft,
and never corrodes,
won't splinter, can't shatter,
as permanent
as recurring hope,
sure to outlast the mine
itself, to wait
long after the collapse
of the town, as if patience
were a jewel, or a metal
for jewelry, as if pleased
by waiting, the longer
the better--pressure, heat,
and darkness conspiring
in the thoughts
of the men who wait.

1/6/08

An Empty Glass

Well, not exactly.
On the inside surface are water stains,
the remarks that calcium
leaves, a parting shot, the clever
circles suggesting a vessel
as even the skin of water can be a vessel
for drops, water containing
itself, why not? since even the air
can invert, like a greenhouse,
trapping heat, like a sheet laid over a glass
to slow down evaporation,
but here we would be closing the barn door
on white, crystaline horses
collecting in this otherwise empty glass.

the hard-packed ice. Of course, I am dreaming--
standing at the window, regarding the leaves
through the steam rising up from my coffee cup,
I pretend to see signs of the improbable,
imminent snow in high-desert California.
A little delusion, it seems, is also needed

1/11/09

Electric Space Heater

The electric heater moans, the metal dish expanding
slowly, a ring of truth-like metal tones ascending
like a slide whistle, an incredibly shrinking gong.

The silver dish takes on its orange, becomes a sun,
wide eye of persecution, disc reflecting coils red
with heat Prometheus never knew could come from sparks.

I stumble into my office at dawn, exhaling white,
claw the air for circulation, set down to type.
Every few minutes, I cup them beside the heater.

Then back to the keys, which stick, the flickering
monitor, my breath becoming invisible as the sun
comes up, and through the mini-blinds, to blind me.

The dish knows what I am thinking before I do.
Unblinking, jaundiced ajudicator of words--
I flick it off, and tend the clattering keys.

Mischief in the Barometer

Dawn brushes off his trousers.
The garden leaves conspire.

The cat, in her basket, dreams
of potted shrimps and sardine oil.

The clouds begin to cackle,
electrons being their life force,

even as ours is blood and breath,
even as the wind inhabits trees.

So begins a day of whispering--
leaves, clouds, and snoring cat.

When it comes, the rain will sigh
like old lovers finally spent.

For now, the air is cellophane,
a twitch of retractable claw,

the tom-tom drum of a pulse
in the sea-green light of day.

1/20/09

An Empty Glass

Well, not exactly.
On the inside surface are water stains,
the remarks that calcium
leaves, a parting shot, the clever
circles suggesting a vessel
as even the skin of water can be a vessel
for drops, water containing
itself, why not? since even the air
can invert, like a greenhouse,
trapping heat, like a sheet laid over a glass
to slow evaporation.
But here we would be closing the barn door
on white, crystalline horses
already escaped into a swaying field,
having left behind pale
shadows, the aftermath of sweat on haunches,
spume on ocean rocks,
the sting of chloride on the clear-eyed lens
of an otherwise empty glass.

The Wound Acts Up In Inclmenet Weather

    The bones ache of it,
and the glassy-eyed
    serving man's jowls
retract as his mouth
    goes tired of talking
and thins to a line.
    The bones ache on,
while rain sheets
    and somewhere a dog,
smelling of damp moss
    starts a low sound
in the living engine
    of his wet fur snout.
Aching, these bones,
    for the breeze of touch
scented lavender,
    the brush of cloud
over blue, polished sky.
    Tell me, my bones, many names
for this weather, drone
    in the language
of wood as its cut by the saw.

Tea Ceremony with Sea Change

She is whisking green tea into macha,
while the kettle steams into a February sky,
contributing?how much??infinitesimal moisture.
Even as drops of the sea-green spume
collect, like algae on porcelain, she hears
gulls chastening her imagined seascape,
sandpipers tick-ticking like the whiskers
of whisk against the smooth kaolinite,
polished in superheated kilns to a glass,
which appear to her, now, in winter light
like beach sand shellacked by salt water
when the waves pull back, thinning the glaze.
Then the sun touches down, a match in kerosene,
bursting in oranges and reds, extinguished,
far off, by a riot of green waves and spume.

1/29/09

During the Occupation

They are marching again
on their beautiful legs
through the center of Paris,
past an alley where a wet
mass is stirring, breaking
step across the bridge, lights
winking on the Seine, then
back into ranks before they
are feed in through the black
gates with gold-tipped spikes.

Someone is playing Satie
across the street from the palace,
hands flapping like angry swans,
pecking the black notes with feeling.
The monuments remain in place,
and the pigeons still perch
on the fountains, quarreling, and
emptying their bowels in the water.
The watchmaker's shop is boarded up,
but the bells of the chapel still sound.

The Wound Acts Up In Inclement Weather

    The bones ache of it,
and the glassy-eyed
    serving man's jowls
retract as his mouth
    goes tired of talking
and thins to a line.
    The bones ache on,
while rain sheets
    and somewhere a dog,
smelling of damp moss
    starts a low sound
in the living engine
    of his wet fur snout.
Aching, these bones,
    for the breeze of touch
scented lavender,
    the brush of cloud
over blue, polished sky.
    Tell me, my bones, many names
for this weather, drone
    in the language
of wood as it's cut by the saw.
    The wound sighs
with feeling, the boughs
    ache with meaning,
the touch of the wind
    full of ice, gnashing
its steel teeth, passes
    through, passes
out through the eye
    of the moon,
shivering, its patient
    blue gaze--
while the planet below
    aches on.

2/14/09

Instead of pecking his name on a keyboard,
he rattles a can of spraypaint
stomps the muck from his trainers,
and paces his lank teenage gait,
lugging a duffel up the overpass.

He pans across the duplex roofs
and vinyl streets, the junkyard
with its sleeping boxer, Cassanova.
He twiddles the razor wire, manages
one leg over, toes for the ledge.

The density of girder is precise,
it meets him, and he eases down,
a froth of acrid spray inside the bag
whimpers as he swings on faith,
into silhouette against the sign.

He rattles a brown the color of loam,
which Aztecs might have lumped and shaped
into bricks for flat-roofed homes,
the modern, stuccoed equivalents
gessoed in beige, below, awaiting murals.

He wipes a film of ash from the green
metal, and with practiced dexterity begins
to swipe a code of letters onto the sign,
the sweep of his fizzing can swiping
the thick and thin of a bamboo caligraphy.

2/17/09

I am seeping, sometime later, in heavy traffic,
clotted up for half an hour between the onramp
and his sign--his, because he named it--
for himself and maybe his friends,
none of whom have been to prison yet.

The night is overexposed. Taillights
bound down the curve of the freeway,
lit signs for burgers and gas glare down
and in the middle of my windscreen,
his sign, in spotlight, growing slowly.

In a different kind of dark we watched
a film about a Frenchman who strung wire
surruptitiously across the Twin Towers
and walked into the air above the street.
He lay down on the wire and spread his arms.

Afterward, the American reporters jostled
in a cluster around his closed-mouth smile,
asking, as trained, "Why did you do it?"
His eyes shone black in the black-and-white
footage as he shrugged, "There is no 'why.'"

In one room of my mind, I am propped
on a stool, asking this young man sensible
questions and offering him chewing gum.
Another room of my mind is smooth adobe.
Here I ask, "Why didn't you do more?"

I can barely pronounce the name of Aztec gods:
Quetzalcoatl, Coatlicue, creatures like Ahuitzotl--
the water-dwelling dog-monkey who snatches humans
in the fist upon his tail, and devours them.


2/18/09

Stuck in Traffic, a Figure Emerges

"Art is the big 'Yes.'"
-Marvin Bell

Instead of pecking his name on a keyboard,
he rattles a can of spraypaint
stomps the muck from his trainers,
and paces his lank teenage gait,
lugging a duffel up the overpass.

He pans across the duplex roofs
and vinyl streets, the junkyard
with its sleeping boxer, Cassanova.
He twiddles the razor wire, manages
one leg over, toes for the ledge.

The density of girder is precise,
it meets him, and he eases down,
a froth of acrid spray inside the bag
whimpers as he swings on faith,
into silhouette against the sign.

He wipes a film of ash from the green
metal, and with practiced dexterity begins
to etch a code of letters on the sign,
the sweep of his fizzing can swiping
the thick and thin of a bamboo caligraphy.

The night is overexposed. Taillights
bound down the curve of the freeway,
lit signs for burgers and gas glare down
and in the middle of my windscreen,
he and his spotlit codex slowly advance.

In a different kind of dark I watched
a film about a Philippe Petit, who ran wire
between the Twin Towers surreptitiously
and walked into the air above the street.
He lay down on the wire and spread his arms.

Afterward, the American reporters jostled
in a cluster around his closed-mouth smile,
asking, as they were trained to ask:
"Why did you do it?" He laughed, bewildered
then explained, "There is no 'why.'"

In a well-lit room in my mind, I am propped
on a stool, asking this young man questions
and offering him sugarless chewing gum.
But out here, in the dim chamber of my car,
my sensible car, I hear myself say: "Yes."

2/24/09

Blessing the Bankers

"...one of the principal Chinese curses heaped upon an enemy is, 'May you live in an interesting age.'"
? Frederic R. Coudert

They are still out there, the stars, commanding
more depth than ever. The light from Venus

seems closer than is safe, more luminous
than a bad idea ablaze in an innocent mind.

But what is innocent? We think, at first, a baby,
upon whose face the weather moves in bursts,

who has not discovered volume control
and empties his bellowed lungs with wailing.

Here, too, in the dusk of life, we wail.
We thought the good times would never end,

forgot the dams were built against bursting,
how terrible the water, still and black.

We troubled no-one with our dreaming.
The surface of the sky went on with changes.

The blessings laid by our mothers on our forehads--
let this one live a simple life, uncomplicated--

catch fire beneath the weak-but-omnipresent moon.
"Let this one be a banker, made of bricks."

Even the tear-down crews are out of work, must find
something else to pull against, at home.

It is winter still, though it feels like spring.
The newspapers print ads for filing bankruptcy--

such a word, the rupture of banking, which means
to pile up, as along the edge of a river--banks

to guard against the overslop, the rebel wave,
the slow rising water, seeking the floodplain.

Gather that child into your arms, the one
you hoped was owed a simple life. The waters rise.

3/14/09

When it is Easy

"Chess is everything: art, science, and sport."
-- Anatoly Karpov

Mid-city, in the park, they curl toward the board,
looking for a pin, a fork, they slide their proud
rooks, like fathers-of-the-bride, down the aisle.
Meanwhile, pigeons pick at the city's decay.

A lantern swings in the mind of one, a prince
thumbing his peon pawn as he waves away the fog,
stakes it down to a wriggling square like a merchant
counting out change--so sure, he could have wept.

But the enemy knight swipes over in cold pursuit,
a vial of arsenic tucked in his porcelain saddlebag,
his mover stifles a chuckle, rattles his hemp-and-bead
bracelet as he lays down midnight's messenger.

His fingernails are amber, and cracked, and a smile
forms from a hole in his beard as he hefts the piece
into play, reciting a scripture he's just made up--
"It's as easy to pick a piece up as it is to lay it down."

3/21/09

It sounds good enough to be true, pronounced by Moses.
Yet certain pieces hang in the mind of a chess player,
demand a regal gravity. In the trajectory of flight
versus falling, the way up and down are not the same.

And he is falling. The student recites a passage
of Chess Federation rules, while the ragged victor
leans back in the cooling air, combing his beard
with his fingers, and says, "just gimme my twenty bucks."

4/3/09

Morning Exercise

I boot the laptop and click on the kettle.
Already, small birds are scuffling around.

The domed light of morning makes a theory,
explaining our lives to the irridescent clouds.

I have coffee now, black as a panther, steaming
like rainforest dawn above my chintzy mug.

Now, start typing. At parties, if you're a comedian,
people will tell you to say something funny.

Poet, say something profound. Or better, imply it.
Look up long enough at the pearl of morning,

and down at the dumb birds surviving, look
in places we can't, listen past the tinnitis

of morning news, find detail in dark silhouettes.
The ones we pay least, we give the impossible jobs.

4/6/09

It is easier this way to dismiss them whe they fail.
Dismiss me now, and I will go outside. I will stand

in my slippers on the wet lawn, admiring the sprinklers.
It is a scientific fact: if you want to be struck

by lightning, you must stand for awhile in the rain.
Pretend this is rain. Pretend this is sanity.

Pretend for awhie to want nothing more than to peck
the grates of the drain for something tasty,

and a sky overhead that feels like being inside a marble,
and strung on the grass those beads of light for slurping.

Although though the sun will not come for awhile,
and the fabric of my soles is slowly filling up wet,

behold me in my terry-cloth robe and gooseflesh legs,
and think of me as smiling, like a poem.

4/15/09

Apologies to the Insomniac

For me, this is quiet--the floorboards
creek imperceptibly, and when I grind
the coffee, I wrap it up in my robe.

Still, you hear me, along with the morning
doves and garbage men, and you turn
away from the window's light, curling

like a winter leaf, the veined fingers
clutching around the air until they meet
and touch, and then the whole leaf falls.

But you do not fall, not back to sleep,
but hang in that place where my coffee
sips and squeaky chair mean something.

Forgive me, fellow traveler, for adorning
your transcendent world with sonic trash.
I have tried to be still in my clumsy way.

But nothing is still on a world that turns,
nothing is silent when the day wakes up,
and even in sleep, there is snoring.

Forgive me, also, my rhetoric of quiet.
I have cultivated silence like a bush,
pruning away at the edges, finding a shape.

At the center of this bush is a terrible secret.
The trunk is made of nothing but the air.
You, too, would clamor awhile in the emptiness.

And so, for me, this is quiet--the bells
of the clock tower sounding far off, the birds
on the lawn, the clatter of keys as I type.

Forgive me, if I sometimes check on you.
I like to hear you stirring in the dark.

curled around my emptiness, in bed.

"Syndrome" means it happens, noone knows why,

and fuming, and shaking a pajama-cuffed leg.

5/2/09

He Walks in Holding his Stetson

We agreed never to ask each other
anything as difficult
as "how are you doing today?"
Instead, we talk about breakfast:
eggs, toast, coffee.
"Pretty good," he'll say.
I don't tell him about my cat,
and he never mentions
the horse that threw him,
how he used to sit and trace
the contours of its mane
in a sketchbook he hid
from his drunk uncle. Instead,
he swaggers down the hallway,
his legs outlining the sweat-white flanks,
each step a burr, each clop of heel
the sound of running horses.

5/4/09

Small Gestures

Forgive me, rose petals, my fingers
could not resist the habit of plucking.

Some would call it childish, and those
who waggle a shaming finger know best.

I do not own my hands, but slip into them
each morning like a pair of work gloves.

That is why I flex, breaking up the stiffness.
Forgive me if the popping sounds annoy you.

They are all I have, these slender tongs,
to do what I think in the tactile world.

Sometimes when they mis-type a word,
I wonder what they are trying to tell me.

Sometimes they keep lifting popcorn
to my mouth, trying to fatten me up.

I wonder about the wartime practice
of soldiers shooting off their trigger fingers--

were they more afraid of dying? Or of killing
someone with a gesture as slight and easy

as curling an index finger into a teacup?
Look what we have done to you now, little flower.

I sweep the petals quickly, into my five-fingered hand.

5/9/09

Small Gestures

Forgive me, rose petals, my fingers
could not resist the habit of plucking.

Some would call it childish, and those
who waggle a shaming finger know best.

I do not own my hands, but slip into them
each morning like a pair of work gloves.

to do what my mind instructs in the tactile world.

someone with a gesture as slight and easy

little flower. Let us sweep the petals quickly,
from one full-fingered hand to the other.

Mothers' Day

I wake up to the promise
of your face,
eyes sealed like brickwork
a mouth for hunger,
hung like a single magnolia
above the chin.
The silk doll of your night-
dressed body
strains against the archive
of sleep. Answer me
with secrets,
dark equations
for the tuning of a song.
The light seeps in,
turning apricot,
and what we fear most
is upon us,
a dragon gloating
just before the kill.

Mr. Ergosum Speaks

(after Zbigniew Herbert)


None of it matters. Let me say
that again: once, it mattered, and now,
when I snap my fingers, only dust.

That absurd cake! Justice. How it tilts
in layers on it pedestal, while party-goers
observe, "how remarkably straight."

My hat is a chimney, chugging with promise.
What I think becomes soft smoke in the dampened air.
My coattails wave a continual flurry of goodbyes.

The nineteenth century was my favorite. Yes,
I have seen them all, through my monocle--
the one present I kept from the deposed Czar.

5/19/09

Who can tell if they are small or just far away?

I wipe a tear from the corner of my eye.
The air, full of soot, encourages such weeping.
I wear a monogrammed kerchief in place of a heart.

Questioning the Infidel

How long have you been there, answer me? How
long at the chimney door,
promising cake and vestibules
to the living, a face
for song, a trickle of regret seeping down
your chin, how long has this forked
hunger eyed you
like an apricot, swaggering along
on your absurd neck,
rag-doll for the dragon horde,
denominator of bruises,
silk brick in which the pins fit.
Say, "always" and then
say "always" again,
like you mean it.

Dream in G Major

I am singing a little song
about your face.
My head bobs
on my neck like a doll
as I sing
my little sing-song
ditty, mouth
blooming like magnolia,
quavering like a Valkyrie--
Wagnerian, draconian,
the song takes on
its own dark hunger,
a cabinet blown wide.
And why won't you answer?
My lungs
demands to know.
I fill up the silence
that falls
just beyond
the question mark.
Our equation
turns to division,
the song increasing
our separateness,
the music perhaps
we are, late at night,
dividing us now into
flesh and flesh,
while my uvula
flaps out a frantic
noise-making,
a party favor,
tongue unfurled,
the meaningless
and ecstatic
sound of love.

5/26/09

Gift Horse Fly

I made music
from these words I had,
wrapped them in brown paper
curled my fingers on top
like a bow,
which resembles, also,
a heart, or a
pair of wings,
able only to lift
some creature so light
it could ride
on the breath
of a song.

5/27/09

A flying horse would be something. But
this is not that. The windows have long since
been boarded,
and already the children
test stones in their hand,
eyeing the landscape for targets.

5/28/09

My wife is sleeping, so I close the door,
and minutes later find it open. The cat,

on padded instruments of walking,
creeps up, and with a cootton ball thump

commands the obstacle before her
to hinge apart, and let her through.

Little Moses, for you, it is always
this easy, and you sleep at our feet

and twitch your whiskers when dreaming.
Sometimes you scurry your feet.

A Kick or a Punch
    i.m. J.V.P.

I sing the love of a child.
I sing the love of a father for his child,
and yes, in this case, a son,
except that whatever facility
by which he might have formed
a human personality
we scattered, in this case, at sea.

Months later, I found
a toy truck in a drawer, and felt,
not like a father, but a boy
who had lost a playmate, one, in this case,
imaginary, since the only
game we played
was a kind of morse code,
me gently tapping his mother's belly,
and there--can you feel it?--
a kick or a punch in reply.

6/3/09

A Poor Explanation

Even from this distance,
there is little I can tell you about my life.

A cricket is rubbing his legs together
in anticipation of the night.

How nice for him. An evening in summer,
and grass still warm from the sun.

Even if I were the sun, I could not tell you
what it meant to be myself.

How many thousands of spiders walk the Earth?
One is making a web across the lintel.

If I brush, and break it, with my hair in passing,
more strands will appear tomorrow.

Beware of tidy conclusions, and perfect webs.
We want some moral to take home like a souvenir,

and rub in our pocket, and remember
our own interpretation of events.

The cricket is joined by a second, in syncopation.
The spider is pulling more silk from his abdomen.

Why shouldn't this be enough for me tonight?
Once I too wove time together, calling it music.

# # #

6/4/09

Once I wove time together, calling it music.
Language was my only instrument.

Tonight, the sky is polished to a glow.
We walk under arches of palm fronds,

the fingers giving way to radiant cloud.
If I had a lute, I might pluck it as we stroll.

Instead, I make some music from these words.
Like scraps of leaf found in the gutter,

they rustle in my mind, and whisper gossip.
They act important, as if what they might say

could tell me something new about my life--
something imperceptible, even from this distance.

# # #

Something imperceptible, even from this distance
has moved across the surface of the water.

6/9/09

On Being Prolific

It has been five days since I wrote something.
Five days, and no letters sent to my other self.
It has been too long I have listened to chatter.

Now, let the deeper words come.
But they do not come, even though I tap
my foot in encouragement, shift in my seat.

My poems protest the way I have lived my life.
Little poems, don't do that. I have lived
like anyone else, halfway entranced.

Nothing I can say now will console them.
All day, as I peer into a back-lit screen,
leaves flash green and gold behind my eyes.

At night, while the TV flickers and babbles,
wind plays in the trees, somewhere far away.
Let this poem speak back to them.

# # #

6/11/09

The Presence

The chair
has not moved--
why should it?--
since we left the palace
of sleep,
in search of what daylight
discovers under upturned
rocks. We left
our clothes hanging
over the chair back
and dove into each other
as refuge
against the too-bright stars.
Upon waking, it seemed
as if  someone was sitting,
watching us wake up.
I was not startled.
I think you, too, were calm.
The presence was wise,
but tired, perhaps
lonely, hunched and swaddled
in our discarded jeans
and underwear. I wanted
to ask him something
before I realized
that no one was there.

7/1/09

Nocturne with Writers' Block

It has been five days since I wrote something.
Five days, and no letters sent to my other self.
It has been too long I have listened to chatter.

Now, let the deeper words come.
But they do not come, even though I tap
my foot in encouragement, shift in my seat.

My poems protest the way I have lived my life.
Little poems, don't do that. I have lived
like anyone else, halfway entranced.

Nothing I can say will console them now.
All day, as I peer into a lighted screen,
leaves flash green and gold behind my eyes.

At night, while the TV flickers and babbles,
wind plays in the trees, high up and far away.
Let this poem speak back to them.

Let this poem speak to the birds, also,
nesting in those trees behind my eyes,
defying the garbage trucks and leaf blowers

with their songs praising potassium in the soil,
passing through the puce bodies of earthworms,
as they telescope through darkness in the clay.

Now it is getting late, and still the inside world
refuses to come out. The birds will settle for seeds
on the ground, and I will fall asleep to talk shows.

In my dreams, I am both wind and tree, the sound
and the feel of it. Each leaf I am is turning,
my only goal to catch the sun, to catch the sun.

Perhaps, like the leaf, this poem will never end,
but go on casting back the light until it fades.
Perhaps I have held it hostage long enough,

turning on its thin, cylindrical stalk. Forgive me,
this nostalgia for my own invented world.
Sometimes the one I live in seems unbearable.

Sometimes the light behind my eyes
becomes more real, and dazzling, because
I want it to be real, even as I am not.

For five days, I have been nothing but real.
Time to shed that skin, and write.
Time to spin, like a child, into blindness.

Here are the muted utterings of the night--
those monks, the crickets, are building a
rhythm, spinning their prayer-wheel legs.

In the distance, a piano is being played
by someone who also enjoys the cover of night.
Five days of stillness, and a night of spinning--

what next? The green and gold of a blessed life.
Let the stillness come again, now I am ready.
Let the darkness take me down into the well.

7/6/09

Assignment:

Use at least twelve of the following words: mothballs, target, incognito, boardwalk, ashes, notice, kite, string, bare, moon, architect, waddle, flash, tyrant, scorpion, pan, predator, lungs, tea, river, mention, salt, balloon, opportunist, disc, socks.

Include a major city.

Include something philosophical--the name of a philosopher, a philosophical statement, or even just the word "philosophy."

Smell something, or think about a smell, to start off. It doesn't have to end up being the beginning of the poem. Just see if it takes you somewhere.

7/7/09

Last Gasp

There are places the light can't reach,
and they smell of the damp, smell
of life receding, the surrender of ferment.

One could construct a philosophy,
call it, "divination of the mosses,"
or simply, "love of the incognito."

The scorpion is an opportunist for sure.
For days he waits in a desert hole,
the heat driving his prey toward the shade.

Once, a deposed tyrant hid in a hole,
breathing dust and ash, unable to stand.
They found him and hanged him.

The lungs balloon and drain
eighteen thousand times per day.
One day the alveoli all flash shut together.

One man's last breath is Baghdad city air.
Another drowns on his feet in chlorine gas.
Desdemona breathed the perfume of her bedclothes.

7/9/09

Use at least twelve of the following words: mothballs, target, incognito, boardwalk, ashes, notice, kite, string, bare, moon, architect, waddle, flash, tyrant, scorpion, pan, predator, lungs, tea, river, mention, salt, balloon, opportunist, disc, socks.

There are mothball-scented chambers in a life,
places moonlight cannot light up blue--
a held breath that the breather knows is final.

Dare we mention the soft grasses
growing, somehow, underneath a stone?
They, too, must have known the sunlight once.

A salt stain bleaches the pillow cover.
Who can tell if was is tears, saliva, or sweat?
Only that the head was wanting rest.

6/10/09

Only that the fluids of the human animal
cannot be contained in sleep, in love, in death.
Some call the incognito "intimate."

The chambers of our secrecy are vast.
While sleeping in his hole, our tyrant dreamt
of the high, gilded ceilings of his ballrooms.

More intricate than Arabesque tile,
the workings of a mind in flight, glimmering
and expansive, as a polished marble floor.

# # #

Do not ask "what next?" unless
you are ready for the answer.

I will not tell you how to live.
I am the grass. In addition

to ground cover, and grave cover,
I offer the following services:

an emetic for overfull dogs and cats,
something to water when the orchids all die,

and a better place to fall than concrete.
Think of photosynthesis as your friend.

Do not ask me to symbolize the ones
you love. I do not whisper "be patient"

or "endure." My words are audible
only to ants and potato bugs.


7/15/09

Grass-Talkers

I am the grass. I will not tell you
how to live. However, in addition

to ground cover, and grave cover,
I can offer the following services:

an emetic for overfull dogs and cats,
something to water when the orchids all die,

and a better place to fall than concrete.
Think of photosynthesis as your friend.

Do not ask me to symbolize the ones
you love. I will not whisper "be patient"

or "endure." What I say is audible
only to ants and wood lice.

Do not ask "what next?" unless
you are ready for the answer.

And do not ask anything of me. Unless
you want to roll yourself downhill.

Then, I will provide some useful padding
for sharp parts, like elbows or your tongue.

At the bottom is a patch of blooming clover.
I would say, "Watch out for bees!" however,

you mustn't say you heard this from me.
Such people they remove from mainstream life.

"Grass-talkers," they might be called, or worse.

7/17/09

A nice cup of tea
and a mean cup of coffee
were talking across
the deviled eggs,
trying, you know,
to have a conversation.
At least that's what she,
Rosie Lee, kept saying.
But Juan just wanted
to read the paper in peace.
He wasn't mean
so much as agitated.
And Rosie, sure,
had sensitive feelings.
Sometimes she felt
overpowered, sometimes
lonely on her saucer,
which kept him
from getting near.
Juan was complex,
no doubt about that.

Couples Therapy

A nice cup of tea
and a mean cup of coffee
were talking across
the deviled eggs,
trying, you know,
to have a conversation.
At least that's what she,
Rosie Lee, kept saying.
But Juan just wanted
to read the paper in peace.
He wasn't mean
so much as agitated.
And Rosie, sure,
was sensitive.
Sometimes she felt
overpowered, sometimes
lonely on her saucer,
although she stood
upon it deliberately
to keep him
from getting close.
Juan was complex
though he thought
himself straightforward.
When someone tipped
the milk jug over
neither could admit
how much they wanted
to be somewhere,
anywhere else.

7/27/09

Baumwolle

"a wonderful tree which bore tiny lambs on the endes of its branches. These branches were so pliable that they bent down to allow the lambs to feed when they are hungrie"
--John Mandeville, 1350

O wonderous, sprouted children,
each green thimbleful of lamb,
grown in a lilliputian land.

Men with leering faces on their chest,
elephants drawn like giant horses,
O preposterous incomprehension.

It stands to reason a wool-bearing tree
must bear small ewes and rams,
its umbilical stalk bent low for grazing.

What, then, is so great about reason
that it must be given its own age?

7/29/09

The Disaster of Waking

Nothing about memory
guarantees sleep
and yet, every night,
we forget--

7/30/09

the smell of a lit match,
the curve of Brazil
into the South Atlantic,
how many died
at Gettysburg, how many
grains of rice
it takes to keep from starving.
Instead, we invent
new memories while sleeping--
locked in a house,
or center stage without trousers.
The people belong,
though some are dead, and others
live continents apart.
It fits, and flying is easy.
Perhaps our memories
become someone else's dreams,
a kind of recycling
of the energy of thought.
What I know
is the ease of slipping
out of thought,
and how it takes a moment
to recall
which way up I am when I wake.
I could roll
out of bed, accidentally.
My worries
take time to return to me,
but they come,
darting through the blades
of the ceiling fan,
and my skin
seems familiar once more.
When my feet
find the cold floor,
I lift up
and put my weight on them--
hoping, moment-
arily, to carry on ascending.

7/31/09

The Night Repairman

In his toolbag, there are candles,
long tapers like the stiff fingers
of giant's dolls. He uses matches,
strike-on-box, and the flame begins 
long before the matchhead leaves 
the end of its textured runway.

8/3/09

Night flares up, from time to time,
just like this. The firemen
are no use. What is broken--a line,
a song, the handle of a door
into the life you always wanted--
can not be joined with cellotape.

He is short, and, like a toad,
his eyes blink from the sides.

The Night Repairman

In his toolbag, there are candles,
long tapers like the stiff fingers
of a giant's doll. He uses matches,
strike-on-box, and the flame begins 
long before the matchhead leaves 
the end of its textured runway.

Night flares up, from time to time,
just like this. The firemen
are no use. What is broken--a line,
a song, the handle of a door
into the life you always wanted--
can not be joined with cellotape.

He is short, and, like a toad,
his eyes blink from the sides.
He takes his coffee black.

8/11/09

In his toolbag there are ointments
for the eyelids, powders to inhale.
Arsenic fills his bubble level.

Keep him busy with idle talking,
keep telling the truth to confuse him.
Watch his webbed hands, both of them,
how they delight in the leverage
of the spanner, the heft of a crowbar.
Admire from a distance, just in case.

Father-Son Conversation
	to J.V.P. (Jan. 24-27, 2006)

You don't have to turn the music up loud
to hear the sound of hopefulness grating
its jowls, grinding a pipe in its teeth.

Dear imagination of a boy, my round idea,
you will not know the callouses on my hand.
I will not teach you to wave hello.

You came in waving goodbye, in your way,
and the sound of your own music filled
the hallway between this place and another.

Beauty is not for everyone, but you gathered
the light from the hospital into your face.
To be brutally honest, I loved you.

I tried to hold you carefully by the stem,
but you were determined to fall, and falling,
blaze up like an evening populated with cloud.

You wore your mother's womanly lips, pursed
in a pleasant smile, but your eyes you protected
from the too-bright world we learn to call home.

The music within me is quiet, but persistent.
One day, like you, I will return to being the song.
Beneath my eyelids, too, runs the sound of water.

Beneath this world, another, and another.
Who would give me a map to find you, the paper
superimposed with a constantly moving "X"?

Noone has to shout to garner my attention.
I will tread the water itself to listen up close.
I will throw myself into an ocean of hearing.

To me, you were first a synapse, then a son.
Now grief sparks again in my dome-covered brain.
I row the underground waters, lantern in hand.

8/13/09

I tried to hold you carefully, but goodbye
was already on your lips, a silent prayer.
I will go on speaking to you as long as I live.

Leave    awhile      the dark
no trace    has come    persistent
of me     already     dawn

8/17/09

Invisible Ink

"Here lies one whose name was writ in water."
-John Keats epitaph

Some poems are not meant to be read. This
is one of them. This is not a poem but a
practice drill for the scribe of forgetting.

A record player can spin as well as a Sufi.
A match can burn as bright as a monk on fire.
Yet never do we ask one to stand for the other.

Do not let this poem decieve you into thinking
distillation of experience will end up words.
More likely, it is fire, then smoke and ash.

Who can see the cords of oak in the embers?
Who can spin and burn like a man in love?
Only the scribe of a poem never meant to be read.

Only a child writing in wet beach sand, who 
contains the crashing waves in a single glance.
Feed us, we are hungry, chant the masses.

Let us take shelter beneath your absurdities.
Only the scribe can un-write their mortal dreams.
He dips the quill in their ear to draw out the poison.

The nib singes the paper, and looks like ink.
What you thought indelible cools to brittleness.
Washed in the light from a lantern, words blur.

The scribe wets a finger, and turns the page.

8/18/09

The mind is a terrible place to live or die.
Here lies a poem written for noone in particular.

Father-Son Conversation
	to J.V.P. (Jan. 24-27, 2006)

Mining for seeds in the pomegranite,
each blood-red bead near bursting
at my touch.

8/24/09

Get something going. Fast and loose,
the jackrabbit's back legs
kicking up dust and pawing the air.
The mechanism flips, and we flip
with it, whizzing in the gyroscope.

Ex

She called me to tell me
she'd slept with our rommate
as soon as I moved out.
She wanted to hear me moan
with disapproval. I kept quiet.
She wanted to hear the flush
of anger in my face, the vein
in my throat against the receiver
quickening with this news.
No dice. I knew she wanted to try
new things, was happy she could
be free now, that's what I said.
Once, the tensile strength of silence
between us became electric,
a held breath full of possibility.

8/25/09

I recall the beige carpet
of my small back-house apartment
in the hills, where I sat talking.
The world had abandoned her. I knew
"the world" meant me, an island
in space, one man in a house
full of women in South San Francisco.
But more: her five-year lover,
and once best friend. She wanted
me to think of them in the shower,
and the time we all almost ended up
in bed together. She wanted me to hurt
a little, sure, but more than this--
to know I, too, was capable
of love's complicated untidiness,
of letting the fog roll over me,
an absentminded chain-smoker.

The hair-dye faded, and I pulled
the ring from my nose. I started 
to dress like anyone, my new costume,
stopped fancying myself, as we did
in those days, a character
from Puccini's La Boheme. I went
undercover with all my desires,
even art, and especially love.
I remember stroking the carpet
as we talked, pretending to be
adults, how much I needed
some softness then, the fibers
giving a little, just enough.

# # #

8/26/09

Artifice

Sure, there isn't much left
but technique, all in the wrist,

as they say, when casting out,
and also in reeling--to tire

the fish, from a wake of silver
into a drowning torpedo, then

to hoist and flick, all wrist,
scale-clad, into the bucket.

Art is artificial, the fanning
gills taking in poison air.

I could write anything next,
and the gills would remain,

sharp-edged, a set of ribs,
as tender and tough as words.

# # #

8/27/09

That's Not Rain

Well, here I am again, with myself
ready to argue, or spit. Can you
spit in your own eye? It takes
some practice, no doubt, this much
self-disdain, not to mention
a firm grasp on the law of gravity, 
some understanding, even, of
hydrodynamics, good aim, quick
reflexes, and plenty of time
to work up to it with brooding.
You can see how tough it is
to master the art of self-loathing.
Don't take someone like Nietszche
so lightly next time you see him.
Be kind to Rimbaud, stop taking
those snapshots of Oscar and Britney.
They only like the attention
for the way it hollows them out,
leaving space for the poison,
a reason to ball up saliva under
the tounge, and test the wind.

I remember stroking the carpet
as we talked, pretending to be
adults, how much I needed
some softness then, the fibers
giving a little, just enough.

La Casa De Maria

"Look long enough at an object until it begins to look back at you." -GMH

The stones look down
the pathway
smeared with dust
and I look down
its facets,
faucet-sound of
fountain water,
a splash of mud,
the statuary
scowling--so much beauty
disapproving, under
the granite veils,
half-lidded eyes
under the oaks,
dark leaves
brittle with dryness
sparking under foot.

# # #

Down the pathway,
stones, whistless,
gleeing themselves
with dust rolls
congealing,
the faucet-sound
of water,
pietritius, trickling,
of which the
statuary dispparove,
half-lids wincing
under granite veils,
the dark leaves gleaning
sparks of otherworld.

As if True

What is furtive becomes obvious--behold
the night sky; it was never lost,
and glances from the blue-swathed moon
were not an omen,
but the burning inward eye you always
knew to trust. The hidden, looked at
sideways is more solid
than this chair,
composed, like a pointilist,
from particles of dust.

9/1/09

Wherein my Cat Becomes Moses

My wife is sleeping, so I close the door,
and minutes later find it open. The cat,

on padded instruments of walking,
creeps up, and with a cotton ball thump

commands the obstacle before her
to hinge apart, and let her through.

Little Moses, for you, it is always
this easy. When you sleep at our feet

your whiskers twitch with dreaming,
and sometimes you paw the air,

as though pursuing a pillar of flame.
You have soft fur in lieu of a beard,

and your only commandments to us
were this: stop arguing! You love

each other. And placing yourself
on four legs between us, you'd yowl

until our shouting became laughter.
Now I must explain that she is sleeping.

Now I must offer two kinds of food,
in separate bowls, to stop your song.

Teach us to sing, unbidden, like this.
Teach us the old songs from Jericho.

Retract your claws like a miracle.
Lead us out of Egypt, little one.

9/2/09

Anniversary
	(to J.V.P, Jan. 24-27, 2006)

Out of the pools of winter, skating light,
the transom parcelling darkness,
a bowl of apples lit like a disco,
the kitchen under water breaking dawn.

Four lines ago, we spoke of winter.
Four years ago, we would not recognize
ourselves, under the ice, lip-veins bluing
eyelids frozen open like disbelief.

9/3/09

And who should say now we resemble
ourselves, blinking in the early light
of a long winter passing casually?
We have come to believe we know

what the flicker of dust on a page,
the wide scowl of sun through fence
slats, and all the swimming minnows
on our porch step, cast by the prism,

actually mean. What does breathing
mean, except--you're still alive?
But a held breath does not mean death,
any more than lamplight is the sun.

Where do we go from here?--the age-
old question. Shall I answer us
in riddle, or in rhyme? Shall I trip
through music blindly? Here are notes--

the bold and soaring G-chords, stitched
to a heart, stitched, in turn, to a
shirtsleeve, on the outside, exposed
to the chill of a latecomer spring.

9/4/09

Take us away, Sibelius, you have earned it.
The darkness recedes one icicle at a time.
Our breath becomes transparent again,
our footfalls cease to crackle and crunch.

We go, from here, as quick and as slowly
as thawing requires.

9/7/09

We go, from here, as quickly or slowly
as thawing requires. We go from here,
through darkness, dripping good intention,
easing ourselves in to the simmering dawn.
