18 March 14

Geopositioning

I am the dot. I am the beaky-nosed dot,
travelling north-by-northwest. I am
the ghost dot drifting through buildings,
saint dot, blue halo pulsing. See me from 
above. See me and be me, one right for east,
two lefts for south. You can't outrun your 
own blue shadow. There is nowhere on Earth
you can go that I won't be you now. There is
only me-as-you, trapped in a tilt-board
labyrinth, steel ball rolling too slow and then
too fast past open manhole covers and around
the squared corners of L-shaped wooden buildings.
This is not a game. This is our life. I have
pinned you to the back of the display case,
labelled by your postcode. I show you off,
saying, "Look at the me trapped in 3D."
No one is impressed. Just keep walking.

19 March 14

Door closes. The smell of rain.

23 March 14

Historic Spring

The gate wafts in the smell of rain,
its must and electric tang, the thoughts
of bracken telegraphing through the air.

The moss, a distant cousin, goes on soaking.
The just-add-water sachets return to life,
because they are simple. Because we are not.

The banked graveyards of Europe retain
wartime dead like bulbs in a raised garden bed,
while moss, grass, and clover compete for cover.

Too little water; too little sun. The pride and hope
of living things goes dormant for awhile, snoring
its spore-clouds upward, detonating in fruit.

Too much daylight; too much rainfall. Vines corkscrew
around a diplomat's neck, climb like approval ratings,
roots deploy their supply routes underground.

Green flags roll out their propaganda campaigns.
The insects are convinced. The airlifts begin.
Beetles rifle the leaf cover, ready for casualties.

25 March 14

A record player can spin as free as a Sufi.
A match can burn as bright as a monk on fire.
Feed us, we are hungry, murmur the crowds.

Walt Whitman among the beautiful limbs

They branch in all directions, fingers curling, 
toes tucked in, bent at the elbow or knee, cut off
from the bough and stacked in cords, hardening.

They will bury one hundred parts of men today, 
the weight of several corpses, taken in pieces
from ghost-white soldiers, given a stick to bite.

2 April 14

Hobby Horse

Climb up on my back. For a small additional fee,
you can use the cort, and I will buck and neigh.

The art of suppressing the gag reflex lets me take
the bit more easily, swallow the words I might say.

Such as: "Goodnight, Empire. It really has been a gas.
Good crack. Fair play. Fun times, foul weather and all.

I never would have known what lay beneath my scalp
until you crowned me with that dunce's cap of tar.

And to think, I had twenty finger- and toenails,
far too many for anyone, so thanks for removing a few.

Sure, the branding iron's hot, the tattoo needle stings.
But this is just how some people show pride in what they own.

I am yours now, completely. The love songs have it right, 
how good be in your own place, how nice to belong to someone.

3 April 14

disclosure fist sound bananas divinity freedom liquor gems become link how nitro nineteen pillbox hound awkward solid hamster enzyme ditch

Canteen

The last gems of liquor 
splash down in my glass,
and I snap the pill box shut.
We have won the race 
with our fists, awkwardly,
hands down, sound bananas, we leapt
the ditch toward freedom
and cleared the hounds.
So this is freedom.
This side contains the same amount
of nitrogen in the air.
This side has full disclosure,
people becoming other people,
hamsters as pets, as well.
What was it all for, Charlie?
Nineteen more minutes of linked 
hands and holy prayers
to that bombshell divinity?
I stir the enzymes into my drink.
I tilt my head back preemptively,
like I did at the dentist.
How do we know this road leads
back to the enemy base?
Go first. I'm right behind you.

4 April 14

Problem [DO AS PROSE POEM]

It's not a problem problem. It's just a problem
for the person whose problems are more important
than yours, or anyone's. So now it is your
problem, please drop everything, including your
trousers, our customary salute. The problem
will not go away by itself, though intervention
may compound it, in which case you become the
problem, and must, I'm afraid, be shot. Please
treat this as a matter of some urgency. Problems
like this don't come around every day. Please
hold out your hand for the branding iron. Please
hold out your hand for the pay check. Please don't
tell anyone about our-little-secret problem, please
be sure to finish up half an hour ago. Problems
like this are a daily occurrence. You must learn
to prioritise, must learn to write your name
clearly at the bottom of every invisible list.
Please submit the solution in mirror writing.
Please submit in all nine official languages.
Please submit to the problem, please admit
publicly that the problem has always been you.

22 April 14

April

Anticlimactic blossom, juvenile and boozy,
tipples the bees in swarm, barmaids
pull green taps on twisted pipes,
frothing up a golden head of pollen.

O, the youthfulness of arrogance.
The bayonets of crocus arc into view,
blackbirds swap their arcane ciphers,
cracking the real, unredacted news.

Squirrels in fiasco, grooving the bark
in barber-pole double helix, shifting
a chance godsend of celebratory petals,
once dew-bejewelled, now confetti.

Here is the sallow-green heart of things,
sap coursing through like amphetamine,
the sticky truth, its plausible deniability,
tapped by a beak, beading the trunk.

Nothing has ever been as easy as this--
loose soil steaming with overfed grubs,
a richness of embarrassment, on it goes--
an arms race toward the beating sun.

4 May 14

Aerial Manoeuvres

In dreams, I am convinced
I have always been able to fly
the updraft from the cliff
will catch me like my mother
when I launched from the stairs
on a bird-brained impulse,
avian memory, invincible faith.
Airline rituals reassure me--
the act is routine ad tedium,
tyres drift up off the tarmac,
metal wings skate the air.
"Falling doesn't hurt," we joke,
"it's hitting the ground."
So I fall, and fall into myself,
gasping awake on a feather bed.
Larks slice through the dawn,
and part of me goes with them,
diving toward the updrafts,
hoping, mid-air, to be caught.

Beginner Piano

Those diplomatic notes. The tremor in their voice.
Adrenalised, they bop from beam to bouncy floorboard,
tyrannical acoustics, no horseplay here allowed.

A bee blunders into the flytrap, the petal's touch
becomes ham-fisted, sausage-fingered, damp with fear.
"Let me stop you there," and repeat, repeat more slowly.

Train the fingers to the brain, the horse to the reign,
heave into the sticky mud against the rock-stuck plough.
"I think you're making progress," smiles the farmer.

Lighter now, and lighter still, ballet hour at the orphanage,
the promise ribbons streaming from unsteady pirouettes,
toe point and finger pad pressed to a tutu's grubby pink.

Ambassadors of music, know when to shut your traps.
Or deploy the careful phrasing of an interrogated man.
Play until the music is all that remains in the room.

Misquoting Samuel Johnson

Nothing focuses the mind.
Nothing focuses like nothing.
The mind, like a hanging.
Nothing focuses a hanging
like those of sound mind.
Focus the mind. Nothing.
In focus, nothing minds.
Hang the focused mind.
Hang nothing. Hangnail.
Nothing. Focus. Nothing.

The Age of the Incredible

It flashes across our eyes, spiralling open the pupils,
which stutter over lines, pausing at the start of words
that progress--each more impossible than the last:

Human fingers are a delicacy in China. They incinerate
their own children to boil bath water in North Korea.
You won't believe what happens next. Go on, one click

to play the director's cut of a killer's YouTube rants.
Click to light on fire. Click to behead. Read commentary
on election results from certified netherworld trolls.

We longed for the day when anything would be possible.
The nerve endings open and close like spiked anemones,
tweeting like chicks in a tangled nest, hungry for more.

The grey sponge laps it up like spilled kerosene. We scroll
in our sleep, through hyperlinked dreams, Photoshopped
for effect. Remember. This is what we said we always wanted.


1 June 14

5 June 14

Allegory

Some leaves are young and full of poison.
Other leaves are old and fibre-tough.
Chewing is part of digesting. Chewing takes time.

I chew over pages from my diary, sketchbook, calendar.
I ball the sticky pulp against one cheek.
I strain out the saliva against my teeth.

What remains is not the past, but its corpse.
What remains is the funeral pyre of a forgotten king.
Heaped with worthless possessions, drifting to sea.

The fish will surface to gulp at his escort of flies.
The gulls will plunge through meniscus to impale them.
Some will defecate on the treasure-laden body.

Others will return to the cliffs, streaking them white.
In one cupped pocket of guano, a seed finds ground.
A shoot emerges, and from it away peels a leaf.

A rodent, hunting for eggs, will come to chew it.
His eyes burning with ammonia, he will take a nibble.
Young leaves are sometimes soft, but full of poison.

Old leaves are often harmless, but fibrous, tough.
The rat will strain green mush against his teeth.
No one will come to bury him. The rat is not a king.

The Computer Programmer's Wife

(Using terminology from programming that appears to be English but is not)

17 June 14

20 June 14

cookies, parent-child, object, class, variable, string, array, token, serialised, red-black tree, node, append, argument, shell, true and false, null, value, constant, cache, domain, server

The Computer Programmer's Wife

When did cookies become the problem? A cache of them
would surely crumble, and any parent-child relationship
with true, objective class would limit their rate.

It's hard, some days, to bring him out of his shell.
True and false blur, two sides of a variable token,
and the argument to string things along approaches null.

He climbs to the top (or is it bottom?) of that garish
red-black tree, and surveys his domain, thinks me a server,
a constant, but my value is becoming indeterminate of late.

21 June 14

I think of Mrs. Blake, lamenting how often her husband
was away in heaven, in places she could not follow, murmuring
a glossolalia that only the angels dared to comprehend.

The debugger is his god, and the compiler his devil.
Such words--I see him piling a mound of compost, 
pitchfork aloft, as bugs surface the warm, wet heap.

He has become more device than person, a machine for making
machines, the tinker in his toyshop, oiling his own elbows,
clockmaker clucking his tongue, boiler-maker fuming.

3 August 14 

The Man with the Kindest Face

The rear-view glimpse is fleeting
as he lets you into the lane.

His features behind your IV bag
wobble like a funhouse mirror.

He might not have a face at all
or change it like a set of masks--

behind a newspaper in the waiting room,
sliding over to make room on the bus.

You resolve next time to look at him,
risk letting him look back at you.

You taste the salt in your throat,
and you hear him ask, "What's wrong?"

You smile at him and say, "Nothing."
And you mean it. "Nothing at all."

11 August 14

Slow Train, Saturday Night

"The moon is an elegy to the light of the sun."
-D.A. Powell

Nothing about the mid-night air is friendly. It 
drapes, cloak-like around our shoulders, each
in our separate skins, faces lit by screens.

The avuncular smell of whisky drifts in,
mingled with soil-stained cricket whites
and a tweeine girl's too-sweet perfume.

You get the idea. The air around you is not
your own, the seat beside you over-subscribed,
your feet arranged against touching or scuffs.

Then someone starts singing, and you hide your face
in your hand against a carriage-full of football
chants. But the voice remains singular, slow.

It must be an old seafarer's tune, by the shape,
the kind grown men grunt after too many beers.
The woman in dyed dreadlocks beside you smirks.

And now you are on a boat, swaying and bobbing 
together under a too-bright moon, the luggage
racks creaking overhead, damp air laced with salt.

This is the crew you could not save from drowning.
The ones you worked with, ate with, second family,
an island in the water, beyond the horizon's slope.

13 August

Look around you, these are the faces that will appear
at the bottom of every glass, reflected in every puddle
the relentless ocean pulls us back, sifts each one to silt.

The wave-slap track rolls on, the singing stops.

16 August

Pain Quotidien, St. Pancras
    for Anja

Pronounce it right, it is sustenance.
But I crumb it with my Anglo tongue.

So, give us this day our dose of hurt,
the doughy lump in the throat.

It is everywhere, ubiquitous,
as commonplace as salt in tears.

We swirl leaves at the bottom of the teacup
trying to reconfigure the future.

They depart for Paris, Brussels, Amsterdam,
squeezing the body for remembrance.

We print the bread with our fingertips,
tear it apart absentmindedly.

Give us this food for the days ahead,
softened in tea, shared between friends.

Slow Train, Saturday Night

    "The moon is an elegy to the light of the sun."
    -D.A. Powell

Nothing about the mid-night air is friendly. It 
drapes, cloak-like around our shoulders, each
in our separate skins, faces lit by screens.

The avuncular smell of whisky drifts in,
mingled with soil-stained cricket whites
and a tweenie girl's too-sweet perfume.

The air around you is not your own, the seat 
beside you is over-subscribed by one, your feet
are arranged against touching or scuffs.

Then someone starts singing, and you hide your face
in your hand against a carriage-full of football
chants. But the voice remains singular, slow.

It must be an old maritime tune, by the shape,
the kind grown men grunt after too many beers.
The woman in dyed dreadlocks beside you smirks.

And now you are on this boat, swaying and bobbing 
together under a too-bright moon, the luggage
racks creaking overhead, damp air laced with salt.

This is the crew you could not save from drowning.
This is the ship the villagers picked for loot,
sifting coins from seaweed in the blue light.

Now you see them at the bottom of your glass,
staring back from puddles on the too-solid road,
while the undertow swipes your ankles, beckoning.

The conductor crackles overhead, announcing your stop.
You sway into dock, and the doors slide back, curtains 
swiped like a rug underfoot, revealing blue concrete below.

26 September 14

You lack confidence among the willow trees, their downbent
tempo, the timbre of slow dripping water, and nothing
disturbs the up-clambering spider, as in the rhyme.

Nothing has come of nothing for quite some time.
The nothing that is not there, and the nothing
we wish would be. This hole to be papered over
in the chest, each of us born with a fatal flaw.

The corkscrew plies away the bottle topper
the rooting pig shovels clods with a snout
disaster comes on a Thursday, dressed smart
and removes his hat before entering the door.

Coded into the spiral, wound tight, and waiting,
art imitates our natures in planned obsolescence
how efficient the machinery of stasis, whacking
our mole-heads downward into each plastic hole.

Do I sound bitter? Let me turn myself to wine,
to sorghum, to jellied beans and candied eel,
whoso delights the tongue has a chance at infamy,
albeit slim, but you need a ticket to be in it.

29 September 14

plunk, edgeways, merchandise, upstanding, rhapsody, legendary, weakness, cross-tree, victim, liquefied, spiritualise, tawny

Plunk

Jostling edgeways into the queue, obedient to merchandise,
we hold, upstanding in rhapsodic trace, our glowing machines,
portable hypnotic slice of thought-benumbing heaven, 

comprised of glowing screen and global palm-warmer, 
eye-test etched into liquefied crystal.

Behold the cross-tree, tawny hart, sacred nickel-plated pistol--
we have spiritualised this glimmering fish, its fanning WiFi tail, 
baptised it in toilets, launched it from heights, 

how it whistles on descent whenever friction fails, lets out 
a muted plastic "plunk", the cracking of the grail.

30 September 14

The man with the kindest face pumps up your bicycle tire

He keeps his eye on the task, fitting the hose and squeezing the bulb, like a man-sized pollinator. The tyre lets out a sigh of relief. "Good as new," he lies, admiring your beat-up Schwinn. His eyes are trained to see chrome, not rust, skip over the tar-blacked chain. He glances up. He best be on his way.

24 October

The man with the kindest face has change for a twenty

He doesn't look rich. Yet his pockets overflow with coins. "How much do you need?" he asks, and you tell him. You want to tell him more---that you need to believe, understand, be heard. He extends his closed hand like a magician. You expect nothing. You expect a dove to fly out from his sleeve. You open your hand, beneath his, and wait.

27 October

Deoxyribonucleic Acid

The corkscrew plies away the bottle top
the rooting pig shovels clods with a snout
disaster comes on a Thursday, dressed smart
and removes his hat before entering the door.

Coded into the spiral, wound tight, and waiting,
art imitates our natures in planned obsolescence--
how efficient the machinery of stasis, whacking
our mole-heads downward into each plastic hole.

Do I sound bitter? Let me turn myself to wine,
to sorghum, to jellied beans and candied eel.
Make a wish, unravel. Paper over the hole
in the chest, each of us born with a flaw.

Finding the best words and putting them in the best order

Oil slick? Redundant. Shimmer? Overused.

You came in the night, and stomped on our clothes.
You drank all the beer in the refrigerator.
I never loved life as much as I loved you.
One can't un-swallow the tablet very easily.

billionaire gymnastic things gurgling bully describes pigeon sexual air adorable

Protest

The day lights up in sherbet,
signals its intentions
in the sheen of pigeon feather,
the shine of gurgling tar.

The victory axe is hung
above the shotgun in the parlour,
these weapons are introduced
to tang the air with thrill.

Adorable and gorgeous
contend on opposite shoulders
while the billionaire coughs
trying to un-swallow the pill.

These gymnastic things we do
give off a sexual air
in the sweat that describes
cloth stretched against our skin.

The holdup in your drive today
is due in part to bullying
and chunks of things, unidentified
lying quietly in the road.

8 November 14

Senate Majority

They came in the night, and stomped on our clothes.
They drank all the beer in the refrigerator.

When we did not salute them, they became angry
pounding the walls to find where we hid our daughters.

They tipped over each teacup slowly onto the floor
punctuating their national anthem with a burst.

10 November 14

The man with the kindest face checks your passport

He glances up, then back at your picture, like a game of peek-a-boo. Leafing through the stamps, he seems pleased with your decisions. He presses down his own approval, pulls up quickly not to smudge. Doesn't he ever get bored? He flips the booklet shut like a card trick, levers it back, bowing at the wrist. You take it with both hands, to be sure.

17 November 14

The beautiful limbs, the terrible limbs, buried
in lime and grasping, rigor seizing the mechanism.




Gravity for Poets

Because they thought the stars might fall,
tugged to earth in a final arpeggio
of shattered glass and cosmic fission,
I had to sit them down, recluse after
golden recluse in a row--immoral formalist,
grave surrealist, parabolic beatnick--arranged
like variables (since variable they were)
knowing more of paisley apricots than algebra,
veering dreamers grating on my migraine nerves,
while I gesticulate in mudras, fingering
chalk and physics, pawing the obscure air.
The fabric of the universe is as loose as
varnished wicker, soft as mesh, the rafters
parting slowly as layer upon layer of silt
pours out, Newtonian vengeance. I rifle
my dusted hair, and the imagist stifles
a giggle, offending the narrarive poet's
quiet composure, and then the spitballs
streak like a meteor shower, unguided
by any principle, a storm of cosmic anarchy,
tipped off, no doubt, by some self-proclaimed
experimentalist. Let them riot. Let them float 
off into space, for all I care. Physics is a dull
school riddle, obtuse as a dusty bottle, good
for clever titles, strange conceits, but pliable.
I flick a wad of moistened paper from my tomes,
collect my thoughts, snatching strange quarks
from where they hover mid-air, eradicate
the chalkboard, since blank is best for an ending,
while the surrealist poet holds the empty door,
and once escaped I hop up and click my heels,
suspended weightless for a moment before I fall.

18 November

Caged, happily, tail thumping the bars

19 November

bloodsucker web gone escalator corrosive amazing cows crop island guest bunny zebra surgeon founder amnesiac evacuate pageant crack pavement old

About peeling versus slicing eggs

Power Breakfast

I learned to tap and crack the top like a surgeon,
an earthquake evacuating the amnesiac island,
bloodsucker churning the white pavement for fun.

I was corrosive. I was amazing. I scourged the crops,
uninvited guest, stole black from the zebra, gone
the good times, the web of disaster spreading.

The zig-zag escalator travelled well, cows tipped,
bunnies scurried for cover, while my pageant
of destruction went on, revelling the old traditions.

Peeling was an afterthought.

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

Peering overboard, I acquaint myself
with the sculpted bas relief of water,
as squid and offspring tidy their affairs,
pint-sized gloves fiddling the surface,
picking chastely at some flaky decay.
A cloud damns them, the water darkens,
sluggish they descend and head for shore.
The ocean has its categories, like chessmen,
bishop crabs on site, tending coral churches,
a greased baleen rook sounds his low cry,
while peon minnows gaze across the beige
undersea battleground. Of course the queen--
let us call her "Lynette"--is a manatee
who has aged very well, considering.

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

Christmastime

Kitty tangles deep in the tinsel,
explains her greed in modern dance,
tumbling through the sparkling marsh,
metallic stubble, recollecting
and replaying millennia of training
on iridescent bugs like trinket charms--
impossible to ignore, marching tattoo
in a long, unbroken, rosary chain
across the sparking desert sand.
Undeterred by crumbs of muffin, she 
angles in behind the mop, perceiving
shades of glint that make our senses
seem fallow, weak and wan--she jumps!--
at what appears like nothing, prompting
chuckles, then sulks off in estrangement,
whiffing bourbon and egg nog in the air,
past the scattered chess pieces, pausing
to nibble a Parcheesi man's round top.

Search Party

A pond is waking in Yorkshire,

28 November 14

Wildflowers cluster like fistfuls of sweets,
broadcasting their codes to the unclean bees,
drifting the air-ways like Venetian gondoliers,
crawling the riverbank, clawing the filthy humus,
dancing an infinity of directions on the ferns.

Life is constructed of unruly intersections--sum
of marching bandit ants and bug-eyed voles
interlocution of unlikely cohorts: stink beetles,
rug rats, poison petals, grateful for decay.
We cannot escape what we never understood.

29 November

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

flickering, greens, entropy, talent, destruction, nostalgia, vantage, punch, bulk, loaf, arranged, spindle, thyme, shock, twitch

Also:

Mention one or more extinct animals

On Trains

You're not there yet, but you're not where you were,
as the flickering aspen, cedar, and oak blur in greens
and brown. You are considering entropy, its talent
for destruction in small, nostalgic gestures--consider
the ravelling of your sleeve, which from this vantage,
bobs in time on your paunch, hovering above your bulk
like a holy loaf arranged for the catechism, suspended
like a spindle whirring on the Singer that would knit it
in time to the shock and twitch of the machinery you now
inhabit, its strain against friction, its victory going
forward and forward, wasting its molecules into the air.

3 December 14

Arson

"Fire!" they yelled, and fire it was, our
ears rung with the shock of it, raising our
blood, mercurial, seething, reddening scars,
head beading, heart thumping a drum in the war.

Woman with child, singed man in suit, come quick,
large puffs of updraft carry the ghosts of ash,
drop soot like candy for the developed-world's sick,
toes singed, hair stubbled, blinking out the flash.

Hate is a building ablaze, ablaze it will not stop,
short bursts of windows rejecting the too-hot gas,
early dawn of factory flames, evening curtain drops
black velvet smog, coating the ground with glass.

Red is the colour of my true love's frozen heart
listen how it hisses when our kindled secret starts.

5 December 14

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

roaring, tricky, simile, harrow, smack, fiction, mesh, charm, stench, blunt, dye, rule, soak, sunflower, distress

Also:

Mention another art form besides poetry

Political Asylum [NEW TITLE]

Tricky, the branches through fog,
droplets form, a simile with buds,
soaked twigs and twiglets weave a mesh
that charms the air to condensation.

Resin masks batik for moisture's dye,
printing sunflower spots, roselets,
and dogtooth in the distress of bark,
harrowing the trunk in black rivulets.

The mist roars in, and the rain smacks down,
blunt ruler of the forest in this fiction,
churning the stench of leaf rot, mulch,
laying claim to the dry world, pissing.

Pacific Storm

Knee-deep in sound, rain grinds down our senses, 
dampening the apron of the lawn
with conviction, releasing its homeless
drops over Seal Beach as well as Alexandria,

glissando, tom-tom, power sander,
O Homeric moisture, do what you can.

Ticklish Banquet

Bundle the calf thighs, pump up the pig's blood,
blithe scimitars cut strife-cramped lumps of flesh,
magnified by perspective, the long dining table,
pierce the potatoes, those meaningless spuds, their
certainty boiled to flaky white fluff, beaked by forks,
laboured over by blob-pasty gravy, the chloride tang
of table salt, flung over the shoulder by appointment
to fortune, wasted for luck, fork stuck, we consume.

Trapper

Headgear of unending quietude,
fuzzy old-fashioned unicorn hat,
startling in your practicality,
flaps dangling a lambskin froth.

Are you dating the young librarian?
Or sloshing through puddles en route
to a kill? Do not hesitate, Einstein
to show your work in ironic equations.

spider webs, about a long string
of vowels as in the Dutch tongue?

oil up your single-geared bicycle,
the knob of fur set on your forehead,
workingman's bindi, reminder of "shh".

Greatest of feats was your scatology,
knowing the creature last ate on a Tuesday,
fingering the tracks in shallow red mud,
saying nothing, your hot head aglow.

3 Jan 15

You have to give yourself permission to wander into the woods.
You must resolve not to leave at the first sight of bear shit.
You must learn all that you can from the woodpecker's code.

Here is the platform for witch-burning, burnished with ash.
Here the enduring ignorance of the nearby lichen and moss.
The slugs pore over the news with their long fat tongues.

An attack comes in from the treetops, wishing for autumn.
Dry leaves on a gust hammer rocks, confirm their resolve.
The wish of the forest is a primal command, all follow!

4 Jan 14

The wish of the forest is a crepe-paper nest, be-swarmed.
Merciless, the hope for rain in fat globules to come.
You must let yourself wander, it is into the woods we go.

8 Jan 14

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

booze, organic, orion, grazing, chased, clink, panda, timber, thumping, peopled, trousers, smoked, upstream, riverbank, portion

Also:

Refer to a war, battle, or famous warrior

Southern Cross

The ocean booze is frothing its certified organic brew.
On the other side of Orion, pouched mammals quietly graze.
Chased from the trees, Lorikeets clink their disapproval.
Panda's cousin, Wombat, meanders thickly through the grass.

At the farthest point from Gallipoli, red eucalyptus bleeds.
Shedding its whorls of timber, thumped by pickaxe Magpies,
who sound piezoelectric sirens in a throng peopled by Cicadas,
leaving their crisp trousers on the underside of smoky boughs.

Upstream, fresh water and fewer problems, so they thought,
portions of riverbank spiked with turrets of mangrove root.

The Four Gentlemen

Plum Blossom - Winter - Unyielding Loyalty

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

eternally, fireplace, custom, revolution, convince, brink, seine, writhing, upset, wildness, fertile, combed, comparison, gore, puzzle

Also:

Mention several different types of plants or flower

Plum Blossom

Peering above the rust-brown hedgerow,
past twigs glazed in black moss,
you greet me with flowers at the end
of a long journey through the dark.

Convince me of your wild fertility,
my bold revolutionary friend, here
on the brink of despair, you offer up
pink sparks from a sunlit fireplace.

The pear tree is sleeping in hard sap,
while the apple puzzles strange dreams,
your uncombed branches writhe to the sky,
flecked with the gore of their birthing.

The Seine is gathering petals of ice,
the Thames runs thick, black, and slow.
You upset our customs and comparisons
like unrequited love and early snow.

Bamboo - Summer - Noble Humility

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

maryland, hunting, await, alexandria, feeble, corridor, headlong, farewell, penny, basin, sleet, tank, o'er, hurried, site

Refer to a particular sensation

Life began in a basin,
progressed to a plastic tank,
now you gather the cupric wisdom
of pennies flipped into a pond.

You never hurried your farewells,
but pitched headlong skywards,
while roots went hunting for clay,
making yourself a corridor.

Kyoto, Alexandria, suburban Maryland--
you homestead where you find yourself,
await transplant orders to unseen sites,
tingling with the present tense.

Chrysanthemum - Autumn - Lofty Reclusion
[Study Chrysanthemums a bit more]

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

ardent, sprawling, swoon, regard, rhyming, delicious, scrub, stress, staggers, character, perpetual, absolute, crib, afterlife, cain

Also:

Refer to an an extreme or intemperate landscape


"Mum" is the word, and silence your character,
draped in sound-proof petals, you stagger
deeper into the mountain pass, sprawling
your light, delicious scent across the valley.

Ardent in your quest for solitude, no swoon
or swagger, but perpetual shedding of stress
in brown-white petals ushers you into this afterlife,
bedding into the crib that will serve for a grave.

You would not do well on bleached desert sand,
but you would try--scrubbing the dust for moisture,
and here in the crevice of the cliff, you regard
the green horizon, Cain repenting in banishment.

19 January 15

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

hallway, substance, devices, mock, mundane, package, generous, dumps, quickening, task, trim, er, escaped, answering, surreal

Also:

Include a childhood memory

The man with the kindest face accompanies you through childhood memories

The hallway is dark, so he takes your hand, substantial. Electronic devices in unseen pockets mock you with their ringtones. "Do not answer," he advises. You cannot see his mundane smile, or the heavy package you trip over, dumping you on the wet ground, quickening your pulse. He lifts you by the elbow, is generous with his drinking flask, focused on the task of getting you through this place, escaped back into the light. He says, "We'll be there as soon as we can."

21 Jan 15

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

mammoth, authentic, landlord, grinning, footnote, cleanup, chimney, parsley, cherished, hopelessly, surrounding, lacquer, whale, tom, diary

Also:

Include a familiar smell

The man with the kindest face at the La Brea Tar Pits

Your eyes begin to water from the tar. Mammoth tusks grin, and whale ribs rise like porcelain chimneys, glossed in black lacquer. "Dear diary," you will write, "the smell of their death surrounds me. Tom abandoned me for his cherished girl, I stare into this pit and await hopelessly his return." The uniformed man beside you--landlord? guide? cleanup crew? fishes a handkerchief from his pocket. "It's just the tar," you want to protest, but your fingers pull it closer, brush a smatter of lunchtime parsley from your cheek. You wish, before handing it back, you'd made note of the monogram, crucial footnote to your tale, but he is--like the others--now long gone.

Adrift in a Coracle (on the Severn)

26 Jan 15

The man with the kindest face cleans your office window

He appears outside your office window,
inevitable rag in hand, swiping shrill tones
across the zebra stripes of rain splash,
deciphering the smudge transparent.
You wish he could decode the street below--
downtown Chicago, Manhattan, the City
toy cars, blondes, brunettes swarming
the columns that balance a saucer of cloud,
shared umbrella, feathered at the edges,
adrift like soft fur in the high breezes.
You look down, and your interpreter is gone,
lowering himself on the platform he shares
with buckets and squeegees, saving ammonia,
counting the sips of his long-cooled coffee,
going down, level by level, without you.

27 Jan 15

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

brink, careless, vine, quilt, cartoon, zu, loosening, console, spout, posture, solace, tuna, disorder, daniel, quaint

Also:

Make up an unusual name for a person and include it in the poem

Tao Sketchbook

Lao Tzu drifts in a spinning coracle
his posture irrefutable,
loosening his silk belt
with a gesture 
on the brink of carelessness
he floats beneath the vines
and over the spawning tuna
quaint as a cartoon,
the blank page everywhere--
reason his companion, disorder
his lover, he resists the urge
to paddle, lets the river
widen, folds his arms, and sleeps.

31 Jan 15

Chrysanthemum - Autumn - Lofty Reclusion



Romance of the Three Kingdoms
    (After the 1988 video game)

"I ain't gonna study war no more..."
    -American Negro Spiritual

I loved the names of my enemies--the ones
I couldn't pronounce: Dong Zhuo, Liu Bei, 
Sun Jian, wanted to recruit or destroy them,
admiring their proud, pixellated faces.

Long divided kingdoms eventually unite;
long united kingdoms eventually divide--
territories numbered in yellow, magenta, cyan
becoming mine one click at a time.

On the flight to Hong Kong, I keep awake
by watching Platoon on the seat-back screen.
Sgt. Barns executes the village elder.
Lt. Wolfe gives the order to burn it all.

We swore brotherhood in the peach garden
yet diplomacy has small limbs, brief memory.
After losing so much ground to Han Xuan,
I resolve to think more like the machine.

Sun Tzu warned: "When you surrounded an army,
leave an outlet free." I preferred this loophole--
light fires near enemies at the edge of the map,
as the winds change, whole regiments blink away.

The numbers beside their rice supplies tick down
as crude icons of straw huts are crowned with flame
burnt bridges turn rough blue snakes of river
into a barricade against what had to be done.

Eyes singed by long hours at the screen,
the white letters type themselves across
the battlefield: Han Xuan has been executed.
Han Xuan is dead. The fight is over.


Willem Dafoe scene
Napalm

2 Feb 15

We marry our daughters neighbouring warlords,
and they sob sob in monochrome behind a fan
we squeeze the peasants to feed growing armies,
they kneel before the tax collector's cart.

Charlie Sheen is learning the mechanics of war.
Willem Defoe, betrayed and wounded, staggers
out of the jungle as the helicopter pulls away,
the enemy surrounds him, lifting his arms in crucifix.





Romance of the Three Kingdoms
    (After the 1988 video game)

"I ain't gonna study war no more..."
    -American Negro Spiritual

I loved the names of my enemies--the ones
I couldn't pronounce: Dong Zhuo, Liu Bei, 
Sun Jian, wanted to recruit or destroy them,
admiring their proud, pixellated faces.

Long divided kingdoms eventually unite;
long united kingdoms eventually divide--
territories numbered in yellow, magenta, cyan
becoming mine one click at a time.

We marry our daughters to neighbouring warlords,
they sob in monochrome behind a dithered fan
we squeeze the peasants to feed our armies,
they kneel before the tax collector's cart.

On the flight to Hong Kong, I keep awake
by watching "Platoon" on the seat-back screen.
Sgt. Barns executes the village elder.
Lt. Wolfe gives the order to burn it all.

We swore brotherhood in the peach garden
but diplomacy has small limbs, brief memory.
After losing so much ground to Han Xuan,
I resolve to think more like the machine.

Charlie Sheen is learning the mechanics of war.
Willem Defoe, betrayed and wounded, staggers
from the burning jungle. The helicopter pulls away,
Viet Kong close in. He lifts his arms in crucifix.

Sun Tzu warned: "When you surrounded an army,
leave an outlet free." I preferred this loophole--
light fires near opponents at the edge of the map,
as the winds change, whole regiments blink away.

The numbers beside their rice supplies tick down
as crude icons of huts are crowned with flame
burnt bridges turn rough blue snakes of river
into a barricade against what had to be done.

My eyes singed by long hours at the screen,
the white letters sear the battlefield: 
Han Xuan has been captured and executed.
Han Xuan is dead. The fight is over.

You let your hair grow long

from your hobo camp
beneath the railway bridge--
Hanshan making a bed
that will serve for his grave.

The Man With the Kindest Face Whooshes Past

He is the breeze on a summer day in Miami, knee-deep in glare, the driveways slicked in haze, you dismiss the probability of a benevolent presence, and this breath of candy-sweet air is his reply. You continue on your over-lighted route, envying hogs their mud and Ma her fan, hands swollen to catcher's mits, feet sloshing in sock sweat. Then it happens--a cyclist whooshes past, galvanising the tiny lakes in your pores. His back to you, he disappears pedal pump by pump. You never do see his face.

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

Bōs

The aurochs staggers as if drugged,
unhappy prize of the pygmy wolf pack,
shakes the sleet from its coat,
paws the shale with a bruised haunch,
huffing the significance of its defeat,
on a fusion of mud and grass one day
paved into a gran boulevard, bankrolled
by cattle farming in the Loire, home
to a natural foods store, whose labelled
products boast free-range, grass-fed,
the counters stacked with paper packs,
and under the weight, the beast goes down, 
one horn entrenched in muck, the wolves 
break circle, tucking in, the bellowed sound
of the giant's last defeat, crescendoes
across valley, echoes once, and is gone.



The coffee has gone cold, so I swill it into the sink.
The sink-cracks lap it up, revealing their secret map.
A phrenologist's guide to the fused porcelain plates.

The Lord's Prayer
Our Father, which art in heaven,
hallowed be thy name;
thy kingdom come;
thy will be done,
in earth as it is in heaven.
Give us this day our daily bread.
And forgive us our trespasses,
as we forgive them that trespass against us.
And lead us not into temptation;
but deliver us from evil.
For thine is the kingdom,
the power, and the glory,
for ever and ever.
Amen.

Gehalgod

What we remember re-members us to the corpus
riddled with eth and thorn, swallowing consonants
like a good Thane, six thousand miles from Saxony,
memorising the Lord's Prayer in Ancient English
in a West-coast American border town, clicking off
Fæder ures in gringo penance and fifty centigrade.

I sweated through gedæghwamlican fervent recitations
in the locker room, this lingua oscura my round shield,
huero rezando oraciones, doodling dragons onto swords.
I was first to the front of the class, thin and pale,
wheezing out we forgyfað urum gyltendum, pardoning
bullies, crushes, even myself, before the sweet soþlice.

I took a puff of my inhaler, and stood for a moment adorned
in a helmet of secret knowledge, its weight upon my head.



4/3/15

The Prayer

I remember poring over the corpus, a wounded king,
riddled with eth and thorn, swallowing consonants
like a good Thane, six thousand miles from Saxony,
memorising the Lord's Prayer in Ancient English
in a West-coast American border town, clicking off
Fæder ures in gringo penance and fifty Centigrade.

I sweated through daily bread and fevered incantations
in the locker room, this lingua oscura my round shield,
a spell book in the dungeon, light in the dragon's lair.
I was first to the front of the class, thin and pale,
wheezing out we forgyfað urum gyltendum, pardoning
bullies, crushes, even myself, before the sweet soþlice.

I took a puff of my inhaler, and stood for a moment adorned
in this helmet of secret knowledge, its weight upon my head.

Anti-Social

To the woman drying her blue nails
under the over-door air heater
in a suburban London train shelter:

I salute you, unexpected diva, clomping 
down the platform catwalk on the stars 
glued to your clogs, nimble as dynamite,

righteous as tattoo ink drying under skin.
You harden metallic polish under the heater,
prop the shelter door to keep it blowing

driving the acetone deeper into our lives,
smell of corrosion, starvation, dissolving
the plastic coating from our smiles.

10 March 15

The raised yellow warning strip is your
red carpet, the flash from overhead cables
a paparazzo bulb, and so you throw shapes

beside the sign that boasts breakfast:
"Croissant and coffee; tea and toast--
whatever you fancy, two for a pound."

X The motto of your world: in gloss
X we trust--lipstick, vinyl, magazines--
X a dash of glitter on whatever you touch

X including the social contract, it's thou
X shall nots doodled over with skulls
X and roses, looping ironic hearts.

You process to a chorus of one-nostril sniffs,
part an aisle of scuff-conscious shoes,
and when you are gone, finally, I miss you.

Encounter Group

Gather the tribes around the fourth plinth,
generally occupied by a noun not a verb,
but today "encounter" is perched there,
resolving to entertain, that whore for attention,
poking fun at the pigeons, adrift in the square,
fellow seekers, like the men who shovel their guano,
and scrape the awnings with trowels, patient surgeons
excising white cancer, the factory worker scrubbing up
the black scraps of his nail, winking at his wife, 
as the black water drains, foretelling encounter.

19 March 15

Why Do You Wear That Thing?

Headgear of unending quietude,
fuzzy old-fashioned trapper hat--
startling, un-urban, practical--
flaps dangling a lambskin froth.

Are you dating the young librarian?
Or sloshing through puddles en route
to a kill? How do you feel about orange? 
Is that real maple syrup I see?

Do you check your chequered shirt 
for fleas, or oil a single-geared bicycle?
That knob of fur set on your forehead--
Workingman's bindi? Or reminder of "shh"?

You must shoulder irony like a shotgun,
stalk pinball balls in your pal's basement.
In God we trust? In trust fund, trust?
Have you pulled off two people at once?

Greatest of feats was your scatology,
knowing the creature last ate on a Tuesday,
fingering the tracks in shallow red mud,
saying nothing, your hot head aglow.

Delusion, California

Welcome to town. You'll find
it all here: fire station,
convenience, butchery, find
the people willing to help,
the sidewalks unusually clean,
and the longer you stay,
the more it grows on you--
sunlight browning the grass,
sprinklers celebrating
an endless water supply,
housework done for minimum
wage, gas prices going down--
it will be this way from now
until the next now, having
learned to live in the moment 
from your attractive yoga instructor,
learning to worry less about
things you can't really control,
like those episodes, catching
your own eye in the mirror,
and wanting to salute, wanting
others to salute you, to make posters
where just one of your eyes
is as big as an ordinary man,
waking from a dream, and remembering
that you should always follow
your dreams, even when sometimes
people get hurt--remember omelettes,
acceptable losses, friendly fire,
remember greatness thrust upon you,
and thrust yourself out onto Main
street to announce: I am here.
I have arrived. Citizens. Behold.

Bad Burrito

The ideal interview
does not involve staggering
with a trouser leg of crap
polyester denying the stench
as your sphincter ceases
to be properly introduced
by name, title, and rank,
the radiator humming
its desperation tune
wishing you could spark
a joint, wishing the deaf
would hear your pain,
wishing you had mastered
the art of winning friends,
and not lied about composure
being one of your heroic "flaws".

Early Shift

Cafe full of workmen. Cafe full of
workmen drinking tea. Cafe full of
workmen drinking tea in orange vests.
Cafe full of workmen who are deaf.

The are gesturing to one another.
Someone drank too much last night!
Stirring my coffe with a teaspoon, 
I am the loudest person in the room.

They point at the board to order.
The owner knows exactly what they want.
More tea for the men in steel-toed boots
who never look away from one another.

I stare into my crisp cheese omelette.
The cheese omelette looks like the moon.
I eavesdrop from my peripheral vision.
I am the quietest person in the room.

5 May 2015

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

soldier, victim, coffin, wardrobe, hissing, gene, victim, ethereal, tool, sarcophagus, inconsolable, balloon, imperfect, plump, revealing

Mention something absurd or impossible


The man with the kindest face helps you rearrange your wardrobe

It's not his thing. But he holds out an arm for you to drape with coats and hook with hangers. Where does all this stuff come from? He smiles. You excavate the sarcophagus, victim of your own fashion sense. Here is the sleeve where you wiped your nose in the wind. Here is the lipstick-pocked collar you would rather forget. Too plump now for this, too skinny for that. Do you think I could still pull this off? you turn to ask. Only a curtain replies, shrugging in the breeze.

10 May 15

Return Flight

They are loading the kids on the school bus like bullets,
squirrels are swapping out nuts in the pock-marked trees,
from a sky mall position 30,000 feet above Maryland
the trailer parks become blurry, all equal,
to the Georgian estates with well-watered lawns,
like ink on a well-worn bank note, evening out
the share of green, smoothing the complexion
of our slave-owning founders, fashioned in hatching
and tri-cornered haberdashery, folded to an unreal
slant, bulking out the cow-skin billfold nicely,
only certainty in scarcity, the only unsure thing
left is when the next loud pop will signal
the end of the race for someone we are said to
have loved, and this is why squirrels keep on 
squirrelling, and schoolkids comply with
all posted and verbal safety instructions,
and trying too hard to make better goes sour
in the winds that propel you to arrive back home
thirty minutes ahead of schedule, and isn't
the view from here lovely, of hedgerows nobody
can be persuaded to straighten out anytime soon,
because revolution take energy, not just a spin,
and here comes the landing gear, here comes
the loudspeaker, low and tight across your waist,
we're going down, here we go, we're going in.

13 May 15

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

hind, enclosed, condition, tooth, involuntary, unborn, circuit, bathing, fold, friendly, drenched, tore, Ithaca, polar, siding

Also:

Mention something disgusting: rotten eggs

Magnetic Resonance Imaging

Our hindparts enclosed by metal, we slide
into the tube like a tooth in its socket,
stifling the involuntary, becoming unborn,
we complete the circuit like a blow-dryer
while bathing, the waves bouncing and folding
in friendly succession, we are drenched
with inaudible sound, the meniscus we tore
showing bright, ragged edges, mapping Ithaca
at the bottom of a dark lake of space, blue
as though polar, the siding packed turquoise
with fibres of ice, how you long to stand
at the edge of your old sports injury, face
the sleet, paw it from your goggles, look
out from the shore as a particle, and wave.

21 May 15

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

jack, marker, puddle, homage, fathom, assembly, swiftly, librarian, frequent, steal, basil, scant, transparent, sewer, parchment

Also:

Mention two kinds of weather

Dream of the Desert

The good kind of rain, and the rotten kind of rain--
I have felt them both, adding weight to my fringe,
collecting the jacks from their hammers, markers
of wet righteousness, unionising in puddles, paying
homage to the fathomless deep, that greater assembly,
that swiftly fans over its moisture, its muck, our
librarian of wetness, tut-tutting our complaints
while we frequently steal a glance in the forbidden--
a book smelling of basil and sage, scant dust warmed
by its transparent cover, declaring: Tuscany, Extremadura,
lifting us from the sewer that collects in our bootsoles,
lifting us, light as a sheet of parchment, out of the two 
kinds of rain--the known, and the tolerated--out into
the sun-benumbed stratosphere, where all is clear, and bright
and nobody asks you to shake your umbrella or stamp out
your shoes on arrival, for there is no arrival, just a constant
state of temperate present-tenses into which you can sink
and sink deeper, admiring dry wrinkles forming in your skin.

28 May 15

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

bog, butt, livery, aspire, flushed, unit, groom, tarry, ornamental, smack, rattled, tedium, cosmic, tire, curious

America Takes an Interest

The airwaves clot with corruption
charges, bounced like a soccer ball
from head, to knee, to post.
We are the butt of our own jokes,
bogged down to the cleat-tips,
flushed green with grass, striped
like a referee in good dark mud.
The ornamental smack, the rattled
coach, the tedium of missed shots--
the unit working as one, groomed
cosmic, the legend more meaningful
than any one person or game. Cheer
for the team you like best, and pass
the buck while tipping the sports drink,
some sponsors are legal, some offend
the pride of oil-rich nations, this
our new warpath, these: affairs of state,
matters of resounding importance, ringing
like the sound of a hollow stitched ball
as the red card goes up, and the hammer
smacks down. FIFA. Viva. For heaven's 
sake, says your mother, it's just a game.

Credo

Because the act of living is an act of making.

Because the bread of life is warm and salted.

Because the poem is an act of defiance.
Because each morning before work.
Because more than a living.
Because I still am.
Because I can.
Because.

31 May 15

I have been misery's excellent pupil,
studying lice in a labyrinth of follicles,
pressing my wine-stained lips to the revolver,
lashed to the mast, giving way on the street
I am pain's most excellent physician,
an orchestra of wailing, the funeral attendant
benumbed by his creator, hobbling on one bad ankle
in an effort to make the recital, the grade,
I wash my face three times before sleeping, I spin
in place before crossing a doorway, I rake the soil
for insects before I dare set foot on dry land.

Double Talk

I expect nothing            I am nothing
less than complete          less than wholly
fidelity to our cause       disillusioned


1 June 15

First Book

Because someone else glued the pages together,
wrapped them in a cover that wasn't hand-drawn,
"This one is a real book", scolds my mother
not meant to be coloured in or cut apart.

The first thing we do with new media is make porn.
The first thing we do with new energy is weaponise.
The Germanic tribe of Vandals lives on by reputation
pressing chewing gum into the pages of library books.

Holding my first book of poetry in both hands,
I want to detect a warmth, like bread from the oven.
The name on the cover is almost the one I scratched 
on my sister's doll-house, flipping the "b": "Rodert".

I have bottled the water from the stream, now I thirst.
I have wrapped and unwrapped the parcel many times.
Rodert the Vandal has made his mark, and so what?
Something will come of something, but who can say?

The tree of true originality blossoms outside my window.
Each flower different, each bearing different fruit.
My wife suggests I open the window for a bit of fresh air.
I sneeze at the pollen and shiver at the over-moist draft.

I have moistened each draft with oil from my unsteady fingers.
I have smudged the ink and ground deep the red crayon wax. 
What I could not understand, I tore into ever-smaller pieces,
and pulped them in my mouth, like a publisher eating his words.


2 June 15

Getting On With It

The weather forecast is a yellow frowny-face,
the radio static coalesces a rhythm,
the kettle, when poured, gives only steam.
With the snap of a switch, the walls change colour--
blink, and the trees defoliate; sneeze, and lose an ear;
ask how I ended up naked in this dumpster, and I'll 
reply that things were just going that way.
Forget the slides, let's push each other on mood swings.
You be teeter, and I'll totter you up into space.
Forget the weather, let's mist-bathe and moon-bathe,
becoming more transparent with each exposure.
Let's film our lives and play the reel backwards.
Let's put on a happy song, and pretend to dance.
The economic forecast is a stick-man curled foetal.
I haven't stepped on a crack in twenty years.

4 June 15

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

Collective

A chastity of hawthorn,
a banishment of dandelions,
a wince of stinging nettle.

Adam named the beasts,
but the tribes of wildflower
name themselves:

An omen of buttercups,
a chandelier of horse chestnut,

You say "Grove of beeches."
They say, "Still below,

Who we are, who we
are not--edged in black earth
and mist-white air.

A concubine of ivy,
smouldering of rose tree,
kaleidoscope of fern.

5 June 15

Once, twice, three times
I will say this only once.
Hello, Mr. Squirrel.
Hello, cheeky bugger.

Unbiased Review

Begin to cut into the sternum
with slow, gentle strokes, like
petting a cat. Another incision
below the ribcage, and peel back.

Do not let the still-beating heart
alarm you. And do not let it slip
from the body cavity, though it
writhes like an unconsolable child.

Pinch the diaphragm with tweezers,
pull down and watch the lungs expand.
Now apply a tiny voltage to the hips,
see how the muscles kick the legs.

Replace the glass, the cotton ball,
and count how long to stop the heart.
Discard the mangled carcass in this box.
Now wash your hands. Take out your books.

8 June 15

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

din, nipple, restore, eerie, swiss, supper, pregnancy, bewildered, heavenly, recognition, ferry, preferred, prodigal, pallor, darwin

Also:

Refer to several different beverages

Ambien. Coke.

Swiss super-pregnancy. Twelve months off.
Paid work. Unpaid vacation. Come, buy, live.

If you only want to shop at Wal-Mart, drink Starbucks coffee, and buy books on Amazon, keep using lowest prices as your sole buying criterion.

The low-price leader heads the parade

as girls with plastic buckets flank him,
tossing free samples into the crowd:
Billy gets new ultra-slim comfort-fit
tampons, and Susan is the proud owner
of Gopher-Be-Gone Killz-Um Pest Poison.
The band behind isn't exactly marching,
but simultaneously playing tunes 
for each demographic comprising today's 
eager crowd: old-time for the old-timers, 
electronica for the tween overtones, 
nursery rhymes for the little ones, 
clinically proven to lull children

9 Jun 15

and pets into a sense of security. Speaking 
of which, here comes the homeland team
and don't they look great in sponsored
apparel--the brand-name ones to beat.
Well, send in the clowns, in their tiny 
clown-car, so fuel efficient it's hilarious.
Now here come's this year's pageant queen,
and Bob, do I have this right, the voting
happened a little differently this year?
That's right, Chuck, instead of one person,
this year our dear townspeople elected
individual body parts from different girls,
which have been reassembled atop this float,
and just look at that wave, that smile--
two of our most popular items this season.

14 June 15

18 June 15

No-Man's Land Common

A shadow passes over the meadow, effortless
in its cooling presence, a wake
of songbirds, for a moment stilled,
for a moment passed over
by a presence like night, a shoal of fish
beneath the barnacled hull,
tender in covering, blanket-soft,
the lids pulled over
our welling eyes, to shed a drop
in the pool of soft grasses,
which ripple, concentric,
in an unseen wind that blows
all things, together, onward, all things,
eventually into crossing,
into parting, into the covering-over 
of life with--not death, exactly--
but the other side, the other life
in which cloud, meadow, fish, ship
reveal their true names to us--
flashes-through-sunlight, dark
moisture, ink of relentless progression.
                A brush dipped
in clear water, the pigment's smoke,
a cipher of leaves in the swirled cup.
The Hawthorn renounces her wedding vows.
Slow raptors finger the dryness of heat.
Nameless, in the new world, a congregation
of petals, root, trunk, and branches,
new leaves, in the unnamed world,
hold out their yellow hands to the rain.
                A voice cries out
in a language you recognise, and the cloud--
for that is what it is, just a cloud,
retreats in spinal curvature over the hill,
which is grass, then soil, then stone,
a foetus in the centre, its open hand
a gesture of greeting, of saying "goodbye"--
and now you are on your knees, in a field,
jet-lagged, on a Wednesday, remembering
your name, a gift from your mother,
as the multiplication tables arrange 
themselves before you, pieces for chess,
a calendar full of meetings in which
you can never say: for a moment, I was 
that shadow, say, listen, I have been
to the other side of life, and a child
rests in the womb of the earth,
but instead stare-down at your ink-stained
hands, and nod, and arrange your broken
face into the gesture of listening.
                Blackbird splashes
paint on the wall...

19 June 15

                Blackbird splashes
paint on the wall, a test of faith
its piezoelectric charm, and overtone,
the low hum of a foraging bear,
of diesel truck, trembling to stay still,
bright young man in the methadone clinic,
radial whirr of tyres losing their grip.
                How fast can you go
down the hill, your legs becoming springs,
becoming spring itself, arms winged out,
dodging the pollen-dander flak, you are
young, they say, and may it ever be so,
shuddering to a halt in the long gully,
a shadow passes over, dipping low--
how could you recognise your future
self without a name, a form, it is
cold for a moment in the dark before
thought, and the white grass trembles.

21 June 15
                You wake up. Thursday.
In England, not that house, but the other
one, and you are married, and you are
employed, and today is a workday. It is
early. You flex the fingers of your dominant
hand. You are lying on it. You are lying on
your right-hand side, and the pillow is damp
around the right-hand corner of your mouth.
You roll out of bed onto your left leg.
The shock of it travels to the hip-socket,
left shoulder, the spine reverberates
in sympathy. You wake up. You are you.

22 June 15

The day after Father's Day,
cold brewing like a summer storm.
The body tends its losses.

The push-mower whirr, a sweaty pastoral,
as blades of steel whizz blades of grass
with deft urgency into the storm-brewed air,
a pitcher of sun tea suffusing clear water,
the nameless grass seeds soaked and reborn,
I prop the mower awkwardly against the shed,
hold out my hand to the lead-heavy drops,
mop my brow, take a sip, and bow inside.

25 June 15

               ...You wake up. You are you.
                Two egg cups greet the morning
tapping and slicing, you lay waste the shell,
meant to be broken from within, meant to be
the emergence of life from protective slumber,
the first waking, coated in the soil of blood--
a leaf periscopes, a small beak probes, shakes
off the dirt of its making, and you who have
forgotten, find it now underneath your nails
and curl it out with a dull knife, scrub it
out with a brush, mould the soft clay of your
belly over the first scar of your making.

29 June 15

"These fragments I have shored against my ruins"
-T.S. Eliot, "The Wasteland"

                What is poverty? I wonder,
in the Chateau du Bois, in the gardens
of Boboli, of Babylon, hanging this thought
over the old cliches of love-and-be-loved
familial relations like a dangling sword
set to cleave the stone of our hard-life-
making, set to blow the dust of philosophy
back in my overfed face. Are we born free?
Or is the meniscus still and set skin-tight
just out of view, and aren't we happiest
letting slack the knots at our wrists,
since struggling only tightens the bond?
Aren't we happy? Aren't we ourselves?
Becoming more like our ancestors every day.

30 June 15

                The newborn next door,
shielded from view, from the sun, blue eyes
in the process of taking colour, blue as water
before the ink curls in. Next door, the still
contentment of garden tasks. Next door, as if.
The other side of the fence. Just out of view.

3 July 15

Lingua Franca

Mine, I break on purpose. Yours,
you are learning to join up.

Both lead to consequences--
the bad reviews, sideways looks.

Where am I from? Where are you
from? Implication: not here. Not
anywhere like here.

This place, our home.
We drew a line on a map, and you,
at the time, fell outside it.

The teams have been chosen, I'm sorry.
The teams have already been chosen.

To be picked last in the first world
is still to be picked, as fruit
from its tree, we comb

the surface of language, sprinkle
the field with shibboleths, raze
bridges, blockade back-roads.

Each new word replaces an old one.
Even your mother says you sound strange,

these days, distant, and drawing further
away, the gap between tongue
and mouth-roof widening,

spitting, a bit, through your teeth.

8 July 15

                The taste of blackened sunlight,
the ash-taste of morning, stabbing the drapes.
We rise, assemble, tuck in, fall out, lie flatly
through our teeth about how much we love it here.

I am the spike at the top of the acorn,
splinter in smooth wood, punchline's sting.
That salty taste in the mouth, red spot
on your napkin, sliver of under-nail ice,
that's me--

10 July 15

That summer you were alone
with your thoughts, which is to say
you were never alone.

Nuage. Vapours. The Narwhal.
"Nothing could have been worse 
for the development of my mind" (Darwin)

Birdwatching. Beetles. Suffering
pheasant, shot the day before,
delivered a coup de grace.

I jot the words "mad genius",
try striking out one or the other
word, for they are--just words.

I must have them all, even the invisible
species that slip though the net.

The balance of your checkbook is disturbed.

The endless list-making, the smudges
of ink, darkening the corners
of the mouth.

Touched with the fire of rejection,
consuming an armament of facts.

The grey of death in their skin.
Dante addressing Beatrice.

16 July

The Essence of Instinct
    for Charles Darwin

That summer you were alone
with your thoughts, which is to say
you were never alone.

Nuage. Vapours. The Narwhal.
Collecting iridescent bugs
in your barely-visible net.

Cataloguing, by sputtering candle
the endless lists, ink darkening
the corner of your mouth.

Your armament of facts
was nothing much to her, as she
tested your reflexes with a pin.

Birdwatching. Beetles. Pheasant
blood-ruffled, shot-riddled,
a black stone's coup de grâce.

Once there was no pattern,
the crochet unpicked by needles, 
coloured threads, broken limbs.

Pricked, you bleed like a prism, 
dividing light from light
through the aperture of pain.

All at once, the peacock
opens his eyes, and the threads
pull tight, stitching you in.

roll dipped in black coffee

26 July 15

the smoke of our cigarettes

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

traveler, aisle, serpent, crew, singles, unimaginable, gut, plump, ether, permanence, fray, chopped, solstice, mumbling, itch

Bread roll dipped in black coffee
sweet and bitter, bitter and sweet.

Flames in the Sky
    i.m. Pierre Clostermann

Fast traveller, through an aisle of smoke,
serpent inching the blue,
crew of singles in the race
to discard your aces,
spilling their guts in the ether.
Chopped air, and frayed permanence,
mumbling away the days off into
bread rolls dipped in black coffee--
itch for the yoke, long dark solstice
followed by flak and flares,
St. Pauls a glassed-in Faberge egg.
We lit the air like a cigarette
after sex, dragged our smoke,
scarring the belly of the sky.
It sounds better in French, the one
about the airman on a victory fly-by
who crashed into his buddies,
the punchline coming in threes,
in sheared metal and slag eruption,
peeling back the shattered goggles
from the sockets of their eyes.
How many kills can you be sure of?
Dabbing another enamel swastika
onto your long Spitfire's nose.
You count as one, air sergeant,
wing commander, god of unholy air.
You count. You counted. You scorched
the terrible air, and strafed the land,
belly-down in a two-winged fuel tank,
dropping, under pressure, going--
the last direction left--down.

28 July 15

 The taste of blackened sunlight,
the ash-taste of morning, stabbing the drapes.
We rise, assemble, tuck in, fall out, lie flatly
through our teeth about how much we love it here.

I am the spike at the top of the acorn,
splinter in smooth wood, punchline's sting.
That salty taste in the mouth, red spot
on your napkin, sliver of under-nail ice,
that's me--

            I am waiting, pincers aloft,
and abdomen throbbing, mandibles poised
on each side of my articulated face.
Step on me, on a crack, step up, fall in,
and be singed by the translucent barb
as it plunges through willing skin.

We have been lied to, boys. We have been
has-beens strung along like a toy caterpillar,
marching in millipede time to the sound
of our own amplified heartbeats in the echo
chamber of belief.

                        Stand tall. Taller.
Rise up, my ocean of balaclava-clad faces,
sea of gas-mask respirations, kicking the gravel
for large-enough stones, linking arms and chanting
along to the bannered slogans, casting a spell
against the mirror writing on ricochet shields, 
batons proud as in the old traditions, here comes
the turtle of our undoing.

                            This, the machinery,
of a broken-down world--how it whirs like a kitten,
pawing the air with its gears. It has no fears,
except the wooden clog, the misplaced spanner

30 July 15

Here is the glossolalia of our delight, our sudden
acquaintance, frothing to tell all she has seen.
Sit down, my dear, and take tea, take laudanum,
take sodium pentathol with cream, and one lump or two?
The bricolage of the self, a race against entropy,
race to place the final tiles before the glue dries.

Come spanner, come hammer, come wrench, come saw.
Comme ci? That will do, mon pardieu. We will brick
ourselves in from the inside, from here, from afar,
coming close, the night-visions of our other selves,
come ratchet, come knife-jack, come tromboli spear.

The beauty of nothing. The beautiful nothing-much.
How we whimper at its absence, stroke the mantel
for dust. This is a must. This is madness, this.
The tang, the gong, the smell of must. We rust.

like a cataract.

Ice House

We wanted it in squares, like building blocks,
wanted water hardened like cement, stacked
in rectangular abstraction, angling to get it right.

Fire was first citizen of the modern world, 
licking the undersides of shields and cauldrons,
conjuring a nameless awe in red-orange hues.

But who would carry water in a hay cart?
Who would chill the glut of meat from rot?
Flatten the curvaceous stream like a Picasso?

Leave the door propped when you enter the ice hut.
Wear you heaviest garments, even in spring.
It is winter here, always, and we have made it so.

With ladder and hoist, we build our white ramparts,
chiseling out the moisture from the stone.
Brick by brick, we have mastered every element.

The clink in your tumbler of gin came at a cost.
The joist rope frayed, the pick once slipped,
the heft of frozen water is the fist of a god.

No sound can pass through the sandstone walls,
least the drip of daylight through the door,
scalding ruby flecks in the snow-packed floor.

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

Our Foreign Correspondent's Missing Notebook

We spat outright the rancid gristle.
We ran outside, spanning the thistle.
We bloat the tide, and then we whistle.
We glut our side with gleaming missiles.

We hoist our gin to lonely lasses.
We joist our grins with mumbled masses.
We gain our goal on fumbled grasses.
We make our mark, them make our passes.

Who knows who knocks on our door so late?
Who's come unhinged and latched our gate?
Who's called a pal, a thief, a mate?
Who's knacked our knockers, swung our fate?

You can't contain the bunk you see.
You can't un-gunk the junket's spree.
You take your sight in hex, a bee.
You vex your funk, then get set free.

You swing your moods, the rhythm swung.
You ball up beetles, roll their dung.
You can't be hanged if you're well hung.
You mumble words that should be sung.

What tides will come to knock us flat?
What flattened knife will slice the fat?
What words you swallowed, which you spat?
What expression matches your best hat?

I wear my elbows thin, chin-dreaming.
I skim the skin from whatever's steaming.
I whisper myself hoarse with screaming.
I poke the gristle's sizzle, gleaming.

I tip my hat, and adjust my faces.
I grin, grow thin, unpick my graces.
I swing cat toys within small spaces.
I have covered over graves and bases.

14 August 15

Where is the song that will sing us whole?
Where is the shot 

16 August 15

Where is the song that will sing us whole?
Where is the thimble to shield our soul?
Where can a shot un-taken make goal?
Where am I now, and what is my role?

They dot the skies with dirty traces.
They streak the mud across our faces.
They sing, they shoot, they empty spaces.
They wear our shoes without the laces.

They came to liberate the square.
They left our bathtub full of hairs.
They build us up, and make us scared.
They put the fun in funhouse fairs.

When will the silence end in this cell?
When will the radio repeal the spell?
When will the offal lose its awful smell?
When does victory get declared in hell?

He paces down the drowning streets.
He meets his mates, salutes their feet.
He gluts at home on rationed meats.
He whistles, fumbles, pats his seat.

He sniffs the air for whizzing bullets.
He trusts his brain, but follows his gullet.
He touches his gun, then taps his wallet.
He preaches from an armoured pulpit.

Why ...

She ...
She ...
How ...

17 August 15

South London Motorcycles

An "L" plate doubles for a riot shield, license to revv
Pas to pass, and weave, and wheelie if you can.
When did we ever do anything except to impress a girl?

The smell of tyre on tarmac, burning, the smell of kerosene,
and two strokes, three ticks, a kick to the clutch
to smooth out the space between here and wherever.

It's not the ride that matters, the blur of people and property,
edging into spaces made by the parting of the two.
In this economy, two wheels is plenty. Four is pushing your luck.

18 August 15


He paces down the drowning streets.
He meets his mates, salutes their feet.
He gluts at home on rationed meats.
He whistles, fumbles, pats his seat.

He sniffs the air for whizzing bullets.
He trusts his brain, but follows his gullet.
He touches his gun, then taps his wallet.
He preaches from an armoured pulpit.

Why is the air so arid and calm?
Why do we arm our soldiers with psalms?
Why do dates drop dead from the palms?
Why did we drop the un-droppable bomb?

She breezes in on a breath of air.
She wears a badge and a bun of hair.
She blindfolds justice to keep it fair.
She wonders how much one should care.

She is at ease in hermetic airports.
She wears two band like strips of asphalt.
She remembers random codes for passwords.
She stole a glance, then took my passport.

How do you take your beverage of state?
How would you describe our mortality rate?
How do they shut those invisible gates?
How did it get so terribly late?


Mixed Blessing Bakery

21 August 15

Mixed Blessings Bakery

On the one hand, flour.
On the other hand, salt.
Applaud the way language
is malleable, idiom dough
re-shaped into myriad
postre, patisserie, 
roti, pitha, naan.

Heat the ovens before dawn.
Remove your jewellery.
Massage the dough with fingers,
your fingers with dough,
squeeze home-longing
into the rising sponge,
and punch, and punch down.

Give thanks to the grey world,
the grey people, hardly baked,
who come for a morning loaf,
and chuckle at the sign outside,
offer them a crossed bun of hope,
offer a scones worth of regret,
crumbing in waxed paper.
