Hemel Hempstead
    (The Magic Roundabout)

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

frantic, legless, crisp, rate, tallow, acquired, peaceful, canary, don, ham, sind, cinnamon, deny, tawny, atmosphere

Also:

Mention something absurd or impossible

Into the frantic motion, we go swirling,
crisp packet whizzing across the dash,
we are legless with concentration, heart
rate throbbing upwards, fumbling the gears.
Do we signal? Or would that give us away?
Finding peace in the stomped-down clutch,
the reprieve of engine, coasting centripetal
like a pinball into our well-flipped slot.
There must be some magic in the method, some
method in the magical madness, some mad magic
that keeps cars flowing in loop-the-loop,
as byway signs direct us: face your fears.
We plot our course, and give it all we've got.
Mirror, signal, manoeuvre like we've been taught.

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

error, rubber, role, irony, intercourse, accompanying, antique, assure, imitation, accustomed, shameless, silken, judge, victory, naive

Also:

Mention one or more extinct animals

Stevenage

Mirror, signal, manoeuvre like we've been taught,
coasting off the Roman road at junction seven.
The first new town shall be the last new town,
in plus-ca-change repurposing, Dickens regarding
the shopping strip as "wide for its height, silent 
for its size, and drowsy in the dullest degree."
Even the Aurochs takes an ironic stance, brought
from the hills to browse the well-meant cycle-paths.
Nimble in our N.I.M.B.Y.-ness, we shutter up each bungalow,
rubber out the pencil lines of future planning--
history will be our shameless judge, for a man's castle
is his waterproof, and all that his wife has bought.
Drive the lanes in drizzle, the street a silken gown,
give way, merge in, and follow the roadway down.

Watford

Give way, merge in, and follow the roadway down,
for north of here--or was it the gap?--is a wilderness
of pure vowels and swallowed consonants, a people
well-suited to work in damp conditions, meaning sweat.
But this place is built with London brick, Thames clay,
respirated by green lungs, circulated by the brown
Grand Union, dotted with theatres and strafed by tracks.
One could grow up in worse places, smaller spaces,
trying your luck at the local broker, placing a bet.
We threw a dart, and landed just inside the M-25.
Let others toss cabers and spears, everything here
is going south, at an angle, lapping up the overspill
from the blitz-wracked capital, we formed a tribal band,
blowing north in a wind flecked with sharpened sand.

St. Albans

Blowing north in a wind flecked with sharpened sand,
news travels of the raised forts at Verulamium,
the peasants in mob at the bishop's double gate,
the whipped ice cream cart at the far end of the pond,
where swans reenact their pas-de-deux, while Cromwell
bed down like Saddam amid the the musk of ale casks.
The market stalls stretch out at an alarming rate,
while a busker carts her speakers to the a song
of buy-my-lovely-apples and fresh-fresh-fish-today,
and the mews go mewling and the bread goes mealy,
prams fitted with plastic windscreens, and busses
wipe their long, flat faces, smoking out the back.
The gutters gurgle down the stalls' effluent streams--
all may not be near as pleasant as it seems.

Welwyn Garden City
    (Sir Ebenezer Howard)

All may not be near as pleasant as it seems,
but who can fault the deep breath in, on coming down
the escalator, through the self-opening doors, to daylight
on the tight-clipped hedges, fountains popping champagne.
You've made it. You should celebrate. Have this voucher
for free tea and cake, upstairs in the department store.
This second-draft of ideal living, trying like marriage
number two, to blend and mend the sharper bits of family
into something called "community", which starts on the green
and ends in a scale model of, "a full measure of social life
but not larger", proportion being everything in taste-
testing each free sample--both on the way up and down
the ladder you struggle to step onto, the queue that's rife
with those, like you, who want to put the quality in life.

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

flee, compose, shovel, parsley, frightful, seaward, dissolving, asphalt, arch, hoof, wail, trojan, bucket, pillow, pan

Also:

Mention one or more extinct animals


Cheshunt

With those, like you, who want to put the quality in life,
timing is essential, which means trains, seats, and carriages,
means football on Saturday and no more than two pints,




river lea -- from Luton to the Thames
playgrounds
bridges
duck pond
lock gate
red double-deckers up from charing cross

old build, new build, pebble dot-dot-dash

The grass, the gas, the interests of the muddled middle-class.


Heading away from London, along the Cambridge Road

===

Harpenden

With those, like you, who want to put the quality in life,
timing is essential, which means trains, seats, and carriages,
testing the lot for parking spots, taking a wide platform stance.
This is the line to cheapside, line for the fleet of street
and smarts to edge up on an artery direct to greener pastures,
better schools, tougher headmasters--here you can live, from ten
to five, and halfway on the weekends, far enough from the click
of the digital ticker tape, the million-pound clack of keys.
Strum the harp of happiness, an arpeggio up the strings
the note just above you always leads to sweeter things,
now snap the laptop shut and slip the briefcase in your bag,
you've made it, in so many ways, even if the hour is late.
The platform advert promises a better tax discount:
you invest in missed bedtimes, so make each penny count.

Hatfield

You invest in missed bedtimes, so make each penny count
of the stuff you scored to turn this rave into a sense orgy,
kicking out the Corgis from the grounds of Hatfield House.
You want to dance like there has never been a matriarch
above you, like you still can't trust the ground beneath
your feet won't end up strafed, or shelled by roundheads,
like the hangars aren't hanging empty now for nothing,
and Spitfire is what your friends call you with vodka
and a match. You want to dance away your sensible choice,
the yellow brick near hilltop, shops, and university
that serves for a cloister cell. You want to feel again,
in lights that flash in heaven, flicker like hell.
Lost in nosebleed beats spun by the DJ from Berlin,
you think, with any luck, you may never sleep again.

Wheathampstead

You think, with any luck, you may never sleep again,
from the squeal of lorries down this tiny lane, jacking
in for a shop delivery, clogging the vein and clotting
the zebra crossing, starting up the honking, and the rain.
There have always been blockades, as far back as people,
Catuvellauni mounding Devil's Dyke, mill-makers shunting
the fast-moving Lea, remote-control bollards for posh
private drives, and cars lining both sides of Brewhouse hill.
But, patience--what is a homestead without obstruction,
what is a settlement if you can not sit still? And so,
the pincer-point remains, just past the disused tracks,
halted also by decree--first the monasteries, then the trains,
silence itself is slowed, and stalled, and finally shut down.
But then, so much pastoral quiet may be the ruin of a town.


Whitwell

But then, so much pastoral quiet may be the ruin of a town,
so gather up the horses, hams, and clowns at Christmastime,
thread them through a well-worn story, weft with crooked jokes
and cock-eyed bits, get the kids all wound up before bedtime.
Be a dame, and do this one thing for me: be the backside
of a dressed-up cow, for better a kick in the rump than a pie
in the face, since this year's costume cleaning budget
is the round egg of a golden goose. Make the gags as crisp
and pungent as the bite of cress on a youngsters tongue--
come one, bring all, from the towers of the estate to the white
bottoms of the long-sunk wells, trickle in behind the velvet
curtain, babbling like the Mimram on her stones, come alone
and leave in arms, to spring a hole where the water once sept--
dig down, dig down, to where the core of things is kept.


Champneys Health Spa
    (near Tring)

Dig down, dig down, to where the core of things is kept--
under the white robes of the self-pampering flocks,
under the hot stones from the aromatherapeutic massage,
dig into the soul of the estate's bereaved bourgeoisie.
Who buys a place like this and leaves it empty?
Marble, wood, and tarnished copper hoisted to the sky,
a century's tended hedges in a replica Versaille.
Here is the place you planned for retreat, to conspire
with a daughter-in-law in the drawing room, while the men,
minus one--your beloved--retire, leaving you in black lace
with some company, and a snuff-box's worth of dignity.
But your eldest son chose to live in a modern secular style.
While your youngest decided one day to snuff your gift of life
Under layers covered by time, some terrible secrets lie.




Charles Rothschild (http://www.geni.com/people/Charles-Rothschild/6000000002764356315) suicide 1923

a house intended as the residence of a widow, typically one near the main house on her late husband's estate.

Another house related to Tring Park, though never a main family seat, was Champneys, in Wigginton, which served as the dower house for the main estate.  This sizeable house had originally been built in 1874 in a French Second Empire style by Arthur Sutton Valpy, before being bought by Walter Rothschild's parents in 1902.  However, Walter's mother, Emma Louise von Rothschild (b.1844 – d.1935), realising that her son wouldn't marry, sold the house in 1925 to Stanley Lief, who created his first 'Nature Cure Resort', forming a brand which is still well known for its spas today.


Berkhampstead

Under layers covered by time, some terrible secrets lie—
where motte meets bailey, on an arrow-strewn field,
the river brought the raiders, the milkmaid heard their boots.
Only a ring of wall stands now, canal boats lap nearby.
Here is a homestead someone wanted to hold, keep of last
retreat, place they would have to take a stand--and not
against hikes in council tax, or a nearby rubbish plant,
but here they put a stake, and said, "We stay", and meant it,
for whatever came, and come-what-may, while now the teens
can't wait to flee downstream, to wider waters, longer paths,
the walls of baileys reaching to the sky, in Shard and Gherkin,
to work in glass houses and drive to work, never driven
away, but settled, and solid, hiding a fact they never could prove--
we liked it here, it's all we knew, and knew that we could love.

1 September 15

Hertford

We liked it here, it's all we knew, and knew that we could love,
where the gold vein of the Beane meets the arterial Lea,
two rivers commingle at the weir, dancing down the steps
like Fred and Ginger, their shared hearts beating in time.
A heron watches over, stilted in the shallows, and over
him a hart in bronze, branched antlers holding back the sky,
on a plinth of Portland stone in which is etched the names
of those who died in a war to end all wars, and under that,
another war, and under that the dais on which we stand
in Parliament Square, far from the sound of water, or shells,
a long time since the first white stag, crowned with a tangled
crucifix, showed us how to ford the stream to another life.
Sometimes the simplest acts can ease the living's burden.
Sometimes the best escape is to start all over again.


Ashridge

Sometimes the best escape is to start all over again,
a primal being in a primal space, just you and your whippet,
good shoes, and decent rain gear, setting out into autumn
fire--the bronze, for miles, the tunnelled flames, as though
you are being passed into, and through, the incinerator's heart.
Father chose cremation, not interment, to be lifted into air 
as ash, like dry leaves giving dust under foot, no further
down than humus, peat, some part of you is breathing in
the him you want to know, agog with golden, crimson,
burning out the sadness from your bones, and then--be still,
a herd of of several, dozens, scores of deer, appearing
more the more you look, and each one deeply watching you.
You meander back to camp renewed, winding all this in,
spinning toward the beginning, hoping to make amends.


Knebworth House

Spinning toward the beginning, hoping to make amends,
the youngest aims to share a see-saw with his frenemy,
while the mid-sized boys mock the dinosaur statues
with bark armour and big sticks. This is how the estate
survives, in distant Gothic splendour, hiring out
and putting in the water park and woozy slides, hosting
weddings, holding jousts and japes and half-price family days.
We are a family of two, come to see the library, come
to walk the perfect gardens and stalk the sculpted alcoves
of one man's eccentric past. We grow ourselves eccentric,
outside the plan of tuition fees, inheritance tax,
meeting the neighbour's children with a friendly glance.
A school group cuts our path, whooping boys hurling, 
so into the frantic motion, we go swirling.

Minor Delays

Relieved, and at ease, the strain taken
by the train, we are regulars, unmoving,
stalled extravagantly just before
the station platform, haunted
by the notion of forward movement,
confirming our suspicion of a life
interrupted by the slash of petty
budgets, the waste of the waistline
and the slap of back-in-your-box
imperialist interests taking us down
a peg, so we'll be lighter, we'll
be British, one fox in the acres
of another man's estate, red tail
flagging the roadside in death,
let this be a lesson, a warning,
don't try to pull the emergency
stop--it is connected to a buzzer
that gives a mild electric shock.

10 Sept 15

To The Black Dog's Master

O come o come holy hellfire night
    where the tabletops glow incandescent

And you return with a child's promise
    shaking a handful of dust in the wind.

You have copied my key and smashed the lock,
    invited yourself to my business meetings.

Dark saint, bright dagger, merciful inquisitor--
    not all questions have been answered.

Your flights unlisted, visits unannounced,
    trap door bulging just under the carpet.

Come rain of glass, breath of ice, fire
    in the blood of blood-shot eyes.

Please do go on ahead with your happy lives.
    I'll catch up. I'm just behind.

On the phone, a tic, and then it's your voice,
    at the keyboard, and click, your name.

Your phone lists all of the ones we lost,
    but you can't let us see their number.

Company policy prohibits further contact.
    I can only confirm name, date, and occupation.

I can only confirm, yes, he was once your son.
    Sorry, I cannot disclose whether he loved you.

Let's just take five minutes for a breath of air.
    I'll be fine, Mr. Chairman, just tired.

I'll be fine, in my chair, in my bed, when you
    come, flashing scalpel, grinning teeth.

    (ten reasons for ten years)

7.  Because you had choices (not choices).
8.  Because you look best in photos when you smile.
9.  Because beech-leaf orange rages the valley unchecked.
10. Because who can remember his name anymore?

____, Twinned With ____.

We read the names at the city's edge: Neu-Isenburg, 
Ingelheim am Rhein, Marignane. Countless schoolboys smirk
at earthy St. Albans twinned with a place called "Worms"?

But who can pronounce these sister words, vowels topped 
with umlauts and pointed caps, Cs trailing scarves, cute
and grave strokes keeping their round heads warm.

Do they laugh at our names too, Vatford und Schtevenage,
'atfield-uh? Riddled with voiced consonants, diphthongs,
local and irregular--a field of unexploded shibboleths--

each sign a tiny border crossing, each twinning a filament,
webbing up a continent with silk to smooth and fill the gaps,
a gesture, like raising two hands, palms out, above the head.

Two fingers can mean "peace", two fingers can mean "victory",
two fingers (in some countries) can be rude. Thumbs up is universal, 
thumbs down is too (as all Christian martyrs know).

Here is your strange-sounding brother. Here is your sister
who eats insects, pets, and rarely bathes--take her 
to meet the older boys, the ones you want to impress.

You come from the same European womb. Perhaps not monozygotic,
and slightly metric-skeptic, stamping your own ruler's heads
on the circulated disks gods flip to decide our luck.

We read the names and wonder who has it worse. We read
the papers for clues, swirl ultra-heat-treated bovine 
baby food into tea sweepings, just the way we like it.

Do they have Eton Mess in Beaune? Do they make Bakewell
tarts in Alzey? Best fish and chips on the Costa del Sol?
Nobody asked if I wanted a sibling. Return it to hospital.

All Those Forgotten Memories
    for Lorena Krause

Timbre of a water-drop,
opals in a brooch,
the lips care to smile,
but the eyes must frown,
nose gently tilted
in a slow inward breath,
chin curled to stifle
a tremor, cheeks angled 
back in open reverie,
high forehead yielding--
like the sea floor
to its beds of kelp,
like pine trees
in a woodland blaze--
call it diablo's lace,
an oil-slick labyrinth,
smoke from the shisha,
spray on the prow,
all that is behind her,
blown back in that hair.

guttering the candles with breath.

We go, we have gone, gone are we,
and well gone for the recompense.

I like the idea of nothing. The round-
ness of the zero, containing space.

The only appeals now are urgent appeals.
A child, with a name, needing help.

You can't buy class. Can't buy fame.

Taste your own blood. You'll like it.

24 Sept 15

Self Released
    (after L.K.)

I search for you in the eaves
of churches, the tangle 
of disused nests. You took
the golden colour from the sun,
the song from my throat, you
flew between my thin fingers
into the star-specked dawn.
All day I waited. That night,
I braided the length of my 
hair into a home for you, slept
with the heft of its emptiness.

What's left to enjoy? The dust in the back of the throat,
lichen overgrowing the magician's wink.
I think of explosions. Wanting to be one.
I am the bomb itself.

Kerosene lamps. 

Captain's Log

The kerosene lamps are lit not with a spill
but a tongue of flame, and Ah! how white
the nets become, taking on St. Elmos' blessing.

We are at sea, and so the light sways below deck
as the wheel in spin unsettles the rudder, aiming
to name our next of kin when it stops, and stares.

The crow's nest buffets against the fog-laced black
of sky, and our hero, long since lashed to his mate
and keen to overcome the apocalypse, stands proud.

Below, the grenadier weighs out rations of powder,
choking on sulphur and pulverising the charred coal,
his eyes wet with the smoke-before-smoke of promise.

Will we get through this night? Only if the lamps
hold fast to their rent in the darkness, casting
starlight as nets over the shoals in our wake.

23 Oct 15

Intermediate Sword Swallowing
    After David Clarke

Look down, from this dizzying height
on those who cough at a feathery
trout rib, sputter on pepper, gag 
at the smell of decomposing flesh.

You know why the transsexual behind the till 
is saving up for chondrolaryngoplasty,
set to excise that final bite of apple 
that Adam himself just couldn't keep down.

The lips never get tired of blowing vodka
into a match, nor the teeth of tucking in
to the duvet, ball gag, or minty chewing gum.
The throat, however, burns a different longing.

Relax. You've come this far, and only a bit
more left to go. Who ever said living
life to the hilt was going to be easy?
Now extricate like an awkward party.

Your pinups are of throat muscles,
training them to be smooth and slack,
reassuring the touchy uvula, like a poet
passed over by a cold steel prize.

It's not that you won't keep making mistakes,
nick a tonsil, bruise the windpipe. Spit
blood into the sink, repeating your motto:
To scrape is human. To suppress, divine.

29 Oct 15

Occupation: infant

Revised poems per Paul's suggestions

Poem: Two Fingers

Two fingers can mean "peace", two fingers can mean "victory",
two fingers (in some countries) can be rude. Thumbs up is universal, 
thumbs down is too (as all Christian martyrs know).

One pair rests on "d" and "f", the other on "j" and "k".
They are just the right amount of fingers to poke both eyes,
unless Moe makes a blade of his hand at the bridge of his nose.

Two are probably enough fingerprints to convict a suspect.
With two you can pull back a bowstring, squeeze the arrow notch, 
curl first knuckles into a cradle, and silently let go.

Table for how many? The perfect photo-bomb pose. One finger
over the mouth can silence, two can seal the lips for good.
Two inverted fingers walk their way across my thigh.

Cross them, and you're hoping for luck. One the one hand,
live long; on the other--prosper. Two fingers pre-renaissance
for benediction. Two in mudras, baseball signals, sign language.

Part and un-part them to ask "Where have the scissors gone?"
How many fingers does it take to pose with a cigarette?

# # #

Two Fingers

Two fingerprints are enough to convict a suspect.
One pair rests on "d" and "f", the other on "j" and "k".

With two you can notch an arrow, curl first knuckles 
into a cradle, tighten the string and--quietly--let go.

One finger or two for rudeness? Thumbs up is universal, 
thumbs down is too (as all Christian martyrs know).

Table for how many? The perfect photo-bomb pose. One finger
over the mouth can silence, two can seal the lips for good.

Cross them for luck. First, live live long and then, prosper.
Mudras, benedictions, secret hand shakes require this pair.

Part and un-part them, asking "Where have the scissors gone?"
To pose behind a cigarette, this couple has it under control.

They are just the right amount of fingers to poke the eyes,
unless Moe makes a blade of his hand at the bridge of his nose.

Two fingers can mean "peace", two fingers can mean "victory",
two inverted fingers walk their way across my thigh.

Cuirassier

The Other Party
    for David Atenborough

Slant the match and drag it into flame,
a moth, a magpie, loves the bright.

Crack the icing, slice the toppling cake,
an insect's spongy chatter along the blade.

The clink of dishes in the draining rack,
an ice cap groaning under polar-bear strain.

There are sounds, and sounds preceding them:
leaf-scuttle, rain-pock, wind-sand crescendo.

Birthday candles spitting under breath,
tortoise shell splashing into spume.

5 Nov. 15

The man with the kindest face points out that your shoe is untied

Like Adam, reaching for the finger of God, he is casual, discrete. No fuss, just an adjustment to the spaghetti of laces now bounding and lashing your ankles. You could have tripped. Broken not only a neck but your stride, your pride in the grace of perambulations, here among your peers, who peer into you, seeing all, you could be "klutz" for the foreseeable future, nomenclature of imperfection, flecking the sidewalk with your own red blood. You wave in thanks, but catch only a gust of cold spring air.

12 Nov 15

The Vessel
    "Only a Woman, divine, could know all that a woman can suffer."
    -Willa Cather

She will not look away. Apparition,
egg cup, spectre. The eyes look out,
but the gaze is inward, deep-set
in the kohl of a thousand sleepless
dreams. Ask her. What the jewelled
and looping sigils, the vines of life,
sunflower presiding an incubator's
warmth--ask what it means to be her,
to hold the promise of a wind-chipped
star, the seamless matte of calcified
longing. Breathe into the snow globe,
and shake the frozen air. No questions
left, only the puffed cheeks of breath
held against the stillness of water
freezing. Listen. A tap. A crack.



Young Boudicca

She has made her hair redolent with the muck
of hoof-ground hay, the sweet manure and salt-
licked crust, hewn a braided mane of rope.

Others snap gum and selfies, twist and sniff
their locks, dreaming of shampoo commercials,
tossing back a cataract of shining water.

She craves a helmet of fur, two chestnuts
for eyes, skimming in furtive panorama across
the frost-sprayed horizon, soft nostrils

huffing the wind for data, a mane, a blade,
a rudder twisting in the storm she has become,
dragging the terrified clouds behind her.

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

The Walrus
    after Lewis Carroll

I have seen the head of a king 
roll faster than a cabbage, scraped
sealing wax from a boater shoe,
given wings to a pig on a dare.

There will always be a walrus
among you, sorting the biggest
oysters into his hat and weeping,
sifting the shells with his tusks.

I like the way they slide between 
my whiskers and down the throat;
born to be eaten, my evidence:
the half-shell resembles a spoon.

Bring them to the far low rocks
on shoes without any feet--
you shall lament the untidy sand,
while I admire a sunlit night.

Yes, we shall talk of many things,
and walk upright in pleasant clothes,
and gather youngsters beside us lifting 
their spirits beyond the briny beach.

Let them rest, if they are out of breath,
and practice their finest speech.
I am the walrus. Inventor of patience.
I know cabbages. I know kings.

7 Dec 15

Lily

Up-do outdone never, she wears
a calla beehive, stamen buns,
her stare beyond the tubular stems
as moisture wicks her incandescent
head dress, centrepiece, ikebana,
bunched for sympathy, resurrection--
"I love your curls," say the girls
at the sock hop, back-combing petals.
She nods a cloud of pollen, drops
thirsty split ends into the sink,
re-traces her eyebrow pencil, shoos
away an unsuitable suitor bee.

10 Dec 15

Griefquake

11 Dec 15

Bringing up Hades at a Bacchanal



How to End a Business Meeting

After drinks, the phones come out,
in that stage of ritual bonding
where we share something personal,
but still socially acceptable.

"That's my eldest", says Blue Tie,
passing him around like the bread.
We are smiling now, a good sign
that the deal will close soon.

Two stone heavier, I look paternal--
my eyes are puffy with insomnia,
my hair is thinning like patience.
"Any pictures of your children?"

The pious virgin in me has sworn always
to tell the truth--recalling the single faded
photo, interred ten years in my wallet--
the last picture we took of our son.

His eyes are also puffy, his hair is fluff.
He has not just scored a goal or graduated.
The death certificate read, "Occupation: infant."
Here I am, in my own occupation.

I can see that I have been quiet too long,
their cheeks are starting to relax, eyebrows
form a second question mark. Saved by the plural,
present tense, I blurt, "We don't have children."



12 Dec 15

Animal Pyjamas with Painted-On Whiskers

Now we come to the part in the pantomime
where the smallest children file onstage,
the village hall pumping the dance number
they practiced since September, and still
don't really know, when the audience starts
clapping along to the music, because most
are related to one or another, a mum, gran,
uncle or aunt, and though they don't say
the L-word much, picking them up at school
or tucking them in at night, this is their
way of saying: yes the lights are blinding,
the jokes over even our heads, but we are
here in the dark, loving the way you miss
the cue, hands up when everyone's hands
are down, wanting to absorb like a black
sponge every drop of trepidation, so there
is only the thrill of being totally seen,
alight, in makeshift costume, clapping
to say: give us everything, all you've got.

17 Dec 15

God's Eye

I trace an eye on shower-fogged glass,
walk past twenty-seven CCTV cameras
each day to work. My father is making
the beginner design in the macrame book,
looping yarn over a makeshift cross.
What he began, she will bring forth;
what she completes, he will protect.

If the eye of God is truly single,
He has no depth perception, sees us
as cartoons on a flat world, hitting
each other over the head with hammers,
exploding dynamite tied to our tails.

Perhaps he is eating a bowl of his
favourite cereal, rubbing his single
rainbow-coloured eye with a knuckle,
waiting for a parent to wake up.

Bird Boy

We are winged for the asking, in comedy,
in serious play, flapping our featherless
elbows and wrists above the waterline.

Birds dive, and swim the air, lifting
our eyes to the milky horizon, a skein
draws its line over stippled hues.

We behold, and feel our bones go hollow.
We see ourselves in the warped reflection,
poised to glide, to swoop, to wheel an arc.

We will dance on the docks, taunting the water.
Let others bathe, and preen, and gather seed.
We will pray for wings with two hands apart.

"But did he ever jump?" the bookkeepers ask.
The water keeps no record after the splash.
The sky, too, has emptied out its pockets.

Be here with me, Bird Boy posed for takeoff.
Smoulder with ignition, ready to jump.
Burn your ashen shapes against the dusk.

4 Jan 16

Hangnail

You are the pinch that pierces the veil,
    improvisation on anguish, a mild delight
in immodest weather, speeding your message
    of compromised veins, and severed corpuscles
up the doll-like sleeve of mother time
    you bleed, you catch, you dispatch shocks
like a small white louse, biting into my thumb,
    fellow feeder on discontent, you bend
and waggle hair-like in the wind, in the end
    you will undo my faith in faithfulness,
and with a snip of gleaming clipper, be done
    but not forgotten, bruising the air
with your wry, and wicked remarks.

12 January 16

Then one day the pain becomes too much.

Too much to bear, with its long nails

and breath of salmon.

The pleasure of orgasm simply this: I stop
being my loathsome self for several seconds.

Just Shy of the Pinnacle
    "Second place is the first loser"
        -bumper sticker on American Ford pickup truck
        
Being an uncle is second place
Being a minor poet is second place
Coming first is second place (in sex)
Life outside London is second place
Marrying an invalid is second place
Dropping out of engineering is second place
Getting fat, and going bald, are second place
Cat instead of dog is second place
Getting B grades in Spanish es el segundo lugar
Working part-time is second place
State-run school is second place
Being known, but not loved, is second place
Killing yourself is last place
Not killing yourself is second place
Second place is second place
Your potential is first place, you lazy fuck
Step on the necks of weaker men
If first place stumbles, you will be there
Take turns in second gear, in second place,
ready to spin around and face the crash,
for weathering the crash, I second
the motion to carry you away, fat uncle,
minor poet, dropout outsider, all in favour?
Say "second", and stay here in your place.

The Waiter Has the Same Name as Our Dead Son

So, then the gears cease, get stuck or are fused
into a new likeness of your face, the stunned
Norwegian next to me at dinner, watching my mouth
open and close without inviting conversation,
without the acceptable losses of fifteen sunlit
days preceding the long, backwards winter drag,
the oblique designs and obsolete patterns I thought,
--ha, ha--that I was over this by now, the stammer
of sound belief that punishes my limbs, my jaw, 
the power of speech, for power it is, locked 
up in the clamour of jailhouse keys, my mouth 
gone hollow, round and dark as Plato's cave.

Afterwar(d)s

EC: On a soft January morning, we left the Land of Futility, 
we boarded the plane with no wings, both of us

RP: in our seal skin jackets, as the fuselage hummed
patriotically, and we laced our gloved hands for takeoff

EC: holding on to each other's oxygen,  as if one was 
more than two, as if lost pride could ever return

RP: the clear air to our lungs, tarred with the smoke
of progress, our eyes watering with modernisation,

EC: our ears crackling against the enemy steel, 
sharpening the colorless particles of success like spears,

RP: we became, for a moment, weightless as a missile,
lifted by unseen fingers, into the flak-stained air.

# # #

Bright ocean
    for Hokusai

Curl is limb of a mother,
drawing you bosom-ward, glint
of a scythe,
                cutting the cord,
paring knife crackling through melon,
icicle's taper, bubble's glow.

O bright stampede, come trample-quick.

The neck bends low in humility,
            the scimitar hesitates.

To wipe out is to be made clean,
on a tabula rasa
                    of foam and fiberglass,
a rag doll
            in a tumble washer,
the crest of translucent demolition
advancing,
            the afterthought of spray.

# # #

Dark woods

Into the wooden tangle I go, nostrils filled with peat,
among the glowing mushrooms and opaque woven vines,
seeking shelter under tree cover, with moss to pad my feet--

no guide but upshot limbs, branching skyward for a sign,
no path but onward, deeper in, to lose myself in clay
and covered leaf-litter, the jagged vein-spread lines

and hilly contours, raked like furrowed brows in grey.
She appeared, a disappearance, erased into the shade
of a rotting sycamore, white flame among the dark decay,

a horse, a ghost, alone amidst the grove's slow cavalcade,
a mark of unmarked beauty, a star trampling the dust,
she came to me, and I bent low, and we were unafraid.

Let woodland tangle, lichen glare, rotting into must,
I lower my eyes to the blaze of pure white rust.

Dos Voces

It sounds like your voice,
which is why you believe it--
telling you to accept
the acceptable losses, 
befriend friendly fire,
weaving, all the while,
bars around the emptiness,
telling you to put away
soft childish pencils,
those little word-games,
until one day you forget
the feel of air under wing.

It sounds like a voice
you used to know, chirping
in agitation, pouring
the antidote into one ear,
calling you into the terrified
wind, unpicking your excuses,
pecking out the sensible
braids and tidy topknots,
as the storm gathers heft,
and the air seems untenable,
you sing yourself awake,
and each feather trembles.

9 Feb 16

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

momentum, escapes, buzz, yawn, contract, sails, grind, magnified, ein, tickle, vi, bounds, retire, wir, livid

Also:

Include the names of three colours

Masters' Palette

Crimson is for luck. Cobalt is for commiseration.
King's Yellow for better days gone by. The painting
arranges itself, if you let it, from burnt offerings

to the eyes and the deep-felt percussion of a distant
time, the drums of spite, the low thump of boots
crossing the bridge into your village. Paint the village

as it was, and let the echoes of shelling ring down
the cobbled streets like rainwater overspilling
the guttered eaves of the neighbours' rooftops.

Momentum escapes the brush on the downward stroke,
tapering the cloudscape to a tidy yawn, breaking
the contract between earth and sky, blurring up

sails to horizon point, grinding the textured muslin
into the fore and tickling the topmost slick
of varnish into a boundless, over-bright offering,

a cup overspilling its vivid pouring of light.
Cerulean promises the breathable sky. Bright ochre
taints our darkness. Bone black for the ashes of love.

What Will Survive Us

Bronze statues of men on horses.
Small pieces of tarmac, glittering like shells.
Skeletal towers, disconnected.
Concrete hives with glassless windows.
Yellow foam congregating in eddies.
White bridges overtaken by gulls.

12 Feb 16

Spring Clinging

Damned fool that I am, eulogising winter,
as though the yellow spearheads
forming at branch-tip
had it in for me,
as though chlorophyll
were chloroform,
a knockout blow.

Someone said, "psychotic break",
and I heard it as a kind 
of rest--a pause
in the relentless pace,
a rent in the coarse
fabric we made
and called it "real".

Perhaps this is winter--
the heart's arrhythmic
murmur, a break-beat
lull, the shush of sap 
coasting awhile,
things left to go
the way they do.

I will not trample Narcissus,
primping in the frozen
birdbath, don't worry,
I am assured,
in each new meeting
of flat-capped man with dog,
that spring will come.

So I moan like the wind,
flick sleet from my fingers,
burying the icicle
between my ribs,
so that even the tulips,
those Dutch prostitutes,
can't melt it.

I will carry you, winter,
on my back through the desert,
tumbleweed congregating
at my ankles like the cat
just after a bath,
recalling the clean glint of ice
in a chokehold of dust.

Let the engine turn over,
in bird-splash, bettle-clack,
tuneful hive-hum.
I am ready for the spotlight--
dressed in coal buttons,
carrot for nose, snowballs 
weeping behind my eyes.

13 Feb 16

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

apt, persistent, heath, skyscraper, surf, plumb, spoiled, heft, cock, pan, remote, amazing, flour, shakespeare, steadfast

Also:

List three unusual habits

Homo Digestivus

Oh look! It's rain again.
How delicious for the plants.
Delicious for me also
to not have to go outside.
I will lie here, smelling
of myself, the duvet
smelling like me,
lie in my own crumbs
digesting last night's
biscuits.
            Now it has stopped,
but I know it will come again,
the craving for biscuits,
and I will expose my waxy
white legs, the curlicue
hairs trembling as I breeze
down the hall in my nudity,
trying to outpace my heating
bill by telling myself: "Brisk,
refreshing" as though I like
how desperate I have become
for a snack.
            The rain is back!
And I am back, with packet, in bed
wondering who the first man was
to get out of bed, if he was lonely
or hungry, or tired of the weather,
and whether he took comfort
in his body being familiar,
wanted five more minutes,
scratched unthinkingly,
heard voices in the gurgle
of the drain.

Sound Advice

"Stay in shape" says society, and I wonder
what shape that should be, or who "society" 
is for that matter, rolling myself up

into an unobtrusive ball, elbows in,
a pliable form, even pitiable, pious,
the original me, curled tip to toe.

But then I want to be a triangle, composed
of three as all good jokes, brief lists,
and whatever else makes three here.

A square? I've been called it! Like the tape
holding together the bridge of my specs,
the pocket protector that doubles for a badge.

The Pentagon takes all matters of security
seriously, so no joking with the man
feeling down your leg with an electric paddle.

Bees put a hex on everything, then they're gone
on to the next pollen-friendly bar. Oh, bees!
Come back, we need you for our witches' brew.

You see how amenable I am? I ask society.
Or I would, if I knew whom to ring, and now
we're back to the circle, the jingle, the bell.

Time's up! Pencils down. This poem is over
without having gone very far beyond light 
conflation of social commentary and geometry.

Then again, what do you expect? Should I just
roll over and be the poet you hoped for?
Be smart. Think safe. Eat fibre. Stay in shape.

# # #

A current of air, a crumb of dust. 
A bright rectangle of thought,
cast down from the double glazing.

By staring into space, we occupy,
casting our eye-beams over the hedges,
trimming untidy branches in our mind.

I must remember to put out the milk.
Two bags of dry leaves from the shop.
Peel the tulips over boiling water.

What are you making? A soup for the
unwise, a liquor of meal, a gravy
in which to drown your napkin.

We metabolise. We burn. We ache.
A farmer came into my dream,
smelling of horses and soil.

# # #

O! H2O

It has gone wet again, not the weather but my life.
I awoke in damp bedsheets, stayed long in the shower.
I put on the kettle, still dripping. Outside, moss was 
growing everywhere that moss could. I admired persistence, 

then "click" and I poured my tea. The steam encircled 
my wrist like silver bangles. I was crying at this point, 
but never mind. The wind picked up, spattering the window 
with an invisible Jackson Pollock. "Nothing good will happen

today", I mused, swirling the leaves in my cup, sloshing
onto my already-sodden slipper, then scratching behind
my ear, making my wet finger wetter. I reached for a cube
of sugar. It melted on contact, while the dishwasher

scrubbed furiously, huffing in discontent. I sat down,
and ruined the couch. I stood back up too quickly,
bashed a lamp that shot a blue finger in my direction,
like God condemning Adam to rise up from the clay,

and still be clay, but also more than clay, a vessel
of holy breath, visibly white on frost-coated mornings,
yet part-baked and therefore permeable, as I am now,
as are my contact lenses, to the unrelenting damp.

Tottenham

1 March 16

Waking Up to the Last Winter on Earth

We tear at our clothes in short burst of feeling.
    The wounds, the visible wounds. 

The last of the junipers have pummelled the ground.
    The dark ground, the rotting ground.

No mystery to the guttered eaves singing.
    The old songs, the rain songs.

Here come planets, a speck of dust in the eye.
    The sharp pain, the dull pain.

Nothing left for it but two white pills.
    The remedy, the cause.

A burnt switch of reed is waving us on.
    The last goodbye, the first goodbye.

The spokes of the bicycle becoming invisible.
    Their one trick, their best trick.

No mention of the war on wistful thinking.
    The lies, their truthiness.

The dead struggle to get comfortable.
    The shifting, and shifting.

We have become what our ancestors feared.
    The power, the curse.

The bees are dropping from the windowsill.
    The silence. The hum.

13 Mar 16

The dough is always the same. But these days, they shape it into Peppa Pig, dyed pink, or a blue Smurf, and sell it for twenty centavos more than the same amount made round.

Pan Dulce

Blue Smurfs contend with pink Peppa Pigs
for the warmest spot in the fluorescent
display cabinet. Ask, and the tongs
shall deliver, into a white wax paper bag,
and for twenty centavos more, crystals
of black coffee dissolved into steaming milk,
washing down the same dough used for every shape,
the same shapes used for every season,
the same season year-round as the market sundial
paces off each twelve-hour day in tidy arcs
that drove the Spaniards out of their gourds
and into the streets, swords drawn and muzzles
primed, to paint the tiles redder than red.
Purple Cookie Monsters crumb beneath green
Kermit the unlicensed trademark Frogs,
strategically placed eye-height to a toddler.

14 Mar 16

Daughter of Odysseus

To what have you hitched? A rent
in the fog, laying your anchor upward--
or are you being reeled in?
                            The boat
knows its course, always homeward,
seeking the plaited eaves,
the bunched sills, the inward glow
of lamps set in your hair.
                            Be still
and let the propellor swizzle its way
through the gathering loam,
the fizz of mist, its spindrift
scrubbing your cheeks 
                        like tears.
You have come a long way,
under the ink-dark sky, now quilted
with home-longing. Yet the sextant
points where the rudder must follow,
and loose curls sprung free
from the rooftop thatch
                        whisper, "Adieu."

15 March 16

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

site, whim, scattering, skip, water's, gong, extravagant, dey, zebra, urban, sharpened, startle, reply, driveway, tiresome

Also:

Include one or more types of tree

Sharpen the sticks of the sky
against the homely smell, the tell
of mockingbirds twitching out tunes
on the banjos of their beaks.

The gong scatters its sound extravagantly.
The urban myth, the sharpened sand.

Let the zebra startle the fingerprint-maker
let the tiresome whims of old men find rest

Jacob

He is weeping, in daylight, in Central Park
when the evangelist wraps his arms around him,
trembling with an electrical spirit, convulsing

like a Sun-dancer in the ecstasy of torment,
like a penitente hanging by pierced hands,
a hermit flailing the sin from his opened skin.

They are open, like the spiked horse chestnuts
fallen in awe of the first hard frost,
bursting on impact, thorned hearts exposed.

People push their noble children along the path,
play chess and basketball, abide the regulations,
measured in the art of ignoring these men.

But these two have fused, as brothers in Christ,
the bearded man swaying in the world of his arms,
the young man's tie taking on the smell of piss.

I do not know what was said, or will be said,
but the more I look, the more I cannot see
who is doing the holding, and who is being held.

there also. Raw steak from mincing Russian Turks.

That I may slurp off his tonsure of foam.

22 March 16

Standard Evening
    West Hampstead Tube Station
    22 March 2016

The warbler twirls
    a fizz of sound
the pops of which
    contend and blur,
forget which beat
    is rising, which
descends
    amidst the rust-
caked beams
    and bones of rebar,
slats clotted
    with creosote,
brickwork crumbing
    like stale bread.
It is that evening--
    Free. A paper
full of pain,
    the stains of
grease-fried chips,
    a skylark
dips, warbler
    goes unnoticed
this un-special
    evening, hardly
to remark upon,
    bleeding into
the others--
        standard.
"Evening."
    Stand. Hard.
Evening: standard.

Lullaby

All night, the mystery--
of how to return to myself before dawn,
all night,
            warm sheets,
cold moon,
            untethered to my name,
rank or occupation,
        I float
above the pain-wracked globe,
lacerated by
                ribbons of cloud.
Here is the scar
                I was telling you--
Here, the wept eye
        of Odysseus,
                    knowing home
has been dismembered.
    Enter the labyrinth,
one person
            come out, come out
wherever you are,
                the bedclothes shift
the cat's new nightmare.
    Who is she clawing
so tenderly?
            Every mother,
a substitute teacher,
    every child,
half-alone, half-on-holiday,
drifting out the window
    from maths.
A reprieve from being myself,
    each night, 
                the ink stains
crawl my arm,
        each night,
    the body's upheaval,
                    pesant revolt
against the tonsures
    of mind, 
            each    night
a new beginning
    to the story    Scheherezade
keeps weeping, keeps weaving,
    keeps teaching herself,
to spare her life.

28 March 16

Howl for Straight Guys

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

launch, horseshoe, glossy, scum, muzzle, windy, dwell, shock, unreal, quote, cabin, gnostic, weigh, distinct, agamemnon

Also:

Mention something absurd or impossible

Genealogy File

The Victorian era was a parallel universe
launching horseshoes, a whiz of glossy scum
cradling the muzzle of an indistinct mare,
the windy grain of spirits who dwell
between the shock of a blinking shutter,
unreal, the quote on cardstock-back,
"Taken at cabin, 1821" tells us little
of the gnostic weight of hard-set jawline,
the only distinct reality, that of an early
grave, and proud as Agamemnon, the folded
arms of a patriarch, the emaciated waist
of a woman kept like bees, how the lace
swarms her well-covered ankles, our
ancestors girded for the fireball flash.

Dogcentric by Lorena

29 March 16

Dogology

Pooch, pup, plaything, pal,
you wind the lead
around my ankles, curl
your nails into my skin,
and slobber all I own,
flop-eared bandit
wet-muzzled cuddler,
in never-ending fascination
with a beetle or a running
tap, you lap my insides
out in hairball huffs,
your tufts and twirled
topiary send me sideways,
giggling in fits to start
the band up, playing our tune,
O canine moon, let's howl
together awhile and greet
the morning with a lick.

The companionable darkness
folds us in,
             the withered limb
of another wasted morning waves
us in to day
            and the crooked way

            on the mattress

life, the price

worn in as sheet-marks on our face
and bleary eyes,
                 the friendly lies
the rotting food
                 we can't consume
in this economy

Graft

The sap's epoxy sealing
hardwood to mended pine,
blossom stirring
just beneath the bark.

The tattered leaves
roiling their green tongues.

The pulp-flecked resin
coursing, steady-slow.

Restore the limb,
let the alien part in.

Hard work. Hardwood.

Weld the mouth-wound
shut against complaining.

Patience, the virtue
of parentage
seeping to extremities.

Mend. Begin. Synthesise.
New fruit from old sugar.

Paired initials
enclosed by a heart
rise to repair the scar.

Lean in to fibre stitch--

Adopt. Adapt. Begin.


11 April 16

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

garments, lure, poised, firmament, cradle, crowned, aura, george, silt, bundle, interest, fabric, cuff, searing, glossy

Also:

Mention two kinds of weather

The two kinds of island weather: 
wet and warm, or cold and dry.

I'm just going off the cuff here.
Just going on gut. Just gutted.
Just the gutless wonder bread
sandwich you always wanted.

Let me just get this off my chest.
Vent the spleen. Rial the bile.

Should I liver should I die now?
The dogs can have this world gone mad.

Crowned in the cradle, poised on the throne.
The sine-qua-non of je-ne-sais-quoi.
Something will come of nothing. But nothing
will come from contemplating the void.

Stuff it down. The syrupy treat.
Peer through a thousand cataracts.

# # #

Interview

I'm just going off the cufflink here.
I am the sine-qua-non of je-ne-sais-quoi.

I have eaten brown bread my whole life
and never felt healthier. Listen, my heart

beats the national anthem, in both kinds 
of weather: wet and warm, cold but dry.

My spleen is always half full, and my bladder
half empty. Should I liver, should I die?

I peer through a thousand cataracts to reach you.
I just go on my gut, and am gutted, routinely.

My body of work outpaces my fluttering hands.
Manus apiary. Opus ostrich. Flighty, hollow-boned.

I have wrapped myself in newspaper to console me,
for the headlines give off sadistic heat.

Tell me this is all on the record. Tell me again.

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

thicket, lute, blush, upstream, legible, scented, potential, pious, brilliance, crumpled, harrow, auburn, scope, pedal, variation

Credo

I believe in a blush in the thicket
the gurgle-lute upstream
the potentially legible scratchings,
visibile in mud, invisible in dust,
I believe in the pious scent of sap
harrowing bark to a sheen,
the crumpled auburn leaf
pedalling through variations,
light from light, one substance,
the scoop of a burnt-out oak.

I believe in the rain, the mist,
and the drizzle, glazing
the white tops of mushrooms,
which are buried and which rise
from the suffering of wood-rot,
for their mycelium has no end.
I acknowledge autumn
for the remission of chlorophyll,
and seek out the yellow blemish
of buds unfurling the world to come.

A Headstone for Tony Hawk

How high can you ollie 
in heaven? Does your kick-
flip pass through the tail,
smack the graffiti'd cement?
You can skate the parabola
of an uncluttered mind,
grind the horizon's rim,
four wheels gripping a whim
to catch the air, your hair
held back from beads of sweat
the living mistake for raindrops--
you have become the opalescence
of oil-slick, rainbow of gum
under park benches, sunset
on the windows of brutalist flats,
the glint of spilt soda fizzing,
distant throb of a street party,
you have become the free in freestyle,
the brokenness in break-beat,
your best trick was to disappear,
leave the lip of the ramp,
and not look back.

26 April 16

Try to write a poem that will last longer than a plastic bottle.

A throat of scales, blades
of leaf--chlorophyll caught
in the veins, and the bones
of the neck press together,
stretching up serpentine.

Vine of living, fasten close
to the hairs of the nape,
curling your callow tendrils
around an empty column
of chilled spring air.

Nothing destroys me like your scent.
No sense can be spoken, black iris,
dark lily, my petal of purple leather
purpling the air with your dust,
your must, eyes flashing green.

# # #

5 May

Selfie Smile

The first act of aggression is to bare the teeth.
Then the backdrop swills into view, in blues and greens.
Who wore this better? Now remember not to breathe,

or shift your chafing sandals, overstretch your jeans.
Smile through the pain, and call it pleasure, leisure,
declare yourself vacation's victor, holiday queen

keeping your friends at arm's length for good measure,
Machiavelli, now pull your phone in closer for a kiss,
the twist is not to show your own displeasure

with the weather, waiters, back-alley smell of piss,
but remember fans back home, in homely circumstances
fluorescent-lit, half-dying for a stroke of sunlit bliss

now capture what you came for, and give the lens a chance,
flash wide-eyed defiance, the loneliest romance.

Young men in suits sleeping on trains

16 May 16

Agorophobia

"What I thought was a wall turned out to be a tunnel"
-Tony Hoagland

This may just be the cold medication talking,
but really--this could be huge if true:

What's the point of a life that only makes sense
having lived it? Seen from above, the skyline

becomes a block puzzle for children to solve.
The moment we stumble is the time we are given wings.

The key is shaped by the lockpicker's worn-out fingers.
Desperately seeking: Minotaur, Algernon.

No amount of praise will reach the condemned man's ears.
High on the scaffolding, the only leap is homeward.

We spin the cube, worry the iron, leap peg over peg.
Still the puzzles retain their solid defiance.

Try to remember there is no spoon. A wall is just
hardened space; more emptiness than molecules.

Where better to cast our shadows into dance?
A cave is just a space surrounded by rock.

A rock is just the opposite of cave.
The charm around your neck, a constant reminder.

Nothing stumbles faster than a man with untidy laces.
You see? Constraint is not so bad. A cozy tucking in.

What would you do with all those open vistas?

your must, eyes flashing green.

A throat of scales, blades
of leaf--chlorophyll caught
in the throat, and the bones
of the neck press together,
stretching serpentine.

You came to me with blood in the mouth,

Memoria

No sense can be spoken, black iris,
dark lily, petal of purple leather
purpling the air with your dust,
your must, eyes flashing green.

A throat of scales, blades of leaf--
chlorophyll caught in the throat, 
and the bones of the neck press 
together, stretching serpentine.

You came to me with blood on the mouth,
blue pollen over your eyelids, hair
fastened with honey, breath smelling
of mud, and the first signs of rain.

You came for the flowers in the garden,
and left your print on everything in me.

Agoraphobia

The spout of a teapot is the barrel of a gun.
The skyline, a block puzzle for children to solve.

A wall is made of more emptiness than molecules.
A rock is just the opposite of a cave.

We spin the cube, worry the iron, leap peg over peg.
Still the puzzle retains its wooden defiance.

The charm around your neck, a constant reminder.
What would you do with all that open space?

A locksmith is just a lock-pick with morality.
Constraint is not so bad. A cosy tucking in.

Huge if true: life only makes sense having lived it.
Desperately seeking: Minotaur, Algernon, exit.

vision, forest, proxy, panic, osculation, disaster, dress, temerity, library, bird, strength, blossom, attribute, board, butter


Poems for Computers I [3.2]

My vision in the forest? It was proxy
to the burning matter at hand,
which panicked the driver,
his hand osculating the curves
a hare's breadth shy of disaster,
gloves smouldering, and the dress
on the backseat shredded
by the claws our much-loved Temerity,
who once saw a bird in the library
and fainted from the strength
of that new neural blossom,
hence her attributes, hence her name.
I was on board, but not on the board, 
like a slab of butter melting.
I had a vision. En route. It was awful.

25 May 16

Poems for Computers II [4.6]

A toast, to the appetites of the body--
a boulder in the eye
here comes the piper in his festive garb,
the fete of a town,
its grim salutation to the mountainside
let us trot out
the stagecoach, the proxy demands of banditry,
in hope, bacteria,
in friction, the round and glowing breast,
a game of risk
a grisly reminder our aptitude exceeds
our doughy flesh.

# # #

Cyclone

Waterspout

A waterspout is a columnar vortex forming over water that is, in its most common form, a non-supercell tornado over water that is connected to a cumuliform cloud. While it is often weaker than most of its land counterparts, stronger versions spawned by mesocyclones do occur.

Steam devil

A gentle vortex over calm water or wet land made visible by rising water vapour.

Tornado

A tornado is a violently rotating column of air that is in contact with both the surface of the earth and a cumulonimbus cloud or, in rare cases, the base of a cumulus cloud. Also referred to as twisters, a collequial term in America, or cyclones, although the word cyclone is used in meteorology, in a wider sense, to name any closed low-pressure circulation.

Mesocyclone

A mesocyclone is a vortex of air within a convective storm. It is air that rises and rotates around a vertical axis, usually in the same direction as low pressure systems in a given hemisphere. They are most often cyclonic, that is, associated with a localized low-pressure region within a severe thunderstorm.

Dust devil

A dust devil is a strong, well-formed, and relatively long-lived whirlwind, ranging from small (half a metre wide and a few metres tall) to large (more than 10 metres wide and more than 1000 metres tall). The primary vertical motion is upward. Dust devils are usually harmless, but can on rare occasions grow large enough to pose a threat to both people and property.

Fire whirl

A fire whirl – also colloquially known as a fire devil, fire tornado, firenado, or fire twister – is a whirlwind induced by a fire and often made up of flame or ash.


# # #


I. Waterspout

My son, it has been ten years, and whenever
the wind sprays into my face, I taste the salt 
of your absence. Hear me, above the engine roar
spinning your mother and me through the arc of space

between one place, and another that's equally flat
as the low drone of a world swept up in constant war,
and you, my child, are whisked across the dark ocean
in the arms of its survivors, in a homemade raft,

alighting on pebbles you thought would be sand,
with rocks in your mouth for the uniformed guards
(I almost wrote--uninformed guards--) to the gates
of Jannah. It begins, a taste in the back of the throat, 

that rises into a pilar of cloud to follow by day,
across the waters, troubled, and scattered with ash.


II. Steam devil

I see you in the exhalations of a steam train
wheezing through the local zoo, the whistle
whips my nephew's round face to face me, alight
with the joy of being startled, 
                                and still safe.

The Tasmanian devil is far more corporeal,
except in cartoon form, spinning like a top.
At this age, I would spin free of my senses,
crashing into furniture, 
                          collapsing in a heap.

The wind is always spinning, all around us.
A puff of kettle steam confirms it. The devil's
greatest virtue is his patience, waiting
unseen, unheard, in the warm, 
                              moist air.

The brakes gasp, setting us down where we began,
stepping through the air to the cold, 
                                       wet ground.


III. Tornado

Dorothy was no stranger to violence in the Midwest.
Her dog, who meant "everything", cleared "Indians"
from their ancestral lands, with a bark and a Federal
grant. They gave each a copper penny and a bowl of dust.

Her blood is in my blood. It boils 
                                    in midnight sweats.

defeat, liver, estranged, destiny, parent, crane, cart, terrified, gasp, pathos, neat, ferry, artifact, miami, uniform

the parent of loneliness is sleep withheld. 
                                             The damp
rises up from below the floor. The endless spiralling.
The broken pacts. Brace yourself 
                                 in the basement.

Perfect girl in a field of poppies. 
                                    Popping 
                                            perfection pills.
We garland you with kisses, encircle the innocents,
as the scythe goes spinning on 
                                outside your window,
as the dark tilling begins 
                            in the green-sparked air.

First, I was a witch, 
                      then a twitch 
                                    in the knowing tail.
The barometer is shaking, like an old woman's hands.

IV. Mesocyclone

Since the last place you'd 
                            dream of landing is here,
in the silt-choked afterlife of someone's grief,
we have become the vapour 
                          we feared would take us
down the rabbit's hole these long dark years.

The sky is bright with promise, 
                                but we have fallen
into the trap of believing the lies of the sun--for
far too long we dreamt of falling, 
                                   and never awoke.
Far too long, we dreamt of falling, and never awoke

into the trap of believing 
                            the lies of the sun, for
the sky is bright with promise, but we have fallen
down the rabbit's hole 
                        these long dark years--
we have become the vapour we feared would take us

into the silt-choked afterlife 
                                of someone's grief,
since the last place you'd dream 
                                 of landing
                                            is here.

                    and into the silted track, the abandoned
field, the rolling lanes of dune--you greet us just above
the wavering horizon, 
                        clawing marks into the fine beige sand.

Help the cracked and sun-washed ground

reach up to the bleached sky 
                            with a spun gold hand

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

your caffeinated gestures, 
                            a wild dance with much to prove

let the bottled genius pelt us with sand, 
                                            for how it glitters,
let the demons contend with dervishes, lifting their hands
like birds, 
             their skirts encircling a spark transmitter,

signal gap between the gathering clouds 
                                        and ionised land,
here is the beguiling wine that makes all other drinking bitter,
arcing encrypted messages 
                            across the oblivious pineal gland.

Take us to the high mountain, 
                                to survey our new inheritance,
our mouths fill up with honey, the sweetest 
                                            form of arrogance.

# # #

VI. Fire whirl

Come fractal, dog lick, crumpled gold foil, come respiration
of the embers, snowfall of ash on mountains and manicured lawns,
a corkscrew splits the line along the hills' ragged dentition

and we are smothered in the valley by a mud-red dawn,

30 May

 


smothered alive in our sofas by the news anchor's careful diction,
bleeping the n-word: napalm, bleeping the name of a son long gone,

into ash, into sea. Do not ask for more than this small cup,
be happy in your sadness. Spin upright, little top, the fire's song
a full-grown fable meant to stir / whir / whiz / cheer you up.

The Swedish candle chimes are pacing out their heat-praised gongs,
the vintage carousel, the blazing manes and tails, descend, rise up,
and your heart must be handled carefully, so as not to slip the tongs.

Ablaze and spinning on a wounded sphere pricked with old starlight,
with only a pillar of fire to follow through the vacuum of night.

1 Jun 16

Fibrillation in the Troposphere

Sacred heart, have you caught a thorn?
Scared heart, a sharpened thought?
Scarred heart, are the sutures weeping?

You bruise the clouds to violet on ascent,
you cloud the violet bruises in pale powder,
your violent power rising up, up and away.

2 Jun 16

We garland you with flowers to speed you on.
We speed you into the skies with petal confetti,
watch the acorn of your basket grow in reverse.

Your aortal chimney pipes you into parting.
You part from land, are pumped into the clouds--
eyes clouded with rainwater, bow neatly bound.

Punctured into flight, you fled, quietly composed--
on a thorn, a thought, a bruise--you wept, you rose.

Patient Refused Dental Anaesthesia

A threat to our way of life is a threat to our life.
The decision, therefore, was preemptive self-defence.

I was only protecting my country from what the internet
told me was going to happen, and look, so it did.

I watched the predictions come true, on nightly news,
while cleaning out the barrels of my mind. I put

two and two together, just that simple. Maybe you never
did math, maybe you're blind, maybe you're in it with them.

It's hard to tell from a screen name who is your friend,
and who has been put there by the government.

True patriots, facing war, must discipline the body.
You must not let the molar facts dent in your mind.

Nothing about this struggle is going to be easy.
We must suppress the gag reflex, do what must be done.

The gas makes it all too easy to plant a microchip.
I'd rather drown in my own saliva than die on my knees.

Here is the last stand of an angry befuddlement.
Here, the hairy arm of the law in reverse.

What amount of living can teach you?
(Please express your answer in pounds per square inch.)

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

Phrenology

Have you suffered a bump on the head?
Lounging or squatting, cramped in your bedsit,
attended the talks, and glistened in heat?
Good. We may begin. The motor
that whirrs at the fore of man's head,
encasing the lizard brain within,
is a cop, a crutch, an infernal
conundrum--for who among us wants
to be fixed by his skull-shape
to matter, and matter, and endless matter?
Good. Now let us begin.

Mangrove

There is nothing left of me, only
the fist of the earth reopening,
the damp contusions of mud.

There is only mud, and the way
seedlings drown in their infancy,
swirling a hair-root foot past,

way past the inky collusion
of bare light on the branches,
the way night pulls aside,

collusion aside, its materials
for the damning swim through light,
through the bright cells leaping,

materials leaping, homestead lept
into focus by the wayside gutters,
reaching the stream bed beyond,

lept beyond the banks in churn
the gushed waters now seeping
through the tide-like rend, again,

churn again, it is only mud,
flowing its way past, the collusion
of light aside the bright materials,

lept beyond, again, its churning, again,
the banks once seeping now gush,
cells leaping, the fist, the foot,

a hair root, nothing else, me.

To-Do List

Let's paint out the dots between being and doing,
scrubbing the pointilists pointedly, impressing
no-one with our vandalistic proclivities.

Start over. Let's leap the canyon of synapses,
land squarely on our Achilles' heel, the winged
one, always smarting, mercurial, self-evident.

Let's die. Let's die laughing. Let's die together
at the drop of the midnight ball, dropping arsenic
like ecstasy, let's see what this baby can do.

Let's dry out on the banks of a nameless river
of thought. Let's stream our feelings down our face--
Let's begin, shall we? Making signs: "The end is near."

The blank contusions, the banked wall of debt.
The more you strugge, the more the bear trap's teeth
dig in, to that heel we mentioned, a tasty meal.

Let's leap. For fuck's sake, let's leap as high
as possible, not minding where or on what we land,
to put, however briefly, air beneath us.

Here I am again, with my lies. The old lies. The best lies.
Here, in the going down of the sun, we cast shapes
against the by-turns scorched and drenched sky
and I am powerless to change the headlines
to anything more subtle than blame,
and fear, which is heartache,
unable to stand underneath
a banner that reads:
"Make a difference"
because I am
no different
than you

18 Jun

Do German Horses Know That They are German?

Do they know what they have done? Do they care
about the policies of fences, does the glare
of a passing train cause them to blink? Do
they know as much as we do about the coming
weather? Have they been on a diplomatic mission
all this time? Do German horses know
that they are eating German grass? Have they
ever nibbled Austrian grass by mistake? How
many mistakes have been made by now in the name
of German horses? Do the horses have their own
names for each other? For themselves? Do
German horses understand the difference between
meadow grass and meadow? Do they like conceptual
art? Are German horses, in themselves, a form
of conceptual art? Do concepts die? How many
German horses have died while I have been
writing this? Do German horses know? Do they
know what has been done, in Germany, to German
horses, over time? Do German horses like
to be groomed for German voyages? Do they
have a word for grooming in German? In
horse? Are German horses ever lonely? Are they
lonely in a herd? Do German horses ever herd
against the emptiness, and find they are no
less empty than before? Do they know what
must be done? Have they ever done it before?
Do German horses know that I am questioning
their existence in a poem? How many German
horses does it take? How many more?

20 June 16

Fleischautomat
    (imagined German neologism)

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

exchange, expert, debris, warp, alley, dot, zeno, dung, nape, permanence, inland, painfully, henry, parks, precisely

Also:

Insert your card, and the screen lights
up to the sound of gearwork gently humming
through it's task of hack and grind,
the low grunts of a sated carnivore.

Slide your finger over the grease-glossed
touch-screen, take your pick of tubular
delights, packed and twisted into bite-sized
denominations. Tap to pick your condiments.

First comes the smell, pepper and roasted smoke,
then out and out the cooked cylinders process.
Be sure to take your card. Be sure to have
a very nice day. Don't forget your napkins.

Mood Diary

I.

of a train
sliding through the ribcage
of a European capital,

into the humid sky,

28 June 16

Neo-Conservative Time Machine

Time Machine

The only question in is how far back you want to go.
Sometimes we overshoot--aim for the 1950s, end up
depressed in the 1930s; aim for a new Renaissance; 
find ourselves striking a match in the Dark Ages.

We crave a simpler time--pre-literate man as prey
to the dark contusions of his own species, hunting
for answers too tough to chew with a single tooth,
heal with a homemade salve or populist slogan.

Still, we have the poetry: "Take back control" 
rhymes with "Make America great again" rhymes
with freedom from the other, rhymes with bread
in the mouths of the deserving, working poor.

Let us return to Socrates, sipping poison,
leaving the rest of us to do the washing up.

be fooled by the lack of hair. Fool
the hare into springing the trap, tip
sap from the sprung gasket in the maple,
hoaring over with hair-fine frost.

3 July 16

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

peak, monstrous, glare, redemption, wealth, civilization, summit, british, discuss, glued, hera, intellect, ablaze, rice, nightly

Also:

Include the name of a famous person

Summer

We scattered our bodies in the warm grass,
and they caught the glare like shells
awash in foam's redemption. We were
the wealth of a civilisation, summit
heaped on a monstrous peak, ablaze
with the intellect of our ancestors,
which we wore around the temples
in chains of daisy gummed with rice,
and nightly listened out for frogs,
those parliamentarians, booing
in British accents, naming this
one Disraeli, and that Gladstone,
naming a girl-frog Hera, to make use
of our education, while our own names
wafted like mist from ears and nostrils,
leaving us to count the blinking stars.

5 July 16

"Tea or coffee?" the first question of adulthood,
since Lord knows you're going to need something
to get you through it all before retirement.

6 July 16

Assembled "The Man with the Kindest Face" (manuscript)

7 July 16

Free Will

"Breastmilk or bottle?" you don't get to decide
between the hard work of intimacy and plastic ease,
but weaning comes to us all and all too soon.

"Biscuit or cake?" the hallmark of childhood,
since sweetening the deal of your minor role makes it 
easier to bounce between bullies and bicycle accidents.

"Tea or coffee?" the first question of adulthood,
since Lord knows you're going to need something
to get you through it all before retirement.

"Backgammon or bridge?" you must fill the time left
to you by your offspring, smile rememberingly
when they enter, and wave when they depart.

"Oak or Cedar?" you can decide if you like,
or if the whole business makes you squeamish, defer
to the next in line, and await further questioning.

Inventions (That Will Never Work)

"Man is distinguished from all the animals by the fact that he invents and uses tools."
-H. Martin, School Essays and Letters

Bullets coated in anaesthetic.

A newspaper that changes its story
based on your political views

A bicycle fitted with a smog generator
to be treated as an equal with cars.

A tree whose leaves double as tinfoil.

A ballott written in invisible ink.

A gun scope that finds, downloads 
and displays baby photos of its target.

Breakaway seat belts for messy divorces.

The sword in the stone in the shopping mall.

An amalgam of all past Disney princesses.

A company that makes only money from money.

A bank that doesn't have bank accounts.

A machine that turns trees into paper,
on which to draw plans for more machines.

A stick to keep your phone at arm's length.

14 July 16

Unsettling Out & Settling In

Not this dust, but a different dust
clung to the sides of your shoes,
and the light in the sky was different--
more yellow, more pale, more or less
savagely warm to the skin. More or
less is not the same as same, degrees
quicker, more shallow the currents,
more guarded or friendly, the streams,
passers-by, and you a passer among--
chin-up to the skyline--jagged or flat
by comparison, and when you undress,
the light switch flipped, the sounds
of the room gently restless, a sleeper
halfway between this world and another.

I. [Great, the Expanse of an Opened Mind]

With both hands, take it, this piece
of mind, a gift to yourself, a selfie
taken on a stick that extends into space.
Wave at the dot that was you, a seedling
on the prairie, allotment, or balcony pot,
bursting from husk to sapling, grappling
up, and spreading two leaf-shaped hands
out in the simplest prayer: to grow--
and so you water the one thing depending
on you in this world that was humming
before you arrived, and will hum the day
you depart, planting out and patting down,
packing out greater part of you in you,
edging grains of dirt from your nails.

II. [A Challenging Chrysalis]

The doors slide open as you pass, the doors
slide shut. Do not take this lightly.

do not know who you are, but who you will
become. Sealed in glass, your beating heart
apparent as your accent, veined to stimulate
the nerve-goo forming its scribbled blueprint,
tunnelling down the spine's mine shaft,
reclaiming what you thought you knew,
in light, in heat, the gearwork whirring
deep inside the leaf-perched skyscraper,
where already cracks are scaling the sides.
You blink. The winds pursue you at this height.
You flex to find your wings are dry now. Go.

through the ubiquitous coffee shops, giving

the way a lens mis-understands the surface
of places you now inhabit, as if "ordinary"
could describe the burning pleasure of a sip
that used to scald you, cooling in your mouth.

VI. [Difficult Beauty]

hauling up on a humid red-eye, surrendering

VII. [Better is the New Black]

narrow enough to fit a mail flap?

with your song, a melody of war stories,
changling having shed your winter coat.

and remote--catches in your throat.

Hypersensitive

Snails trace their shining highways over the pavement,
a moth beats a cloud of dust in its fiery wake,
but what of the honeycomb's grin, dripping displeasure
at the slow progress of small limbs and fractured eyes?

Once you loved the smell of clover ripening, the sneeze
of the wind carrying news your way from afar, blotches
of honeydew freckling your face in the sun, but now
even the twitch of a distant wingbeat reddens your eyes.

Who is the beekeeper? Who is the bee? A life of hexes
stacks before you--part geometry, part alchemy, mixing
the sweet into sickliness, waxing over the rough truths
and swelling your throat shut to un-pollinated words.

Who will return you to yourself, with an insect's portion
of knowledge, smoking the hive into somnolence, netting
over the keeper's delicate eyes--who but the queen
could command you to stifle that sniffle, this wheeze?
