The Selfish Teens

We formed a band, and went around the streets
raining down our worthless anthems on the poor,
our guts were telescopic, a credit to the wreckage
we succumbed to, wolfing down turnips and kebabs,
angling for a date from dreamy, hated hire girls
like lacklustre spys in meritless B-minus flicks,
we ate the whole enchilada, tossed the parsley
at a hobo, lit our cigars with five-pound notes
and when one passed out, out came the eyeshadow,
our lives a purchase-hire for hire-purchase lino
and cheap, frothy beer. We bested the dulcet tones
of street musicians with football chants, our rants
were against no one in particular. When it came
to an end, we sat down hard on the kerb like robots
whose plugs were pulled, and let the meritocracy
of gutter ants overwhelm our sensible shoes.

27 May 2017

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

fluid, african, genitals, haw, video, grieving, lid, boundless, heartfelt, civilization, manipulation, stitch, eighth, theater, indelible

Also:

Include the name of a famous person

Abduction

The spaceship landed, and we got in
leaving dinner to cool on the table,
nobody spoke, least of all them,
language stuck to the roof our mouths,
tongues probing teeth for morsels.

Why should we tell unbelievers
(who would only call us "hayseeds" and "quacks")
how they glistened like soap on water,
how contact became more tender
the longer we let them wash over us?

Some say we grieve psychosis,
popped like sudsy bubbles before our eyes,
yet all of us felt it--that boundless,
fluid sense of a new life beginning
before we were set down in the field again.

It is their smell I miss the most--
resinous, both sweet and deep,
setting ions in the air on edge,
as though they were always evaporating,
half here, half returned to smoke.

I doubt they will ever come back,
doubt we were chosen any way but randomly,
the way a meteor chooses its crater--
still, on a clear night, in the arc of space,
I feel something vast pass over, call it "grace."

7 June 2017

The Opposite of Sleepwalking

I.

Summer insomnia, I dredge
the grasping hedgerows,
thorned tendrils reaching
to snag and slice me--
an opening in the cloud,
like two lips parting
interrupts the damp heat
that we call "close"--
as two bodies pressed
in the rot smell of grass,
a dusting of seed, chaff,
dander of straw, black dung
sat like immovable toads
daring me to cross their path.

II.

The edges fray between dream
and dream-dealer, card sharp
and simpleton taken for a ride,
I hide behind drowsing eyelids,
the electric sound of agitated
cloud, draw a blue spark from
the fur of the cat, arc toward
horizon, spreading my contrails
like a long line of genetic
disease, inherited malaise,
unrest in the populace, my shoes
untidy, my own self coming untied,
I hide behind wakeful catatonia,
and tremble with the thunder.

8 June 2017

III.

The sky could be a bedsheet,
or a rear-projection screen
taking a little break right now
from machine-gunning still images
at our eyes to simulate movement.
I always wanted a ceiling painted
with clouds, stuck glow-in-the-dark 
stars up there instead, the green
unnatural light so unlike perforations
in the black felt, revealing
a spotlit performance here on Earth,
curtains to twitch at the audience,
and me, left blinking on my mark
delivering a line I don't believe.

12 June 2017

IV.

A single oboe. The low wail that pervades
each morning's commute--milk steamer,
wheel rattle, thud of rubberised door.
A life is half lived without bass notes,
to bring the malady and concussion together--
or melody and precision--depending on the day,
while leaf-rot sticks to your worn-down shoes,
and the laces slap their mocking counter-rhythm.
What are cobbles for, but to trip on, in a drowned 
rat's race to the bottom, wet dog's plea for change,
chugging down hot coffee and stale bagels,
hurrying to the next place you don't want to be--
and always the single oboe, the spitting reed,
crying for her lost calf in an field of hay.

13 June 2017

V. 

The green is going to black,
in a loose rain destined for ground,
the spots on the liver widening,
like pavement swallowed holy and whole--
here is the misbegotten toy soldier,
struggling between two concrete slabs,
propped on his plastic shoulder blades
over the pit of ordinary sidewalk ants.
Every day, I wake and walk suspended--
each cement square a probable trap
door into the familiar dark sludge
of self-decay. I tiptoe, ballerina tight
from water cooler to cork message board,
pinned with slogans meant for another day.

14 June 2017

VI.

The ground is permeable, as am I--
feather-soft in lamplight, packed 
dust under desert sun, cracks
spreading electric, netting
pulled tight to the corpulent 
thigh, mud-filled bladder,
meniscus, the slick of topsoil
fermenting to unction and froth,
as I step, and step sideways
grinding my heel for effect
over the microscope's corpses,
over the battlefield black
with the blood of unseen soldiers
in the mud, and the hardening clay.

18 June 17

The Companion Pony
Bipolar Baby

I ❤ Social Media

Every single one of my Facebook posts
has been carefully, even surgically designed
to give the appearance of someone (like me)
whose mind is not eating itself alive.

Every tweet has been computationally analysed
to ensure a normative use of language.
The interval between jobs on my LinkedIn CV
conforms to mean and standard deviation.

Nobody can tell I am typing faster than light,
or that I slept four hundred hours before posting.
A quip for camaraderie. A quip for reassurance.
Because everyone else is more fragile in the end.

I read. I think. I travel. Here is my non-maniacal
smile, sunlit, somewhere far from home.

19 June 17

VII.

Nothing is left but the wind,
which blows transparent
through my clothing,
what remains of my hair.
No village is safe
from other villages.
And yet we drink
from the same stream.
What becomes of longing
when the fire goes out?
Here are the cool embers
of the one I loved.
And so I walk through night.
And night walks with me.

A furred task. An arterial vacuum.
No rest for the wicker basket.

I am ill, Horatio, and punch
the air not in hard-rock enthusiasm
but to sear the ghosts with my fists,
tourists in the corporeal realm,

forks with knives, spilling soup.
Who else can save us from furred
brush-offs and light winds upending
our not-yet-ink-dried paperwork?

Nothing makes sense. Least of all Tories.

22 June 2017

Hamlet's Brexit

I am ill, Horatio, and punch
the air not in hard-rock enthusiasm
but to sear the ghosts with my fists,
tourists in the corporeal realm,
secretly and harmlessly replacing
forks with knives, spilling soup.
Who else can save us from furred
brush-offs and light winds upending
our not-yet-ink-dried paperwork?
In the eyes of the victors, panic.
In the "ayes" of the mob, a chorus
to sing sweet Ophelia to sleep,
for better to dredge lake bottom
than surface with mud for a face.

23 June 2017

Bipolar Baby I

Bipolar baby don't give a fuck
when the winds whip cool
around his diaper-less front

he'll run like a fool
toward the morning sun--
so baby, stay in school

and baby, watch for his gun.
Bipolar baby has all the luck
with street-girls on the run

and alley-boys kicking up guck
so count your pennies my son,
count yourself one lucky duck.

Bipolar baby can change on a dime
and change out his own diaper muck
that's why baby, he always feels fine.

Bipolar baby can nurse his own wounds,
and baby, these wounds all run deep
he'll lay in his cot, and howl at the moon

And baby, that old moon will weep.
Yes baby, the old moon will weep.
My baby, that old moon must weep.

24 June 2017

BB III

Bipolar baby is out on the street
and the street better watch its back
the lights go out underneath baby feet

and the asphalt goes blacker than black.
He rides in a car without a car seat,
rides your back without a backpack

he rides the bus without a bus pass
and on the bus, baby takes no crap--
he sits by the window, fogs up the glass

draws ghosts with a swipe and a tap.
He says you can kiss his bipolar ass
if you don't like his art or his naps

bipolar baby can sleep for twelve days
in a bed, in your head, in the grass.
And when baby wakes, he sings his praise

and his praises sting like tear gas.
But baby won't cry, except in a phase,
and his phases outlast all the rest.

This baby cries out like the rest.
Yes, baby can cry like the rest.
Our baby cries out for some rest.

25 June 2017

BB II

Bipolar baby has murals to paint
with his fingers, and sometimes his toes
he thinks like an artist, acts like a saint

when his patrons come 'round for a nose.
His brushstrokes are few, his style restraint
coupled with poise and composed with repose.

Yet when they're all gone, he takes up a can
and spatters his studio crimson and blue,
since bipolar baby can't stand his own plan

let alone see his painful plans through.
He bashes his canvas like a diapered caveman,
and breaks up the frames like taboo

he runs through the paint with glee in both hands
and the paint sticks his feet down like glue.
His toes are tingling, his hands shake like fans

Bipolar baby has found something new
he paints with both feet, the feeling is sweet
and the sweet thought makes our baby coo.

Yes our baby can giggle and coo.
This baby starts giggling at you.
Our baby can't stop laughing, can you?

26 June 2017

A rustle, rain-patter, the smell of bread.

Bipolar Baby the Artist

Bipolar Baby has murals to paint
drawing out the light from the shadows
he thinks like an artist, acts like a saint

when patrons come 'round for a nose.
His brushstrokes few, his style restraint
coupled with poise and composed with repose.

Yet when they're all gone, he takes up a can

since Bipolar Baby can't stand his own plan

let alone see his painful plans through.
He bashes the canvas like a diapered caveman,
and breaks up the frames like taboo

he runs through the paint with glee in both hands
and the paint sticks his feet down like glue.
His toes are tingling, his hands shake like fans

Bipolar baby has found something new
he paints with both feet, the feeling is sweet
and the sweet thought makes our baby coo.

Yes, our baby will trample and coo.

27 June 2017

How can anyone write in this climate of disgust
and fear? There is so little left to stomach,
so I write about nothing much and call it art.

I have one nerve left and you're on it.
You had one job, safety pin.

Damage Control

I don't know what to tell you.
The dark will continue to rise
in the street corners, like ink.

The sad admonitions of travelers
fleeing their homes, are accurate
down to the nail, down to the nib.

The world has tired of compassion,
and like a barrel drained of wine,
our tin cups scrape only wood.

Who has not had to chose between sons?
Who has a bomb-proof roof above them?
These are ones rich in possibility.

For us, the story is already written.
Now we wait for the penmanship to dry,
lest we smudge, and worsen, our fate.

our baby is king of the already-dead

baby is rolling the street like a tank,

unclothed imperial, he will never be spanked.

going noiselessly amid the hustle and haste

4 July 2017

13 July 2017

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

centre, boredom, coffin, mid-may, necklace, beef, mild, sexual, ore, paste, graveyard, dye, scoop, trickle, contrary

Also:

Mention another art form besides poetry

Tiny Flying Helicopters

Contrary to popular belief, the waiter is doing just fine.
Look at him, with his bored hauteur, his coffin-nail eyes,
the way he lays the beef on the plate on the table, almost sexual
yet also graveyard-like, the scoop of parmesan redolent of souls.

I was thinking the waft of cheese is the scent of the dead,
when out from my mild dismay popped a more hopeful thought indeed:
perhaps, in heaven, fondue year-round beside a tinfoil fire,
our ski-ankles aching from a day of making snow-angels for real.

But then, of course, it was back to dismay, my old baseline note,
that drone--and not like tiny flying helicopters--that plays
as I abrade the games of life: a teflon pan with plastic bristles,
my knee on concrete (a classic); Marimite against toast.

I almost wrote "a teflon plan", but corrected it for sake of someone
else, like you, dear reader (or listener, depending) to accept
the metaphor more readily, yet now I realise that "teflon plans",
cooked up in musty boardrooms, are the holy grail of businessmen.

I am not a businessman. Yet I'd like to think there's something
in life that can resist the bombardment of sickness, stickiness,
can glide as if permanently buttered through the viscous mud
so tugging at my quotidian ankles (not the ski-lift kind), even now.

I am also not a waiter, though I appreciate their service to mankind,
even whilst lightly ribbing the ones who feign un-death to survive.
Ironic, isn't it? How we do so many un-living things to keep on living?
I, for example, stuff my corpse self into a blue suit and too-small shoes.

I have been in boardrooms and restaurants, and know which I prefer.
Bring the good-smelling basket of bread, lined with a classy napkin.
Bring the profit-and-loss statements for the fiscal (not physical) year.
Bring me a person who can honestly say: yes, I am doing just fine.

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

tray, armpit, johnny, tulip, creep, luckless, zest, method, coax, increased, flap, binding, officer, thyme, amused

Also:

Dead-End Deodorant Factory Job

Bring me the tray of armpits as yet untested.
I will sniff them like tulips, creep my nose
deeper into the zest of each hairy skin flap.

Luckless Johnny, stuck in a binding contract,
unamused, not even madness in your method,
relegated to a pit to sniff pits all day.

Some smell like thyme, others like rotten tar,
and each has a worksheet that needs filling,
the boxes peep, open mouths of square baby birds.

Moistness: 1-5 (please tick), Odour (French spelling,
to give it allure), more finely graded on a 1-10,
with bubbles to give off an air of effervescence.

Only the right pencil will do. Press hard for the carbon,
but not so hard the lead will snap, like two fingers,
which is what Johnny would like to give to his ugly boss.

Beauty is in the nose of the inhaler. Except some scents
will singe your nostril hairs, leave permanent damage,
which is why the insurance waiver is twelve pages long.

All jobs are odious, but some more than others, ergo
to whit, and furthermore, swallowing a stray curly hair,
and don't even mention the lunch break, its pallor. 

Who can eat under such circumstances? Perhaps only a troll,
which is what the forman resembles, with his ear hair
and neon-light-tanned complexion, not to mention the mole.

We all have the right to criticise Johnny's life choices.
Some days can sting the eyes more readily than others.
Who among us has not lain naked, enjoying his own stench?

29 July 2017

Pet Physiotherapy

See how they move, like noodles on the boil,
how they would slip through your fingers
like hair through a comb, how they wave
goodbye continually, wind through wheat,
tidepool kelp, streamers on victory day.
I could swim like that once, dolphin-slick,
bottle nose parting water, toe tips surfacing, 
wave passed through a jumping rope, tremor 
in a steel spring, metallic tang of salt.
I slide my fingers under the arthritic hips 
of the cat and rock her gently as a child.
The rusted mechanics at the base of her tail
crackle like my own toes and knees on stairs,
revving the soft motor, signalling relief.
The skin and loose fur ripple forward,
like maggots on the march, or cellophane.
Now I can believe we mammals are made of water,
once just tubular stomachs filtering the sea.
There is something about a particle we trust,
its pinpoint specificity, the there-ness
we can point at

30 July 17

There is something about a particle we trust,
its pinpoint specificity, the there-ness
we can point at, while waves pass unnoticed
through the air, carrying shock jocks, showtunes, 
and kinky, breathless telephone calls,
pass too under the hull of a super-tanker,
and soup at the captain's table doesn't budge.

Public School Party

Sometimes cocktails come in pill form,
sometimes begging doesn't do the trick.

Who will hold my hair out of my face
as I worship the toilet's gleam?

Burp up a mouthful of Guinness, spit,
rinse, and go back to making out.

The mouth is a stand-in for Ketamine,
Nietsche-an abyss in which to stare.

How did the stars get there so fast?
That is, when did the sun abandon us?

In a maze of tablets and frothy stout,
"There has to be an exit", say the rats.

I can no longer feel his hands inside,
or smell his breath. The party begins.

5 August 2017

Here Come the Dogs

To be numb is to be alive in a different way--
take anemones, for example, curling tentacles
around wayward drifts of sand and toddler toes.

Who wants to grasp at everything fatigues
and fugues, and plays the rhapsodic tune,
but backwards, broadcasting secret messages.

Can you attenuate your ears to micro-waves?
Can you zap and nuke at will, like a president?
Who will protect us from fake news if not a gun?

Too many questions. Let's start from the beginning--
you were born, and that was enough, then stimuli
began, at first quite pleasant, everything new.

You grew, and sophistication grew with you--
of armaments, psy-ops, the cyclops focused
like a laser beam on your preconceived wants.

You want for something, but the hole won't fill--
every shovel of dirt absolved of its dirtiness
and plunged further down, earth swallowing earth.

You too will be swallowed up one day, in an urn
or a wooden box, equisitely varnished--what a waste,
and so you may say of your time spent worrying.

A dog worries a sheep by the scruff of the neck,
the light blood specking its afro-style wool,
with a badge and a riot shield, the dogs advance.

You would halt it all if you could from your couch,
tapping personal details into petition screens,
transmitting with a click your charitable ideals.

To be alive is to be numb in a different way
than you ever expected, once swaddled tight
in a wooly blanket, soothed by the microwave's hum.

8 August 2017

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

complain, pod, bounty, denial, sheen, cypress, print, pumped, lingering, disguise, clapboard, literature, superfluous, scorched, constellation

Also:

Mention two kinds of weather

Walking Sideways

The gooey centre of a crab
is cased, like old-style luggage,
in a thin but effective shell.

I could complain of bounty,
spread denial over my toast,
send back my overdone legs.

Who will trim the cedars
to their recognisable points?
Not the claw of a crab for sure.

There are first-world problems.
and then there are Waitrose problems,
and problems of the patrons of M&S.

To everything, its pecking order--
one has only to study geology
to recognise strata as natural.

My three-layer salad only has two.
My once-sturdy luggage wanders
on its duff-shopping-trolley wheel.

Meanwhile crabs go shuffling,
kickboxers ready for a fight, 
sporting oversized castanettes.

Where are we going?, you ask.
To the centre of the centre of crab.
Brace yourself for soft impact.

Peripheral vision is everything.
Hence the blinders on draft horses,
the dark bliss of the nose bag.

All of us, scorched constellations
of floaters and sunlike-spots
decorating the retina with fireworks.

The glint in the black eye of crab,
gem-set on its stalk like an opal
takes mudflat panoramas sans a blink.

But migraine aura tunnels my vision,
as I shoulder into a painted wall
luggage upended, salad carton splayed.

10 August 2017

Revisions

Horse Optimism

Rain sweetens the grass, 
and the sun makes it tall;
no flies on windswept days; 
no restless leaves when calm.

At a distance, I see where you are. 
Up close, I smell where you've been.

Sun dries the mud,
dew decorates the field.
Lie down in summer heat,
stamp against the chill.

Halfway through every journey out
we begin the ride back home.

Sweet grass from rainy days
a field sparked with dew
breeze to blow the flies away
the sight, then scent of you.


Ancestral Memory

He is yelling something, probably obscene,
though I'll never know as I don't speak goat.
The grass-grinder of his jaw moves aside,
and the tongue protrudes sloppily, a joke
he's savouring like a cheekful of last night's cud.
I want to be his friend, compare pot bellies,
roll in tall grass to scratch and mud to cool.
I worry about the head-butting, though. 
"You worry too much", says the goat, in his way,
eyeing me through the slits in his globular eyes.
He needs a pipe to go with that beard, and a hat--
but of course he likes to eat hats in particular.
I want to help him steal more hay from the ponies,
who stamp out their warnings like headmasters.
He's yelling again, at a bag in the wind,
and soon he'll get his one hooked horn stuck
in the wire fence, craning for better grass.
I pick up a handful, just to feel his greedy lips,
and risk my fingers on those brown joke-shop teeth,
though I try not to laugh, since they're all he has,
and for a moment he rests his chin in my palm,
then yells to scare the life out of me.



Abduction

The spaceship landed, and we got in
leaving dinner to cool on the table,
nobody spoke, least of all them,
language stuck to the roof our mouths,
tongues probing teeth for morsels.

Why should we tell unbelievers
(who would only call us "hayseeds" and "quacks")
how they glistened like soap on water,
how contact became more tender
the longer we let them wash over us?

Some say we grieve psychosis,
popped like sudsy bubbles before our eyes,
yet all of us felt it--that boundless,
fluid sense of a new life beginning
before we were set down in the field again.

It is their smell I miss the most--
resinous, both sweet and deep,
setting ions in the air on edge,
as though they were always evaporating,
half here, half returned to smoke.

I doubt they will ever come back,
sure we were chosen simply randomly,
the way a meteor chooses its crater--
still, on a clear night, in the arc of space,
I feel something pass over, call it "grace."


Tiny Flying Helicopters

Contrary to popular belief, the waiter is doing just fine.
Look at him, with his bored hauteur, his coffin-nail eyes,
the way he lays the beef on the plate on the table, almost sexual
yet also graveyard-like, the scoop of parmesan redolent of souls.

Here I was thinking the waft of cheese is the scent of the dead,
when out from my mild dismay popped a more hopeful thought indeed:
perhaps, in heaven, fondue year-round beside a tinfoil fire,
our ski-ankles aching from a day of making snow-angels for real.

But then, of course, it was back to dismay, my old baseline note,
that drone--and not like tiny flying helicopters--that plays
as I scour my life: a teflon pan with plastic bristles,
my knee on concrete (a classic); thin jam abrading toast.

I almost wrote "a teflon plan", but corrected it for sake of someone
else, like you, dear reader (or listener, depending) to accept
the metaphor more readily, yet now I realise that "teflon plans",
cooked up in musty boardrooms, are the holy grail of businessmen.

I am not a businessman. Yet I'd like to think there's something
in life that can resist the bombardment of sickness, stickiness,
can glide as if permanently buttered through the viscous mud
so tugging at my quotidian ankles (not the ski-lift kind), even now.

I am also not a waiter, though I appreciate their service to mankind,
even whilst lightly ribbing the ones who feign un-death to survive.
Ironic, isn't it? How we do so many un-living things to keep on living?
I, for example, stuff my corpse self into a blue suit and too-small shoes.

I have been in boardrooms and restaurants, and know which I prefer.
Bring the good-smelling basket of bread, lined with a classy napkin.
Bring the profit-and-loss statements for the fiscal (not physical) year.
Bring me a person who can honestly say: yes, I am doing just fine.

The unfinished manuscript is a kind of drug.
It loosens your inhibitions, bleeds your nose,
dilates pupils and knocks the frontal lobe.

I surrender to the royal treatment.
I subsume myself in purple fabric.
Nothing will survive this winter alone.
Least of all the widow in her cage.

bring mead and dancers who ululate
beneath my skin, the red-white-and-blue
of muscle, tendon, artery--enough
to satiate bastille day, July the fourth,

Confirmation Bias

Beneath my skin, the red-white-and-blue
of muscle, tendon, artery--just enough
to satisfy bastille day, July the fourth,
the waving last night of the Proms.

Livery goes back a brutal long way--
arms on the pub where we sip froth
making our livers ache, hands shake,
keeping us in our place at the bar.

Whence the revolution not of planets?
Around it comes again, freedom's thirst.
A ballot is as good as a bullet, hence 
the psy-ops and cyber armies conscript.

Each of us, sway-able as tall wheat.
Each of us full of barley and hope.
The stein of social media self-refilling,
as prophecies we make, then make come true.

These Truths We Hold to be Self-Evident

The Prayer of Santa Administrativa

Give me strength to get through my email inbox,
to draft the memos I have to draft,
to ignore the red tape I can avoid,
and the wisdom to use the delete key as needed.
Grant that I may not so much reply
as to reply all, that those who do not know me
may feel my burning discontent,
for theirs is the keyboard, and the mousepad,
and the salary check they wave over me forever--
all men.

# # #

What We Did in the End

The beeches are darkening now.
The sunlight comes, but faintly,
stencilled into the overgrowth.

Who am I to question the sap?
Its flow determined by raindrops
determined by sun on salt water.

Let us cut the green umbilicus,
run our hands through a cobweb,
shredding the spider's best trick.

We will get somewhere, but limping,
dragging our stick through dirt,
the stench of dead toads on arrival.

Something terrible is happening,
but not here, not to me, not today--
see, new shoots in the scorched grass

while poplars speak the old language
branches scratching at concrete
broad leaves licking the wind.

21 August 2017

Mortgage

It was nothing much, but it was ours--
four walls that gently smelled of damp,
and a black stove to drive rain back.

It wasn't ours, it was the banks
of the slow river, the willow at prayer,
who claimed this place some time ago.

There are more bricks between here and London
than atoms in the sun. My physics is shaky,
my trowel on mortar is a quiver of jelly.

But blow as you might, this place will stand
for home on the edge of an unfamiliar land,
will settle the dust from our worn-out shoes.

When the fourth wall dropped in from above,
the play could begin, us enclosed on stage,
spotlit by desk lamps in overstuffed chairs.

22 August 2017

Homecoming

The airplane was buoyant, the clouds were rapt.
We were doomed to be adorned by our glittering past.

No sounds came forth, the wind went still,
we polished our minds with rags and swill.

Nothing is left of the town that we knew,
our lighters light up the landscape blue.

The air was stagnant, the dust clouds gasped,
we were blessed in the raiment of a gritty past.

The crowds all cheered, the storm frothed up,
we clouded our heads, swilling plastic cups.

The city I left remains how I left it
red in its reticence, dark even sunlit.

We fled in a plane, through air, wind, and cloud,
from our multi-hued past, draped in its shroud.

28 August 2017

The moths and silverfish collude
a pact is drawn in shining dust,
each brushes a wingtip signature
and so the assault on light begins.

We are nothing without internal heat,
though we have lived long underground
rubbing our hands by magma and geysers
feeling our way in caverns by touch.

29 August 2017

So too the insect winter of our past
reminds us, blinking compound eyes
of the wall-walking, ancestral flutter
being bugs and chomping bugs by turns.

What do we fear? A bishop's scorn?
A run on the bank? How little known
the compulsive desires for flame,
flashing like a bulb's explosive dust.

The silverfish and moths advance,
and who can hold the line of shadow,
blinking into the moonlight's glare,
when all we ever wanted was to glow?

# # #

Dividing from Darkness

The moths and silverfish collude
a pact is drawn in shining dust,
each brushes a tendril signature
and so the assault on light begins.

We are nothing without internal heat,
though we have lived long underground
rubbing our hands near magma and geysers
feeling our way in caverns by touch.

So too the insect winter of our past
reminds us, blinking compound eyes--
of the wall-walking, ancestral flutter
being bugs and chomping bugs by turns.

What do we fear? A bishop's scorn?
A run on the bank? How little known
is the consumptive desire for flame,
flashing like a bulb's explosive dust.

The silverfish and moths advance,
and who can hold the line of shadow,
blinking into the moonlight's glare,
when all we ever wanted was to glow?

6 September 2017

Bipolar Support Group

The Winter Garden

Homeostasis for Beginners

First of all, try not to move too much.
Prick your finger before each candy crush,
and after, pee into a plastic cup, and sip it, 
tasting  for sweetness. Blink at a constant rate,
using a stopwatch and a compact mirror,
never--no always--sit before tying your laces.
Stand with the aid of a blood pressure pump,
steady your breathing, a robot with bellows--
blankets in winter, a fan's squeak in summer,
practice stifling both the gag and startle reflex.
Sodium, potassium, and calcium are your friends
but never let on that you like any one too much.
A variable uncontrolled, like a car, will crash--
already you can taste the engine's oily smoke.

11 Sept 2017

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

Religious Fanatic

The articulate arms of a crustacean
will stand for a plate-mailed crusader
multiplying at the soft joints
into a kaleidoscope of poses and figures
with which to lead us through the dark
into wave-smacked shallower waters
lit by silver minnows serving light 
back with their tennis-racket fins,
reenacting a sunlit mirror's shatter,
the underwater birth of a weedy star,
flinging particles of planet outward
to swirl, settle, rejoin the seafloor's dust.
With only a crab to lead us through the cosmos,
we will seek a New Jerusalem in the sand.

12 Sept 2017

Existential

The heaviness of a vacuum can't be measured
with masonry tongs, but must be hauled
up the mountain in silence, day after day
for what we crave is not to fill it,
but to let it empty us, breath by breath
as we pant our steep camino in the sun.

17 Sept 2017

Which witch is still left burning in your mind?
The one with green skin? Or dark pointed hat?
Perhaps the lascivious one, cleavage bared.
Was she just dressing up, or just popping out
with a broomstick between her knobbly knees?
Which witch is which? Come all-hallows-eve
it can be hard to tell the time under dark
streetlight, so you look up at the moon--
ah! There she is, a cookie cutter in dough,
her outline cut with safety scissors,
paper so black, you can't see through--
this witch you want to be, bewitched, betwixt
you and the moon, and now she cackles
to your delight, alight and zooming into view.

18 Sept 2017

Flay [too flip]

"I'm between shells right now," says the hermit crab--
an actress resting or a breadwinner on the bread line,
who can tell? This much is sure--the bird-beak-
defence-system is down; flyovers test penetration
against the soft firewall of his comrades, hacking
and fracking their way to the the sweet white gold.

To be vulnerable is to be alive, [says the charred
body of a protest monk, who won the serenity contest,
smelling his own cooked skin]. I suck a tubular leg
in a cloth-napkin restaurant, careening white muscle
through the knobbly joint with a pop, which turns
no heads, unlike slurping, in the game of manners.

The night thins down my skin, like water glue,
milk skimmed, film exposed. This dark uncovering.

20 Sept 2017

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

item, counter, serving, moan, litter, incense, punch, assuming, rom, chattering, rapt, digging, thorax, widowed, type

Also:

Refer to several different beverages

Cherub Punch-Up

They moan, the willow trees, in delight,
or disgust--who can tell? The ability
to speak Willow lost with the Parthenon.
Though the Dutch Elm shakes its head,

I assume the chattering oak leaves,
scratching pointed remarks in the sun,
know less than they are letting on
about water's pursuit of low ground.

Who will capture the hill of morality?
Who is safe from the floodwater to come?
A root is as good as a dehydrator pump,
when each second-hand tick marks an aeon.

Now comes the cherub punch-up, name flinging,
now comes the Bastille Day of muddy forts.
Let us ramrod our way through undergrowth,
hacking with swords that resemble sticks.

No daydream left. No winner's cigar.
From the moot house drifts decision's smoke,
and we who tremble together in mossy rags
know the full length of days to come.

21 Sept 2017

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

decrepit, steaming, gigantic, variety, attack, fo, buck, careful, casually, lark, candlelight, pizza, ashore, miser, brew

Also:

Mention two kinds of weather

That's Amore

A gigantic steaming pizza, that would suit me well
for a face to wear around town in drizzly November.
The gulls would be a problem, I admit, swooping,
pocking my cheeks to gather cheese in their beaks.

I always wanted to be the moon, didn't you? This song
may be as close as I get, craters oozing pomodoro,
man in a gondola curling his striped back to dip
a pole into thrice-reflected light on the water.

Love has never been more remote and impartial.
Perhaps the love of a judge for justice comes close.
I no longer want to feel, but only beam, to illumine
the lives of others, though faintly, as I am tired.

I am told I was once a gleam in my father's eye.
It mut have been you, moon, who cast that light.

25 Sept 17

Surviving the Rush Hour Commute

I am staring at her immaculate teeth, though she wants 
me to notice streamers of glossy hair, brown arms
of a jellyfish spread wide to embrace the living dead.

Still, it's her teeth, giant oyster pearls,
compact mirrors held up to the shell of an egg,
sun on chalk on bone, a scorching white.

My face is nearly pressed against hers now.
I seal both nostrils against my wrist, huff cologne
instead of alcohol from pores and piss from terriers.

What's a girl like you doing in a place like this?
I want to ask, but know the answer: selling shampoo
to an audience of captive women who want it all.

I don't want very much, it seems. Just another inch
between me and the man sleeping upright in his suit,
hung from the strap, a carcass waving in defeat.

I want to shuffle my feet without apology, lurch
with the train into open space, bend on one knee,
whisper to her, "Right now, you are everything to me."

28 September 17

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

Swing Low

So begins the slow bleed into winter,
crimson in the cheeks of the sky
delighting or warning shepherds--
who can remember? The denial in Berlin
of food for toe-headed children
the open suffocation of Leningrad.
Yesterday in my bipolar support group,
they all walked out, leaving only me
with my thoughts, like, "How appropriate,"
and, "I wonder if someone planted a bomb."
I have never been bombed. Only the daily
strafe of ground-pocking thoughts,
the neuron artillery, radio operator
calling "repeat", "repeat", "repeat"
as the synapses fire for effect.

29 Sept 2017

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

eternally, tube, mock, steadfast, dandelion, abundance, weedy, judge, beef, tether, accurate, unable, coffin, tarry, sixty

Also:

Refer to an an extreme or intemperate landscape

The Shrug

In Gallic, it comes with "bouf",
in Yiddish, "oy", the shoulders
raised on marionette strings,
the mouth goes down and eyebrows
scrunch, while the hands drift
out on leavening bread, palms up.
We shrug a new bureaucracy ("same"),
a failed train, a missed romance.
We shrug to say, "It's not the end
of the world," because we have seen
too much in living memory, because 
nobody ever died from spilling flour.
Bad luck follows like a hungry dog.
We shrug it off. We frown it out.
You say, "What are you going to do?"
to anyone who will listen (but don't 
expect a response) say, "Take the rough 
with the smooth" (but only when it's been 
rough), unbutton your work shirt slowly 
each night, button it again each day.

1 October 2017

Eternally mocking, the birth canal.
You can never ride back up it.

My job is to listen to anyone who cares,
to care for the strong, mop their brow.

What judgment can come from a toad?-- who
knows only the smell of stagnant water,
who just wants to carry sperm on his back
into a safety and algae-stained pool.

Out of the turgid past, a slime-mould
of forgiveness, a patch of brambles specked
with the blood of a martyred hare, whereupon
a spiderweb of dew rests like a shroud.

9 Oct 17

How to Survive a Rush-Hour Commute

First, wear cologne. Waft wrist
to nostrils, exorcise last night's
booze from a workman's pores,
terrier piss, un-showered jogger
pressing his groin to the glass.

Focus the eyes. Adverts are good.
The shampoo lady is smiling at you.
The closer you are to a wall or pole
the less of you there is to brush
with a puffy coat, bash with a bag.

The train will lurch. The fuller,
the more suddenly. Shaken, stirred,
a cocktail never sounded better.
Don't count the stops, just listen
for the headmistress in the speaker

to declare that you may go free.

Titration

The body says, "No"
with a twitch of the eye,
a finger on the keyboard,
stammering. Watch for
the rash, a microfiche
map, ideogram you won't
decipher. Place the glass
on the edge of the shelf,
just as the shelf recoils
from its usual place.
The walls will hold you
as a fipper holds a pin-
ball: momentarily, then
spin, my dervish, mouthing
the words: I just can't 
take any more. Then take
in hand a pill so tiny
the mouth will doubt
it was ever there, 
reaching one finger 
closer, like Adam, 
toward a wholly 
effective dose.

Berry-Coloured Scarf

The day began, almost biblical--
Moses parting frizz-frazzled hair,
Noah in floods of tears by the phone,
Jonah swallowed by his parents' divorce.

Low clouds bumped into each other
like newbies at the roller rink,
a wist for summer was on the breeze,
trees brown and orange as the '70s.

I was popular with the squirrels,
and that was all that mattered--
berries red as teenage lipstick,
mud and moss farting under foot.

Walk with me to school. I'll carry
your burdens, crack immature jokes.
Hair soft and fawn as corduroy,
cheek spots flushed with cold.

30 October

How to Survive a Rush-Hour Commute in London

First, wear cologne. Waft wrist
to nostrils, exorcise last night's
booze from a workman's pores,
terrier piss, un-showered jogger

pressing his groin to the glass.
Focus the eyes. Adverts are good.
The shampoo lady is smiling at you.
The closer you are to a wall or pole

the less of you there is to brush
with a puffy coat, bash with a bag.
Try to avoid the man sleeping upright 
in his suit, hung from the strap,

a butcher shop carcass waving defeat.
Let them all be an admonition to you.
The train will lurch. The fuller,
the faster. Don't count the stops,

just listen for the headmistress 
trapped in the overhead speaker
to give a lesson in elocution,
lament your posture, set you free.

8 November 2017

AR-15

It whispers to me while
the rain-dark road purrs ahead.
I have not become anything
untoward, nor have I been good.

Only God is good they say,
as we sway in the wind,
a willow sweeping her porch.
No time for discontent.

The work we do is the life
we have made, the shape
losing its form like smoke
puffed from a barrel.

What will we find in the rubble?
A rouble, a jam jar, a skunk
poised to protect her young?
We have become our forbearers.

I fix the silencer to my lips.

To say the word "son" and hear no reply

Here are the darkening days. Here,
the imprint of time.
Hera me, and be me, and see me 
off.
No limit, no lovers, no crime.


I have become all the people I never wanted to be
fading in over time like a Polaroid snap,
a Stalinist group picture in reverse, people
added to the crowd progressively, pressing
in for the framing, edging tall ones to the back,
faces round as pennies, showing their teeth.
On the back, one name--my name--written
in grand-motherly script, careful loops
a historian's time-conscious mark.
Look at them!--how could I cut them out now?
So eager to defile my upright sense of self.

11 December 2017

Requiem
	(for Miranda)

Yesterday you were still here.
Today snow has laid itself down
in the lanes, thick as fur.
The flap in the door is locked,
as it would be. You hated snow.
I expect you in my office chair
quick as a ghost, a look to say,
"I've been here all along."
In your final days, my job
was to make sure you kept warm,
stoking the coal fire, tucking
your favourite blanket under
for the payment of a blink.
Once I thought I saw you smile.
Then that last fatherly duty
reassuring you, "I am here".
My body looks for you at night,
a space at the foot of the bed,
and opening the front door slowly
I still look down before up.
No thud as you jump from the sofa.
No late-night wailing for food.
The house-sounds are empty sounds,
the space filled up with snow.

12 December 2017

19 December 2017

Hunting Demons with a Water Pistol Full of Holy Water

I suppose the lumberjack shirts were a giveaway,
stamping our boots after trudging through guts,
the way cyanide capsules tasted of Pixie Sticks,
and the sweet gummy teeth we bared for fangs.
Who can remember the gargoyle face of Aunt Agnes?
Gurning at tripe, lemon-mouthed, golf-ball eyes.
Those are the ones we hunt down in melting snow.
Dear preoccupation, makeshift crosses readied,
one part Joe Friday, the other Father Merrin,
our voices creaking between "out" and "Satan".
Of course the tapwater froze the next day,
glazing the porch with treachery and sacrilege.
Nothing could stop us. The hiss of peeing in snow,
the dragon breath. We subdued a great horned one
then took a break to smoke our candy cigarettes.
"More where that came from," grumbled Jamison,
as Martinez loosened his paper collar, gasping.

Bedtime Story

Tell me a better story. Tell me how
the green man in his glory has made
a place for the living to lay down
their bones, tell me the stones
of the cairns mounded with moss
are hills the dead clamber high.
Tell me two truths, and a tale
worth knowing, string the lyre
up tight, twitch a stitch, give
the goriest facts to the figure
of night, with his scythe, tell
us we're all still alive by the chill
of the dew as it hardens to pearls
and the shaggy white frost gathered
up in the lochs, glassy grave
for the oarsmen veiled in kelp,
who will keep your worst secrets
in his clam-covered mouth.

Sonnet Not Long After Christmas

I never noticed how black the trees
until they were edged with snow,
nor how wide the high street lay
until it was rubbed from its page.

The ground gathers each like a mother,
pulling damp flakes from the air,
sometimes they lay down stark relief,
sometimes they smooth out oblivion.

What little colour winter had left
is eased like a fading photograph,
how gently rooftops disappear
how quiet the breath of snow.

A magpie struts the whitened hedge
in the distance, going blurry.

the sky of colour,

to wind-twanged branches
and glazes the windows
with a blinding sheet
like headlights passing,
the slap of a bellyflop.

29 Dec 17

Pet Physiotherapy

See how they move, like noodles on the boil,
how they would slip through your fingers
like hair through a comb, how they wave
goodbye continually, wind through wheat,
tidepool kelp, streamers on victory day--
I too could swim once, dolphin-slick,
bottle nose parting water, toe tips surfacing, 
wave passed through a jumping rope, tremor 
in a steel spring, metallic tang of salt.
I slide my fingers under the arthritic hips 
of the cat and rock her gently as a child.
The rusted mechanics at the base of her tail
crackle like my own toes and knees on stairs,
revving the soft motor, signalling relief.
The skin and loose fur ripple forward,
like maggots on the march, or cellophane.
Now I can believe we mammals are made of water,
evolved from tubular stomachs filtering the sea.
There is something about a particle we trust,
its pinpoint specificity, the there-ness
we can point at--while waves pass unnoticed
through the air, carrying shock jocks, showtunes, 
and kinky, breathless telephone calls--
they pass too under the hull of a super-tanker,
and soup at the captain's table doesn't budge.
One week later, a needle put our cat to sleep.
She slipped, a silver liquid, from our hands.

1 January 2018

January First

"Happy New Year" said no crow ever,
rooting the mud for a trinket
slipped from a child's wrist
in a tussle with a wet labrador
whose dog-smelling brown leash
was about to give her the slip.
This too is an arbitrary marker,
it says "lost" and then "found",
the way a calendar can change
to suit the Church, peasants
rioting to get back their days.
Soft grapes of deer poop, dog
poop, and clay froth the trail.
No large piles; horses shelter
in wooden stalls with wavy
metal roofs, stamping concrete.
I too stamp after my run,
and brush the sides of my bog-
smelling shoes before clicking
open the lock of the door.
So enters another passage
of time, as though life sliced
like a Dickens novel into serial
chapters, or thin-cut salami,
and who can tell if it's baubles
or excrement coming our way
this time down the path ahead?

2 Jan 18

Legacy

The plum blossom doesn't mind the rain,
nor does the dog mind the plum blossom;
the man walking the dog doesn't notice me,
nor do I notice the snail I just trod.
Between known, unknown, and unknowable,
there is just enough space to breathe
as on a window pane, the spreading blur
into which we swipe initials and hearts,
the better to see the landscape pass.
On what date will the last person
who remembers me finally exhale for good?
My name might exist, a jumble of letters,
in some real or digital record book,
but the train will pass, the features blur.
The rain doesn't notice the plum blossom,
the dog ignores his leash-holder, and I blink
into the mist at shapes I ought to know.

3 January 2018

Shit Christmas

Old black-and-white, whadayaknow?
The branch sways but never snaps.
Are you looking for gold, the glint
of a worm head in slurries of mud?
It's been a shit Christmas for you
too, I expect, this time every year
the wet wool of the sky pulls over,
geese retreat to the green zone.
But here you are, window-framed
and still fending off wood pigeons
from the grubby patch called "home"
with only a pale light to comfort
the slumbering branches, and a beak
to overcome the months ahead.

for sake of sterility. Jotting and pointing, we lay
the books to rest, request a screaming printout
on a punched-and-perforated, green-striped page.
It's all the rage in silicone.

Jesus. It's 2018.

And here I am, in my carapace of dust.
Let me explain. There was nothing,
and then there was the space between
two nothings, and then there was me.

How to begin? The beginning is dull,
like a blade brought down on bone.
The middle is soft at its marrow.
The conclusion splinters through,
tracing its intention in the air.

There is so much more I have to write.
I have so much more still to write.
I am still. I write. Nothing more.

With jacks for you, and a skipping rope
to stand in for a dandelion blown

whose seeds drift like insomnia
over the peculiar lanes of childhood

What is left on the tray is between
you and the playground bullies

devouring soft revenge in mash potato
for the principle of it, making a fist

with jack-heads protruding, picking up
the largest stick you can find

pressing the bars of the municipal doors
like a paratrooper opens the hatch

then headlong into the curse of sunlight
on an afternoon you will recall fondly

through the ant-scorching glass of memory
the fear and bruises long covered over

like the field growing into tarmac slabs
seeded by wind through the weeds.

19 January 2018

Walking Sideways

The gooey centre of a crab
is cased, like old-style luggage,
in a thin but effective shell.

My three-layer salad only has two.
My once-sturdy trundle wanders
on its duff-shopping-trolley wheel.

Meanwhile crab goes shuffling,
a kickboxer ready to fight
in oversized castanettes.

Where are we going?, you ask.
To the centre of the centre of crab.
Brace yourself for impact.

Peripheral vision is everything.
Hence the blinders on draft horses,
the dark bliss of the nose bag.

The glint in the black eye of crab,
gem-set on its stalk like an opal
takes mudflat panoramas sans blink.

But migraine tunnels my vision,
as I shoulder into a painted wall,
luggage upended, salad tossed.

We become scorched constellations
of floaters and sunlike-spots
decorating the retina with sparks.

Picnic for One

I have brought everything with me--
mason jars of bile clack together,
and a canteen full of self-hatred
sloshes when I walk. The sandwiches
are my favorite: peanut butter and 
loathing, and the trial mix is pep-
perred with grief's small stones.
I out spread the horse-hair blanket,
where even the ants will leave me 
alone, to eat my fill of self-doubt.

30 Jan 2018

Will-O'-Wisp
    after the Dungeons & Dragons monster

Sometimes a distant lantern is all it takes
to pull your boots up from the mud once more
and squint through fog (there is always fog)
at the promise of aid for your arrow wound.

A stick-end flame bobs like an oar, a campfire 
stutters, and a cyclops eye must sometimes blink.
Only the light of a god remains constant, seared
in the middle-mind of its clerics and knights.

Someone is calling, from the distance of childhood,
their voice buzzing through a comb of reeds,
splashy words, and indistinct, but calling sure
and meant for you, a tug of mist on your sleeve.

Light an impossible match at the bottom of the sea.
Fish hooks know: "lure" is "love" spelled differently.

Stained Glass

We liked the way it stung
our eyes with colours
nearly lethal,
the crimson scrubbing
of the Lord's wide back
with lashings,
strapped to beams
He concentrates to carry,
lagoon of sweat
like tropical rain,
sweet vinegar the blood
between dark lead.

How coarse, the panes
of molten sand,
cut for commerce
by an expert blade,
the twirl and nick
all in the wrist
cracking piece from piece.

It followed me home
behind my eyes
in negative, a sunspot
where I glared,
and glazed, until
half-dead and dried
my eyelids, blinking
at the chorus
spearing down purples
in their major
tones, the greens'
abrupt falsetto,
defening my eyes.

Ode to Owlbear
    after the Dungeons & Dragons monster

Apex predator, sharp as a beak,
who can tell where fur ends
and the pale feathers begin?
You paw the ground for scent,

then listen like a stethoscope
for beating hearts to spear,
clawing your way to the top.
Never mind who made the tiger--

what deity turned their back
on that mind trapped in its tower
stitching together fever-dreams
to startle the sleepless dead?

Footprints deep in reddend snow
where demons fear to tread.

"Whoever fights monsters should see to it that in the process he does not become a monster. And if you gaze long enough into an abyss, the abyss will gaze back into you."
    -Nietzsche

Aboleth

Your gods mean nothing to me. Show me
the face of an angel, demon, or devil
that has not wept for mercy under my fin.

Crush snails. Spear your frogs and fish.
I secrete a homeland from my pores;
wherever I swim or slither is my demesne.

I know what you are thinking, good or bad.
Your thoughts are excrement to me, jewels
are only tricks of light, weapons toys.

The sound of my coming is deep and sunless,
as the waves once were before the waves,
for I recall the parting of worlds.

You can no more destroy me than water.
You can run. You can swim. You can die.

You all know my cousin. Who mum says
has eyes in the back of his head.

Bulette

Forget "Sharknado", have you ever seen
an earthquake caused by snapping jaws,
a great white leaping from the dirt,
a wake of boulders churning like the sea?

Clean your plate. It cleans plateaus,
ripples the village cobblestones,
pulsing under the land's taut skin,
testing the surface for footfalls.

And when it erupts in the market square,
under a picnic blanket, beneath shards
of starlight in a dozy farmer's grove,
gravel will stream down its crab-claw beak,

like grains in your own short hourglass--
who knew the scythe would come from underneath?


20 March 2018

Crawling Claw

Death is the hand's domain, loss in the space
between digits, webbing that rots before birth.
Even the fingernails are clipped away, heaped
like ribs and jawbones into an unmarked grave.

So why shouldn't the trained hand of a strangler,
knife artist, grasper of simple but effective clubs
take up skilled employment under a new governor,
skittering toward victims on five stout legs?


26 March 18

Crawling Claw

Death is the hand's domain, loss in the space
between digits, webbing that rots before birth.
Even the fingernails are clipped away, discarded
like bodies piled into an unmarked grave.

So why shouldn't the trained hand of a strangler,
knife artist, grasper of the ever-effective club
take up skilled employment under a new governor,
skittering toward its work on five stout legs?

Whoever can read the palm of an executioner,
burned by rope, and callused by the axe-hilt
can trace the dark line of manual labour
back to jobsworth killers of our human past.

A job for life; death comes to everyone.
Snap your fingers, master, it is done.

Dracolitch

Phylactery physchmacktery. My soul was dead
before the scales fell from my eyes, skin 
draped like shredded curtains, dry-roast meat.

Necrosis is a state of mind. Whoever wants
to live forever must die a thousand times
in the blink of a flame-coloured eye.

Call me a hoarder, but who can resist
the sea within a sapphire, dark hills
within an emerald, diamond's glare.

Of immortal works, deeds, and desires
I know which one has caged my soul.
I am bound, but bound to never get old.

Come in close. Feel my hushed breath.
You can not kill what belongs to death.

12 April 2018

15 April 2018

What We Were Given We Did Not Know

Once the thing is inside you, you can travel
with the ash-coloured leaves awhile,
and sip the dark pools in passing
without fear of homesickness or thirst.

Absorption is gradual, as the clay-mud knows,
though once the platelets are convinced,
it will swim through the dark capillaries,
seeking the nuclear light of an innermost self.

So you become by shedding a once-useful coat,
and lying beneath a moon that was always close,
your next breath a foregone conclusion (how nice)
and each thought predetermined by the last.

Sure, one foot can listen to another in snow,
while wind in the desert erases even tiny success,
since backward has become the new forward, now 
even the willow must lift up its arms to dance.

Praise the tiny capsule for shelter, belonging
to the arthropod, the well-hulled vessel we plane
to take the waves face-on, and smother back.
Our beginning was there, in the energetic foam.

Now we have learned of buoyancy, and heft
our small belongings with us place to place,
while the blood rushing by our eardrum protests,
and the gravel mutters under our restless boots.

What We Were Given We Did Not Know

Once the thing is inside you, you can travel
with the ash-coloured leaves awhile,
and sip the dark pools in passing
without fear of homesickness or thirst.

Absorption is gradual, as the clay-mud knows,
though once the platelets are convinced,

So you become by shedding a once-useful coat,
and lying beneath a moon that was always close,
the in-breath a foregone conclusion of the out--
and each thought predetermined by the last.

Sure, one foot can listen to another in snow,
while wind in the desert erases even tiny success,
and since backward has become the new forward,
even the willow must lift up its arms to dance.

Praise the tiny capsule of shelter, belonging
to the arthropod, praise the hull we plane
to take the waves face-on and smother back.
Our beginning was there, in the energetic foam.

21 April 2018 (Amsterdam)

Self-Study

In the form of a riddle, the sparrow came,
a sprig in its beak of unknown origin,
traveling on a jasmine-scented wind.

Standing still is death, and death comes
quietly as a vine curling around its branch.
With only a hammer, I could make a new life.

With only a small birds's mouthful of song,
the seeds begin to sprout in our beaks.
With only you beside me, a witness.

In the form of a joke, of a tirade,
the prophets flocked in their brown coats
to peck at cracks in the town square.

Standing still is life, and life descends
casually as sunlight on a greening vine.
With only a kiss, I could set you free.

In the form of an old man, breadcrumbs came,
and swelled in my belly to fill the hollow.
So I travelled awhile on honeysuckle wind.

23 April 2018 (Amsterdam)

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

infernal, stripes, glorious, rhythmic, mortar, sights, randomly, testing, lid, brightly, vice, signal, bombing, squandered, committed

Regibald Rottingdam licks his fat lips

As this book no doubt attests, Marvin was a wonderful teacher of poetry. He taught me that constraint brings freedom, that the political is necessarily personal, and that writing through good times and bad is what makes one a writer.

He was also an example to me of how to live in the world as a poet. His approach to life served as a template that I could adapt to my own particular circumstances. In doing so, I began not only to admire but to put to use those well-worn human tools: poetry, philosophy, and a sense of humour. More and more, these have become a means to survive in divisive times and still remain sensitive and awake.

I have sometimes compared Marvin to Yoda: a master teacher of the unteachable "force". He is a force in his own right, and mine is just one example of an inner life made richer for having known him.

2 May 2018

"I am not yet able... to know myself; so it seems to me ridiculous... to investigate irrelevant things." -Socrates via Plato

"A person is smart. People are dumb"
--Agent K, Men in Black

An awful lot of staring out windows
goes into the practice of poetry.
An awful window stares out at the lot,
practicing its poetry.

Big data in the fronds exploding spring.
Big data in the chestnuts spiking.
Big data in feathers ruffling wind.
Big data the switchblade grass.

Quantify thy neighbour
for to know him is to love him
to love is to multiply
light sown across the sky

4 May 2018

Ettin

We must always hunt downwind, thanks to you
 You're the one whose tusks are rotting out
You can barely swing a club straight
 You're one to talk, dulling your blade on rocks

We won't find food out here, let's go upstream
 You make me laugh, our meal's the other way
We'd starve if it were all left up to you
 I know my way around. I share this belly too.

For Annam's sake, stop dragging our left foot.
 Slow down, they'll hear us at this rate.
Hear you, more like, shouting at the wind.
 My ear's right here. No need to yell.

Great, you lost them. If only we could run.
 Our legs are fine. Heads are another one.

7 May 2018

Explanation of the Decapitation Trick

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

month, spirit, sovereign, hall, charter, jail, islander, mule, performer, sunset, spire, belfry, creator, party, mammal

Also:

Include a major city

Explanation of the Decapitation Trick (2.9)

A clown on a wooden crate will serve for a victim
in the warmer months, when crowds dredge the square
in a party spirit, eyeing the sunset performers
whose patter and bright mule-carts hook them in.

One plays the jailer, another the sovereign condemned.
So they draw their performance down the long hall
of expectation, chartering the taut spool of time
while pigeons heckle and spatter from the belfry.

Haven't critics always lobbed from pointed spires?
Islanders unto themselves, under-suckled beasts,
gobbing on creators and their vulgar fans alike.
Vox pop looks up, thumb-on-nose, jingling the hat.

For the gasp, a paint-faced man goes ostrich-down
as his double emerges, chin-up to cheers and guano.

12 May 2018

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

monk, flower, wholesaler, health, chin, sky, justice, proxy, performer, plank, board, safety, algebra, portrait, opinion

Also:

Mention two kinds of weather

Art Critic's Opinion (3.1)

There is sun and there is sun,
crowning the monk's tonsure
a bright garland of flowers--
the wholesale simplicity
of surface and light.

You can judge a man's health
by his chin alone, the jut
skyward, jaw-muscle justice,
the point a proxy for purpose--
in beard, a costumed performer.

I lay these planks for you
to board in safety, solving
the algebra of ex-lovers
asking, "why?" with a face,
a pate, a glance, a wink.

Such is the portrait, 
such is the restless sitter.

16 May 18

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

heredity, forest, king, python, dream, boss, passion, builder, fatigue, custom, python, thicket, ticket, heaven, infection

Also:

Refer to a particular sensation

Alas! (-0.1)

Poor Yorick my ass. I got off easy.
Heredity is kindest when it's short.
Let the forest weep, the poisoned king
dream of pythons and immovable heaven.

We who carry princes on our backs
are stokers of another's passion,
infected, jaw-to-toe, with fatigue
and a builder's helping of soreness.

The thicket's the ticket to catch
the breeches of the riding dauphin,
scratch off a flea for quick relief
and a momentary cigarette break.

Even my skull has become your souvenir.
Remember me fondly, parasite.

17 May 2018

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

loquacity, leopard, weapon, mercy, interest, labyrinth, furniture, malice, ghost, chloride, atrocity, cowhide, corner, vehicle, musician

Also:

Refer to an an extreme or intemperate landscape

The Circle (4.0)

The frozen north, where loquacity goes to die,
and even the growl of a snow leopard stalls
in the weaponised air, the merciless ice.

It may interest the labyrinth-like brain 
of science to know what furniture inuit sculpt
without malice from their ghostly surroundings.

Who hasn't tasted the chloride sting outdoors,
witness the atrocity of frost over cowhide,
in a corner, clutching the snow vehicle's keys.

Only a musician can tune the strings of the wind.

18 May 2018

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

determination, ritual, shotgun, forest, silence, interest, deluge, angle, tomb, hurdle, apple, revolt, busybody, diamond, silence

Also:

Include the name of a boardgame

Friendly Game

Checkers will do. Hopping with determination
from square to ritual square, shotgun
at the diagonal ready facing a forest
of chips, the silence of interest waning,
the deluge of pieces, sliding at angles
across the flipped tomb of the folding board,
apple-red and cinder-black, the revolt
of pawns and busybodies, slanting
from squares to diamonds, from diamonds
to specks of ash and blood, to silence
and rage-quits, bodies swept in a box.

Refer to a war, battle, or famous warrior

Here was my predicament, my gilt pill
sliding a trumpet-length of oesophagus
dissolving gold into murder, evidence
of the brute acceptance of minerals
into the body's gymnastic sucker-roots.

Big Data

I.

Big data in fronds shrapnelling.
Big data the chestnuts spiking.
Big data feathers roughing the wind.
Big data the switchblade grass.

Big data buttressed cathedral.
Big data crumbling in bas relief.
Big data the parapet's pirouette.
Big data vector of spires.

Big data the shards of an iris.
Big data bubbles in amber.
Big data dust motes emoting.
Big data still pond taking rain.

II.

Big data slides on a microscope.
Big data takes small, makes big.
Big data soil under fingernails.
Big data digs data big.

Big data my mother. Less of that.
Big shoes on big clowns in small
cars on loose ground digging
graves for big plans doing jigs.

Big data skulls in big fields.
Big minds in small lands
running hands through the data
like diamonds or droplets of gin.

Big wins. Big world of big sins.
Big data my echoing clicks.
Big data the big new big thing.
Big data big men with small dicks.

III.

29 May 2018

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

palpable, mortar, directed, calendar, brimming, wail, sticky, flimsy, unable, amazement, ordered, freezing, secretly, lipstick, security

Also:

Refer to a particular sensation

Ticker

So now the drizzle, now the freezing fog.
Anyone in my condition would agree

to the stethoscope tests, drumroll please
and clean bill to wander the meadows

collecting their small packets of water
as even my own pump slowly fails

to rid the bilge around my heart
of salt collected from too many rains

and the spring sighs, giving over
its once-tight coils to time.

Still, there is dew becoming crystal.
Who can call it less than precious?

Alas!

"Poor Yorick" my ass. I got off easy.

Read Deborah Alma

14 June 2018

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

duchess, erotic, sentimental, noun, romance, graveyard, scented, pie, proof, blur, o'er, sam, blazing, cooling, breed

Also:

List three unusual habits

What it Says on the Tin

We're fresh out of pity, I'm afraid.
It's normally aisle four, top shelf,
kept out of reach of small children.

Erotic Dutchess I think was the brand,
a sentimental noun, brined with romance
and a hint of graveyard flavouring.

Don't let the scent of pie disprove
your blurry notion of unsavouriness,
the world o'er knows it as rot.

Uncle Sam's Blazing Pistol is another,
gun sights cooling on your stern gaze
as you scan the ingredients carefully.

Then there's the generic blue-label,
not quite the same--bean-scented, mealy,
it sticks to the pan like semen.

Be sure to refrigerate after opening.
Green flecks and tadpoles surface-skim--
once soured, it's best served cold.

19 June 2018

Elegant hedgehog. My well-studied scalpel.
How have we not behaved ourselves in sync?
There is still time. The wind blows petals.

We roll the dice blindfolded. One hand punches
above our weight, the other below the belt.

A gilded pill slides the trombone-length of oesophagus

It is the queasy kind of Saturday where everything lags--
the mason jars of sputum glistening in the sun,
your labrador, matted down and hugging the floor,
a pair of boots whose eyelets watch from afar.

20 June 2018

Pig-child floating in brine--
whose were you? And do you share
their cheeks, the double-joints?
Somewhere, a mother ruts dark clay
where you sprang as a pulled reed
into the chilled air of just being.
Now you are kept from decay by salt,
and the studied tricks of voyeurs
who admire your celestial smoothness,
scratch their clipboards with stubs.

25 June 2018

Into the furnace of my body I place
the policies of the current administration
and the young man who, defending them, spat
in my digital face, and promised to find me.

The waves of heat reach from tall grass
and panting, I have no breath for cursing
the mayflies their accidental martyrdom
as they gag me with suicide dives.

It is not a race against anything.
The ground gives back with each step
explosions of chaff and dried-out dung
and my breath becomes ragged with anger.

Now the eyes blur with vinegared sweat,
now the arms strain at their sockets,
and the round pupils of indifferent cows
poised to sound their ceaseless alarm.

28 June 2018

Now the lashes blur with vinegared sweat,
now the arms strain at their sockets,
cows follow me with their goggly eyes
poised to sound their haggard alarm.

I run until my only surviving thoughts
are breath, and step; breath, and step,
until the fire in my legs rises up, ands
I am the torch itself, carrying nothing.

Catharsis, 6am

Into the furnace of my body I place
the policies of the current administration
and the young man who, defending them, spat
in my digital face, and promised to find me.

The waves of heat reach from tall grass
and panting, I have no breath for cursing
the mayflies their unintended martyrdom
as they gag me with suicide dives.

It is not a race against anything.
The ground gives back with each step
explosions of chaff and dried-out dung
and my breath becomes ragged with anger.

Now the lashes blur with vinegared sweat,
now the arms strain at their sockets.
Cows follow me with google eyes
poised to sound their tired alarm.

I run until my only surviving thoughts
are breath, and step, one then another,
until the fire in my legs rises up, until
I am the torch itself, carrying nothing.

29 June 2018

They stand and drowse, and one is down,
brown belly toasting in a weedy sun.
What do they stand for? Horses unite,
but for the herding instinct--why?

They stand for horses. I stand beside,
and let my limbs drift to the muzzle
of whiskered felt and huffing holes
down the long skeptic's bone of nose.

Ah! There it is--horsiness itself
in the hay-blown sneeze, the waggle
of a scrotal jaw, flash of teeth,
millstones set in jagged yellows.

3 July 2018

Glasswalker

Start with hot pavement, beach sand,
pine needles and spiked holly leaves.

9 July 2018

Work your way up to silica packets,
crushed beads and boiled sweets.

Soon you will know the ball and heel
as a captain knows stern and prow,

rolling over the crests of waves,
as the bilge takes on black water.

A toddler can make herself heavy;
make yourself light as a Blakean etch

the line between body and ground
resting as a child on mother's chest.

Breathe and step, a blindfolded Lear,
wet chick nudged over the nest.

You lived for this moment, come at last
weightless over the staining glass.

13 July 2018

16 July 2018

Sent poems to George, edited a bit

19 July 2018

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

rule, prophet, ceremony, spoon, psychic, deft, shrill, blamed, primal, bristle, acting, drenched, guts, oar, mouthful

Also:

Include a major city

Wood pigeon on a wooden branch in a wood

Prophets wrote the rules on our tongue
then blamed our love of nettle tea--
like swollen oars, each mouthful bristled
until even the ritual spoon wouldn't fit.

So safe we were from those gilded pills
sliding a trumpet-length of oesophagus
that we breathed through our nose easily
like spies resigned to the firing squad.

The taste of our own blood was enough,
sea-water, metallic, mixed with catarrh.
Through the thin roof of our mouth
the old songs pressed into our mind.

Who can call this anything less than love?

23 July 2018

Who can call this anything less than love?
The bright cymbals crash their epiphanies
while our minds chatter like box-seat hens
missing overture, chorus, and climax.

No rest for the wickedly clever, sure--
A new headline demanded each day, no matter.
A new outrage to warm the veins in our neck,
saving on scarves and the itch of high collars.

6 August 2018

After the Election

Monochrome

The baker in the black shirt
resembles clouds at night,
smoke in an inky bar,

8 Aug 2018

In the Kitchen where Nothing Happens

Sifting spoons in the drawer
will serve for a glockenspiel.

How brightly the foil gives back
whatever light we send its way.

And the egg does not resist
the tapping of its head.

Somewhere a firearm is loaded.
Somewhere, a border checkpoint.

The kettle sounds against forgetting
lest it burn, lest it bomb.

Somewhere the queue for bread.
Somewhere the stained blindfold.

Oven mits ward against blisters.
A room fills up with smoke.

Therapeutic

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

misery, downpour, accuracy, exclude, crap, pyramid, forbidden, retire, brute, men's, peacock, bending, fatherless, sagging, unknowable

Also:

Refer to an an extreme or intemperate landscape

Therapeutic Application

Misery comes in triplets
waltzing downpour,
accurately excluding
the white dog crap.

Beneath the pyramid,
forbidden chambers
where dust retires
brutalising stone.

Every man a peacock
bending his head,
in a featherless
sag toward death.

Unknowable I like best
no effort required.

Cellophane flickering
like a campfire.

# # #

Waiting Room

There is still time. The wind blows petals.
Take my hand, like a dove, in yours.

The cool beads of honey are are softening.
Through a catheter, the daylight is drained.

Repeat: I will not tear up this time.
Repeat: without moonlight, no sun.

Wallpaper, torn, will serve for a door.
Through the smallest crack, we reminisce.

Bring the limp flowers to my table.
Spread out your hand like a starfish.

Between my fingers, a fake cigarette.
Between our palms, the paper-thin prayer.

20 August 2018

Include as many of the following words (or variations on these words) as you can:

nape, framed, monkey, crippled, lung, woolen, daffodil, executive, homeless, frown, determined, airport, revision, tidy, unstable

Also:

Refer to something that once upset you

There is more to the neck than the nape.
Pick one type of shield: blast, riot, human.

When did thinking of the children
cease to be socially credible?

The logic of dispassion clinks along
bright pennies deep in the well.

A shoe cripples the daffodil
sighing into the weaponised air.

Truck. Duck. Dump. Lump.
Pick two to make an administration.

There are no more prizes. Now what will you write?
Of the daffodils? A planet-bright star? The war?

Come take my hand, and let us whisk the night air
while the drums go stamping across an unsteady bridge.

Flesh Golem

# # #

Cloaker

Even shadows know to watch their back.
I glide the cavern ceiling like glass.
And those who know the soft, low drone
have heard the pumping bellows of death.

Disrobe and I will embrace your neck,
a cape fit for a lich crowned king,
a hood to keep out the darkness,
swaddling the night against you.

A lone wet splotch of camouflage,
ink spilled from a last testament,
my second self twirls like a matador
as I close on your astonished face.

Manta turned mantle, skuldugger skate,
we smother what we most appreciate.

11 September 2018

Flying Sword
Flying Snake

Fire Giant
Frost Giant

Flesh Golem

A glass of water, a mug of ale--
who can say the vessel matters more?
Yet here, in my clay, I am defined
by the dark shambling I have become.

Once, I shelved books in Silverymoon,
now I tremble gruel from hand to mouth.
My voice, hands, face are not my own.
Second to flame, a mirror unglues me.

Then the blood surges my stapled veins,
stripping me to muscle stitched on bone
so muscles to thy maddening chore,
flipping whatever tables I cannot turn.

Encased in piece-work, I rage alone--
even these howls are not my own.

15 September 18

Gelatinous Cube

Let's make one thing clear: a spotless
passageway only leads to no good.
Crypt janitor, cave and tomb Zamboni--
seen only by a torchlight's gleam.

Thanks for keeping the walls so clean,
the spiderwebs and bits of goblin brain
your crudité, aperitif, gliding toward
the main course, table of four.

Without reservation, you press on--
a gigantic one-man Jello shot, boy
you sure know how to party, chugging
the unobservant and the slow.

Seems mum was right all along--whatever
doesn't melt your face will make you strong.

Hobgoblin

My name is legion. My legion is my life.
There will always be drums in my ear,
and boots were made for marching horizons
silhouetted with ballista and mangonel.

24 September

In life, in death, I serve Maglubiyet,
who favours us with his cruelty,
even as an insect collector spears
the strongest and most colourful.

Let it be a halberd, arrow, or scimitar
that crumples away this sack of skin,
releasing the rage of my spirit
upon the endless fields of war.

Until then, I take my enemy's breath
with a sharp blade, and swift death.

Ice Mephit

An icicle for a nose, a spear--
an icicle through the heart.

1 October 2018

How long have we worshipped
the furnace of the sun?

Yet we stand on solid water
making shade with our boots.

No two alike, one day snowflakes
will have their planned revenge.

And we, who took cold for granted
will taste the sharpest slivers.

Watch, in the blue crevasse
how the shadows gather weight

testing in creaks and groans
sharpening unseen blades

2 October 2018

Jackal

3 October 

Jack

Call me "common" if you like--
peasant, shopkeep, farmer, serf
I trade in all professions,
swapping roles like suits.

Average by every measure, I am
here to be taken hostage, point
the way, whisper plot secrets
across the ale mug's froth.

Common is my tongue, common
is all the rest of me--I tumble
like dice from a deity's hand
my gene strands twisted by luck.

Around each corner, it's me as well--
with new face, voice, and tale to tell

6 October 2018

Who said the light on the mountain
promised to last? As the last berries
of autumn deflate, so we gather seed
and chaff in hand, ask, "Don't you know
who I am?" I'm here on full scholarship,
destined to be picked, don't you know
I deserve your body, contractually 
close to mine? O to be young again
and slam the car door, zip his balls
struggle as his breath fogged the window.

Confirmation Hearing

There are more men in this world like him
than I have breaths left to say, "I'm sorry."

Tbe sky gathers light, soft as a blanket.

8 October 2018

Tbe sky gathers its blanket of light
over the sisters, mothers, lovers--

who know: the more brittle the pane, 
the sharper its pieces when shattered.

Never again can I say, "How dare you?"
Never again: "I heard you the first time."

And only a liftetime spent listening
could dissolve, like water melts stone

what was confirmed in those wood-paneled
chambers, pressed into fraternity walls.

There are more men in this world like him
than I have breaths left to say, "I'm sorry."

In the Kitchen where Nothing Bad Ever Happens

How brightly the foil gives back
whatever light we send its way.

And the egg does not resist
the tapping of its head.

Somewhere a firearm is loaded.
Somewhere, a border checkpoint.

The kettle sounds against forgetting
lest it burn, lest it bomb.

Somewhere the queue for bread.
Somewhere the stained blindfold.

Oven mits ward against blisters.
Smoke alarm screams through the night.

Self-Portrait in Smartphone

The thumbs up, the purple heart,
and a man drooling for tacos
are my three most-used emojis.

But enough about me, let's see
some photos taken at arms length
in places you wish you had been.

I protect my screen with a thin 
but hardened film that resists 
scuffs, rage quits, and glare.

All sex, no case, the smooth
rub of--plastic? metal? glass?
against my upper thigh.
