How to be a Gringo in Your Own Hometown

Warm air rises from urinals packed with ice
as my kidneys sigh audibly in relief,
rum and soda, sugar's kissing cousins
expelled like troublesome teens.

My college buddy wanted to see Mexico,
not the marigold dresses of a quinceanera
but the easy-clean dance floors, ever slick
with wasted youth and their commerce.

(I grew up on the border, speaking both.
On the first day of kindergarden,
they called me "huero"--a pale Mexican--
other names followed when they learned the truth.)

With the peso pegged like laundry at ten-to-one, 
booze cost less than the blue bottled water
so we Hemingway'd through touristic squalor
never more than one tank of gas from home.

Mark was a good dancer, but not good enough
to attract much company besides the hawkers
so we found ourselves alone together
in the pulsing city, swarmed like bees.

Trying to have fun stopped being fun,
and having grown innured to the parasites,
I forgot to warn him about the tap water,
so shitting blood, I drove him back across.

We lost touch, and I never went back.
My Spanish has grown rusty here in England,
but the woman from Valencia still smiles at me,
as I search for the polite form of address.

16 July 2021

Nerd Origins

Butter fingers. Butter bones. I go down with a splash.
The secret to living is not wanting to die.
The secret to dying is not wanting to live.
So we drop into the loop, a programmer's bug
punctuated by alarm clocks and ill-fitting suits.

There is no more sagacious herb than parsley,
having evolved onto the plate but never eaten.
Cheering up the egg cup, whose mind is elsewhere,
missing the point of plating-up entirely.
How often are we condemned to die before breakfast?

There is not truth in this, only mild regret
on toast, for it spreads as easily as jam.
I have not been entirely forthcoming, having seen
what happens to those at the front of the line.
I go down with a splash on the football pitch.

I go down after breakfast, and up it comes.
Alone and surrounded by pointing ill-wishers,
I want not only the moment to end, but me.
So began a love affair with computer screens,
typing out commands like a vengeful god.

22 July 2021

Dog is love spelled backwards

Spalted Maple

Craggy half-ghost, sweeping your conservatory roof--
how tidily have you lived your life. I too
enjoy your ruddy-leaved tree, cultivated
to believe it is, and will always will be autumn.

You gave me two rough-split logs, and I shook
the wood lice, peeled the bark, and struck
them into quarters with my second-best hatchet,
revealing what time and weather had done.

An acer so sweet, white rot and black-streak
could hardly resist, and so the weak grain
became marbled with its half-reclamation
in the rough, persistent business of decay.

How long have you got?-- to clamber up ladders,
cut topiary for your nonagenarian neighbour
look up at that maple, and remember the day
you pulled it from a pot, and patted the soil.

Today you look sprightly, weed-whacker
slung like a sword at your side, striding
the newly-mulched lanes of your making,
a pocket-square of land all yours to shape.

The spoons carved up nicely, the pen
turned out pale but dignified, so I
took it round, and put it in your hand
and you wondered at what the gift meant.

You told me your daughter swung on that tree,
the one I've seen visiting in your garden,
from residential care, her thirty-year home,
eyes fixed on the ground as you speak.

Now you've got the chainsaw out, pruning 
the boundary between you and the world
so sharp and perpendicular, so dense
it glows in the cloud-lit green.

2 August 2021

Because Dog is Love Spelled Backwards

list 1:
opinion, carry, craft, whip, acceptance, candidate, metal, concert, allowance, spine

list 2:

vow, marriage, gown, veil, chapel, toast, champagne, ring, diamond, pearl, gold, congratulations, aisle, cake, dance

It is an opinion thoroughly disavowed
that to carry a weapon, like love,
like acceptance, or a whip
one must conceal its intentions
in a sheath made of humorous quips.

Long Covid

Being healthy is a privilege, not a right.
So when in the damp grasses, an elephant
comes to hover its cylindrical foot
over the softest part of your body,

and the spines of grass turn porcupine,
and though in the midst of mayflies
you seem sweetest, the tang of metal
is all you can taste in sunlight--

the word "acceptance" is a smack on the head,
and bending down to tie your shoes, "compassion"
is a kick landed at the base of the spine.
Down we go, bowing to the lesser gods.

Excuse me miss, how long until we land?
I'm sorry sir, we haven't departed yet.

4 August 2021

oboe, weasels, beacon, litigious, bats, unquenchable, protection, untreatable, cathedral, projectionist

Also:

Mention one or more unusual or interesting animals
Include a type of music.

9 August 2021

Animal Short

The oboes begin, in slow retreat
and the camera rises up on leather wings
we are captives of black and of white
at the mercy of the sighing projectionist.

Here is the ocelot, the beaver, the skink,
parading before us in time to the tunes,
slack and ribbing, the flop of clown shoes
saying: more giggly than grotesque.

Perhaps they are watching us too,
in the flicker-light of our velvet cathedral,
quenched in the beacon, swept by the spot
teeth chatoyant in our open mouths.

We children are closer to the ground,
and more recently lifted from quadruped state
so the kinship transmits in rascal photons
at twenty-four frames per second.

So another budget appetiser by Coronet,
Alfred-Higgins, or Castle Films stutters 
to ground, tongue flapping in the reel,
wiped by the curtain from memory.

It is always the feature we're after.
The popcorn goes down wolfishly,
and we squirm, wormlike at intermission,
delight just around the next frame.

What of the weasels, their clever disarm
of the garbage bin ropes and padlocks?
Gone, in a streak of celluloid fur,
blown out like a candle on cake.

Once I was silver, and wide as the sky.
A boy could get lost in my longings.
Now it's all on-demand, and we demand it,
squirming, worm-like on our sofas.

The horns ascend, the lens zooms in,
on a thumb on a box in a lap, clicking 
like a gear or a pendulum clock
in the soft, kaleidoscopic glow.

16 August 2021

Spring Cleaning

Discount Books--what could be better?
I bless the place with poetry--in boxes,
and in bags, tape the broken ends
and double-up the plastic sacks,
no light matter this, I make banter
with the polo-shirted volunteer
who helps me excavate my tiny car,
returning like religion, trip after trip,
to shape his arms into a scoop,
and heft the wordy buggers into place.
"I guess a poet died," he wonders--
I almost wrote "muses", but this
is a serious matter--so never mind.
"Yes, I say, that's right" and grin,
daring him to be bolder, and he is--
"Who was it?" he asks, knowing
one should never ask such things.
I tap the lip of a cardboard flap.
"It's me," I shrug, and turn away.

The Good Berries

How much sweeter for its transience
the blackberries mown down
by the tractor-mounted chainsaw
the lump removed from a friend
whose post-anaesthesia texts
were giddy and unlike her.

What can be removed can be replanted,
or so they say of the cedars
stump-ground by shrapnel.
They are not news. Our interests
rarely stretch the band
beyond kinfolk and profitability.

I have been accused of loving trees
more than people, wood over flesh.
See the rich veins of phloem
reddened in the Norway Maple,
green in Ash, bone white Hazel,
whose branches point at sunlight.

Like a child, we want to sit
in the highest crook
to see what the gods see
when they permit us to pull out
and pull apart all they have made,
blackening the good berries.

30 August 2021

More About Juniper

I used to eat the berries as a kid,
green among the soft spiked fronds,
a bubblegum as bumpy as a toad.

They blotched the cement under foot,
but broke apart a dryness in the mouth
part witch-hazel, part witchcraft.

I slip on a shirt from Jermyn Street,
carpenter's jeans stropped threadbare
and spritz gin cologne on my wrist.

My top half is ready for the screen.
At lunch I'll swap into t-shirt, boots,
and anorak, waltz the dog through mud,

smelling as as I did the night I poured 
a half bottle of of Bombay Sapphire
down the dorm-room sink. "Never again."

I still drink tonic, the quinine sting,
clack of ice cubes, and ritual lime
rare warm evenings when work is done.

Even the dog loves the smell of me.

Teasmade

It starts with a plastic click, 
then a low rumble 
like marbles grinding in a bag. 
Then the roiling gives way
to sputtering, then the gasping wheeze 
of an asthmatic robot, 
followed by slurps and spurts 
like someone trying 
to get the last bit of milkshake 
through a tinfoil straw. 
If that hasn't woken you up,
an interrogation light clicks on, 
along with a buzzer that simulates 
the actual sound of your head 
being plunged into a nest of wasps. 
"Good morning," says 1974's
best thinking on automated luxury,
"I made you a nice cup of tea."

4 October 2021

How the Unicorn Became Extinct

It happened in a word from glossy lips.
It happened in the razor-tight focus
on a soap bubble shot through by robots
in the dim light of oblivion.

All the plastic dolls in the world
had suddenly lost their mounts,
so instead they rode convertibles
and husbands without genitals.

Tiny hairbrushes tangled with nylon,
sticker-mirrors with pearly frames,
small rainbow-coloured bridals,
all rushed into donation bins.

No longer "my", no longer "little",
the riddle of puberty solved
like a crossword on a cereal box
where every clue is so last week.

Adieu, Princess Sparkle-Tail,
so-long Lady Sassypants the Third
up, up, and away we grow,
leaving hoof-prints on the clouds.

Dia

Before it was cosplay, we were just dressing up--
my nephew the beekeeper, his baby brother the bee,
haunting all doorbells to fill up the hive
with pumpkin-shaped toffees and chocolate spooks.

onto a mattress, pond, or over-chlorinated pool
watching myself in the reflection of the water,
falling through this world, into splash.

1 November 2021

They say

truth and rumour, lichen and moss,
cling to the same rock.
A trick of the light dapples
both as I pass them,
stopping only to scatter
a handful of bought birdseed
against the coming winter,
confusing the dog.
But wait, was that really
how it was that autumn?
So casual, so easy with words,
with strides and kicking leaves?
I wonder how much I want
to remember--which mushrooms
are called Chicken of the Forest,
which ones are Destroying Angel.
Away from the screen, that digital
confessional, it seems as clear
as a roast and potatoes--yes,
the same gravy drapes over both,
easy and gelatinous as politics--
but who will take the first bite
on behalf of the dog or the king?
Her nose is on the move now,
going down for the good voles,
rooting and rooting, since 
she has time on her paws,
with an owner dawdling over moss--
or was it lichen?--and wondering
when supper will be done.

8 November 2021

A Foolish Request

Bring me a ladder, I said to the sea,
and on grey wings, collapsing
it obliged, casting its woollen spray
against the kayaks and cormorants.

The music came slowly at first,
as if from a shell held close
the drums were as near as my pulse
and the whistling came out of me.

Salt-grey, wine-bitter, waist deep
I took the oblique undertow
as resentment for all I'd done
and regretted not doing on land.

But the sea was content to recede
and so I climbed the waves instead.

Bracing for it

The leaves blaze like undersea flares,
a star map against the aqueous sky,

"England's green aquarium" going gold,
and soon to be skeletal, wire brush--

raking for answers, came there none
forking through the marrow and mist,

a scald-mark, ink-drip, untapped vein
of black promise, dreaming of buds.

I too have stood at the trunk, chin up
to know what squirrels see in it.

Yesterday, two blackbirds sparred,
climbing a ladder of air on either side.

I scatter a pocketful of birdseed
at the base of a beetle-chewed log,

whose stair-step of bracket mushrooms
leads deeper than I dare to go.

Who will make it? Who keeps the score?
My open-handed gesture is hardly enough.

Back home I pat the wood pile for luck.

6 December 2021

At least we have Facetime

We're grumping through it, no other
way to say it, pricked as holly,
garlanded with tsk-tsks and sighs,
when in comes the call from Santa Claus,
in an itchy beard and red pyjamas 
toddle-hopping over to their tree
to show us baubles bigger than his fist,
fake frost, flashing lights, and trim
that glitters in the lens of mum's new phone.
That's all it takes to right us on the couch,
and bring the dog to inspect our squees,
sniffing the screen for treats. As the songs
predict: We won't be home for Christmas,
we'll muddle through once more somehow,
but for now, the best Chris Kringle around
is bellowing at us to have a Merry one
and so, by golly, we will.

Omicron Winter

More chalk-and-talk from the minister of quarrels,
as we pull the curtains on another slate day,
the light behind the hill keen but ineffectual,
blackmail in the newspapers, dodos on the run.

There used to be rainbows on signs, and singing,
about pulling through together in the face of death,
before QR codes queueing for outbound flights,
and throat swabs prescient as pregnancy tests.

We've come this far in the Greek alphabet,
and plus-ça-change if we're going back now.
We will cross the Rubicon, pebble-in-shoe,
on bikes and bulls and mobility scooters.

The world ends each day before teatime.
At least we have the rain to cheer us up.

After Walking the Dog

She licks her paws with such tenderness
I can hardly stand to watch
the grey light filtering over her
the darkest day yet to come.

The forecast is for monotony,
the sound of the millstone on chaff.
Relentless in its negligence,
even the daylight cowers in its den.

My jeans unravel at the pockets,
tatty under the load they've had to bear,
and my holy, heel-mashed cuffs
are caked with the good clay she licks.

Like the thirsty cut pine at Christmas,
I've had too much kindness, and gone dry.

Oh, The Mud

I recall the crackled porcelain
in the dry flats of my childhood,
the lake-bottoms white with salt

jealous of our all-terrain dog,

against sprains, falls, and tears
of the long tenuous ligaments
that keep us marionettes up.

###

Non nobis domine the mud of Agincourt

So wet, it is white. So what?

Tired of the weather. 
Tired of taking about the weather.
Now that is extreme.
The only cure is a cup of tea.
It cures extremism.
It cures the arthritic ache of living.
Whatever the weather.
Whatever I said, forget it.
I was tired.
I was tried and found guilty.
My first offence.
I got off with a cup of tea
over-brewed.
That's what really got Socrates.
Not the weather.
Not the mood of a savage people
terrified of light.
Not the watery sun making a cameo
in a tea-stained land.
Do I tire you?
All poetry is gentle coercion.
Some more, some less.
It provokes the bone-deep ache of living
like damp weather.

17 January 2022

Why I Compose in Woodgrain

The spline of the wood is the seed of the world.
Hear me out -- in joining pieces two-by-two
like electrons shaking hands across orbits
we glue together what remains, what once
was whole and growing both high and deep.
Even before our love letters to metal,
etched by sparks on the dusty air,
the tree was life, gave us crucifix
and bore the weight of worlds in its bough.
This is why I sharpen knife and axe,
that the cuts may go clean without waste,
that the body I saved can save for me
one last taste of its fire and sap.

fizzle, whistle, plume, ember, thistle, brittle, christen, kitchen, listen, dome


Reverse Strip Tease
[British]

She likes to watch me dress
starting with yesterday's socks,
pricked with thistles, exposed
at the heel--one, and then another.

Next comes the smudged grey hoodie,
which makes her lick her lips,
and from the kitchen, two trainers
perched by the Aga, baked in mud.

The more I cover up for her,
less she can contain herself,
and when I tighten my track trousers
she runs in circles, panting.

By the time I find the lead,
she is absolutely barking.


Reverse Strip Tease
[American]

She likes to watch me dress
starting with yesterday's socks,
pricked with thistles, exposed
at the heel--one, and then another.

Next comes the smudged grey hoodie,
which makes her lick her lips,
and from the kitchen, two sneakers
perched by the oven, baked in mud.

The more I cover up for her,
less she can contain herself,
and when I tighten my sweatpants
she runs in circles, panting.

By the time I find her leash,
she is howling for it.

PROMPT

handle, barrelfish, zap, happy, doorstep, march, graze/grazing, apologize, crinkle, three

11 April 2022

A Love Letter

Happy the grazing cattle who know
the good poetry of chlorophyl,
and the barrelfish caught 
by the curiosity of driftwood.

My face crinkles more each day
folding over its linen apologies.
Who can say if that pigeon delights
or detests its roosting tree?

I wait on the doorstep of madness,
empty jack-o-lantern in hand,
too tired to knock, too knackered
to run--and even if I did, where?

Here are the last crumbs of bagel
my pocket wanted to keep for you.

Also:

Include the name of a boardgame
Mention one or more unusual or interesting animals

18 April 2022

Jubilate

In the end, it comes down to chlorophyl,
our histrionics a distant harmonica tune
the thistles worrying our inmost brain
plucked and migrating in a cool breeze.

Our faces un-crinkle, steaming out
the folds of our linen apologies
and the even long-nosed shrew emerges,
blinking and stretching its snout.

For all our sophistication, we are leaves
hop-scotching the sidewalks in autumn,
slick-black with snowmelt decay. And now,
we unfurl our green banner, and wave.

I scatter the last crumbs of wistfulness
my pocket had kept all winter for you.

25 July 2022


Who else has come to an end like this? The stomped
leaves, the levees weeping, paintwork chipped.

Death of a Patriot

The parrot I can always talk to is made of wood.
In self-conscious silence, its black pupils watch.
I have become what I least hoped for--
unable to twist anger's thermostat,
and boil up a damn to give for what I loved,
the long shadow of its potential--
drunkard father swearing to change,
dangling our legs, soda in hand, squinting
into the drift of sprinkler mist.
Let them keep fucking shooting each other
I hear the parrot caw, and start
to wonder where it learned such language,
so direct and ineffectual,
as it peers above my cooling tea.

1 August 2022

Hurry, harrow, swindle, marrow, shuck, rib, roll.
Fiddle, carry, hunter, berry, nibble, knotty, knoll. 

Life Finds a Way

I have emerged from the bee-swarmed hedge,
and who can say if it's blood or berries,
bark-graze, branch-fiddle, or hook-swipe barb
that decorated my second-best running shoes?

Only that night will I rub my finger whorls
trenched in ink, ready for the mugshot print,
and wonder why I never got stung that time
I pinched the petals of a honeysuckle,

only to hear the buzz and struggle, the life
that wanted living fight against it smothering--
so jealous of its zeal, I went on tightening
till something said, "let it live"--

now I beat through thorn-rows, searching
for the sting I missed, and still deserve.

10 October 2022

A freckle on a stone in the middle of a pond
the broad stomach of a gutted fish
a continental sandbar, a driftwood drift.

What I hate about fishing is the smell.

14 November 2022

Has you ever been stung by a dead bee?

Old Movie

The horizon approaches whiteout
and the grey mushrooms thrive
crows resume their choreography,
a magpie perches in halves.

The last embers of the maple
have been snuffed in their beds
and even now are blackening
under the rain's soft tread.

How much longer, my love,
can we subsist without a plot,
following the cinematographer
shot by obsessive shot?

In the quiet frame of a window
my life goes flickering by.

2 January 2023

The Lines Blur When Wet

Green grows the lichen on the tree.
In the sapless whorls, an acorn.
In the acorn, a weevil feasts.
Writing this, I have already failed.

Try again. Try harder. Concentrate.
In the periphery, a liquid crow
flows like the dark ink of forgetting
I blot my eyes, and soldier on.

It has been 97 days since my last
omission, decision, commission, insurrection
still nothing has come of nothing
as the king predicted when he still had eyes.

I have become so inexcusably faint,
Even my beard ignores me now.

17 January 2023

rather, vein, troop, dash, gobsmack (or agog, you choose), authority, interpret, lantern

What I hate about fishing is the smell.

Devein, divine

Summers on the River with Dad

All night, the clink of a lantern hook
then through the thin canvass, dawn
and butter smoking in the pan.
What I hate about fishing is the smell.

Any other son would leap, like you
to greet the morning with a goofy song,
but my head is full of equations
and the birdy chirp of little poems.

You hand me an oar, and we shove
into the reed beds, still as egrets
where I will whisper back as excitedly
as we both wish I really could be.

I puff my inhaler twice, for strength,
then knife out the gills, still heaving.

30 January 2023

Rescue dog: Allergies

Dog of black gums and parachute lips. Dog in the frozen night, unperturbed. Dog so unlike me, made of bone meal and scrap. Ears and eyebrows telegraphing each thought. I lie down beside you gasping for air. I puff twice, for luck, and stroke your chin. With sausage fingers who-hit-you? welts. Red-eyed, not crying, I double-down doggedly. You are more to me than oxygen.

Blanket laced with fur
still warm from your departure
how it smothers me.

2 April 2023

Big Data

Big data in fronds shrapnelling.
Big data the chestnuts spike.
Big data the switchblade grass.

Big data values your privacy.
Big data opts-out of cop-outs.
Big data digs beta, bytes big.

Big data digs data big baby big.

Big data restructuring snowflakes.
Big data encrypting the weather.
Big data drops acid in rain.

Big wins. Big world of big sins.
Big data my echoing clicks.
Big data for men with small dicks.

Big shoes on big clowns in small
cars on loose ground digging
graves for big plans mass appeal.

Big data skulls in big fields.

Big data night full of crickets.
Big data dust motes emoting.
Big data still pond taking rain.

Big data in robot close-combat.
Big data concussed by sound math.
Big data locking on target.

Big data permission to kill.
